======================================================================
GRIDFORGE V2 — CURRENT WORKSPACE / PANEL AUDIT STATUS
======================================================================

Architectural authority
-----------------------

MainWindow
    owns the overall Qt dock-area/layout infrastructure.

Workspace/Layout
    owns the logical arrangement of editors/panels.

WorkspacePlacement
    owns canonical logical placement.

WorkspaceRealizer
    translates Workspace layout decisions into MainWindow operations.

PanelsPlugin
    owns panel composition and panel presentation capabilities.

PanelRegistry
    owns panel registration.

PanelManager
    owns panel runtime lifecycle.

PanelInstance
    owns runtime panel lifecycle.

PanelState
    owns panel lifecycle state only.

PanelDescriptor
    describes panel metadata/capabilities only.

Critical invariant
------------------

PanelSpec / PanelDescriptor / PanelState MUST NOT independently define
the canonical logical Workspace area.

Workspace/Layout is the sole authority for editor/panel arrangement.

======================================================================
GF-AUD-071
======================================================================

File:
    ui/plugins/panels_plugin.py

Status:
    🔴 ARCHITECTURAL CONFLICT — CORRECTION REQUIRED

Confirmed finding:

    PanelSpec currently contains placement/layout concerns.

    PanelsPlugin directly performs Qt docking operations including
    addDockWidget(), tabifyDockWidget(), and removeDockWidget().

This violates the frozen ownership boundary.

Required architecture:

    PanelsPlugin
        → panel composition/capabilities

    WorkspacePlacement
        → placement

    WorkspaceLayout
        → arrangement

    WorkspaceRealizer
        → realization

    MainWindow
        → Qt dock host operations

Required modifications:

    1. PanelSpec.area must not define canonical Workspace placement.

    2. Direct docking realization must move behind WorkspaceRealizer /
       MainWindow.

    3. PanelsPlugin must remain a panel composition plugin.

    4. Panel capabilities such as closable, movable and floatable may
       remain because they describe panel capability, not placement.

    5. PanelSpec.tabbed must not become authoritative layout policy.

Decision:
    DO NOT delete PanelsPlugin.

    Correct its ownership boundary.

======================================================================

Canonical plugin dependency graph:

        ↓

Outstanding:

CURRENT AUDIT DECISION
======================================================================

The current architecture is NOT:

    Panel
       ↓
    DockArea
       ↓
    MainWindow

The locked target architecture is:

    Panel subsystem
        ↓
    Workspace/Layout
        ↓
    WorkspaceRealizer
        ↓
    MainWindow
        ↓
    Qt


Panel subsystem
---------------

    PanelBase
    PanelDescriptor
    PanelRegistry
    PanelManager
    PanelInstance
    PanelState

Workspace subsystem
-------------------

    Workspace
    WorkspaceDefinition
    WorkspacePlacement
    WorkspaceLayout
    WorkspaceManager
    WorkspaceState
    PanelArea
    WorkspaceRealizer

Application host
----------------

    MainWindow

Plugin infrastructure
---------------------

    PluginLoader
    PluginManager
    PluginRegistry
    PluginContext

Critical rule
-------------

PanelSpec.area
PanelDescriptor.area
PanelState.area

must NOT become independent canonical placement authorities.

Workspace/Layout decides how editors are arranged.

GRIDFORGE V2 — BATCH 3 GF-INT CURRENT RECONCILIATION
======================================================================

The following entries are the current authoritative Batch 3
GF-INT reconciliation. Historical audit material is preserved
elsewhere and is not replaced by these current-status records.

======================================================================
GF-INT-018
======================================================================

Scope:
    Application/Core endpoint resolution.

Finding:
    Endpoint resolution required a single canonical interpretation of
    Terminal endpoint relationships and must not silently convert
    malformed Core state into a disconnected endpoint.

Correction:
    The Network endpoint resolver validates the authoritative Terminal,
    rejects unsupported endpoint forms, and resolves supported endpoint
    relationships to the canonical Bus.

Architectural requirement:
    Endpoint interpretation remains read-only and within the
    Application/Network boundary; Terminal does not own global topology.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-020
======================================================================

Scope:
    Application endpoint reference validation.

Finding:
    Endpoint references required explicit validation and canonical Core
    resolution rather than permissive or duplicated object lookup.

Correction:
    EndpointResolver resolves immutable endpoint references through the
    active project context and canonical Network objects, with explicit
    validation/error handling for unsupported or missing references.

Architectural requirement:
    Endpoint resolution is read-only Application orchestration over the
    authoritative Core Network.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-021
======================================================================

Scope:
    Network identity uniqueness.

Finding:
    Canonical object identity must be unique across the complete Network,
    not merely within individual typed collections.

Correction:
    NetworkRegistry uses one canonical identity map and rejects duplicate
    canonical IDs before registration.

Architectural requirement:
    NetworkRegistry is the single authority for Network membership and
    canonical identity.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-023
======================================================================

Scope:
    Network aggregate contract.

Finding:
    Network membership, identity, topology state and indexing required a
    single authoritative aggregate boundary.

Correction:
    Network owns the authoritative Registry, NetworkState, BusIndex and
    TopologyManager and exposes controlled mutation/validation boundaries.

Architectural requirement:
    Network remains the authoritative electrical aggregate; downstream
    analysis does not become a competing state authority.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-024
======================================================================

Scope:
    Typed Core membership.

Finding:
    Network registration must reject non-Core or unsupported object types
    instead of allowing arbitrary objects into authoritative membership.

Correction:
    NetworkRegistry validates ElectricalObject membership and the supported
    Core Network type set before registration.

Architectural requirement:
    Only supported Core ElectricalObject instances enter authoritative
    Network membership.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-025
======================================================================

Scope:
    Stale references during Network deletion.

Finding:
    Removing an object while another registered object still referenced it
    could leave dangling Core endpoint references.

Correction:
    NetworkRegistry.remove() scans registered owners and rejects removal
    while dependent Terminal references remain.

Architectural requirement:
    Network membership changes preserve endpoint/reference integrity.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-027
======================================================================

Scope:
    Canonical identity lookup.

Finding:
    Object lookup required one canonical identity path rather than
    independently authoritative typed collections.

Correction:
    Network.get_by_identity() delegates to NetworkRegistry's canonical
    identity map; typed lookup remains a compatibility projection.

Architectural requirement:
    Canonical identity resolution is owned by NetworkRegistry.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-028
======================================================================

Scope:
    Registry authority.

Finding:
    Typed collections must not become independent membership authorities.

Correction:
    NetworkRegistry derives typed collections from the canonical
    _objects identity map and retains membership mutation in the registry.

Architectural requirement:
    There is one authoritative Network registry; typed views are
    projections.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-030
======================================================================

Scope:
    Dependent-reference protection.

Finding:
    Core deletion must protect dependent references and must not silently
    leave owners pointing at removed objects.

Correction:
    NetworkRegistry.remove() explicitly checks all registered Terminal
    owners and rejects deletion while a dependent reference exists.

Architectural requirement:
    Referential integrity is enforced at the authoritative Network
    mutation boundary.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-035
======================================================================

Scope:
    Canonical terminal-role handling.

Finding:
    Terminal ownership, semantic role and endpoint references require one
    authoritative Core Terminal contract.

Correction:
    Terminal owns its endpoint and semantic role, validates its owner and
    role, and retains the canonical role vocabulary without delegating
    global topology interpretation to Terminal.

Architectural requirement:
    Terminal remains a Core domain value/relationship boundary; global
    topology remains owned by Network.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-040
======================================================================

Scope:
    Application transaction rollback state integrity.

Finding:
    Transaction lifecycle required explicit distinction between open,
    committing, committed, rolling back, rolled back and rollback-failed
    states.

Correction:
    Transaction records undo operations only while OPEN; commit creates the
    immutable undo journal; rollback transitions through ROLLING_BACK,
    attempts inverse operations, and represents rollback failure explicitly
    while preserving the first rollback error.

Architectural requirement:
    Rollback is a pre-commit transaction concern and cannot be repeated
    after a committed transaction.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-041
======================================================================

Scope:
    Application undo/redo history transition.

Finding:
    Undo history transition required removal from undo history before the
    record could enter redo history.

Correction:
    CommandManager performs:
        pop_undo(record)
        -> execute inverse UndoJournal
        -> move_undo_to_redo(record)

    CommandHistory.move_undo_to_redo() now accepts an already-removed
    record and rejects a record that is still present in either stack.

Architectural requirement:
    CommandManager owns undo-operation execution and transition
    orchestration; CommandHistory remains a state-only history container.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-042
======================================================================

Scope:
    Application command transaction/history commit boundary.

Finding:
    PRE-COMMIT, COMMIT and POST-COMMIT behavior required explicit
    separation so that post-commit history failure cannot roll back already
    committed Core state.

Correction:
    CommandManager executes handler work before commit, obtains the
    immutable UndoJournal at commit, and records history only after commit.
    A post-commit history-recording failure marks the command boundary
    degraded instead of attempting to roll back committed Core state.

Architectural requirement:
    Core transaction commit is authoritative; history recording is a
    post-commit application concern.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-046
======================================================================

Scope:
    Project runtime isolation.

Finding:
    Activating another project/network required a fresh project-bound
    Application command/history runtime rather than retaining the prior
    project's history boundary.

Correction:
    Bootstrap builds a fresh CommandManager/read/validation runtime for
    the active Network and Application replaces the runtime atomically
    during Network activation.

Architectural requirement:
    Project-scoped command/history state must not leak across active
    project boundaries.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-047
======================================================================

Scope:
    Project lifecycle activation integrity.

Finding:
    Project activation required validation and rollback-aware replacement
    of project-bound runtime state.

Correction:
    ProjectLifecycleService validates the candidate and coordinates
    presentation, Network and project-state activation with rollback
    callbacks; incomplete rollback enters ROLLBACK_FAILED explicitly.

Architectural requirement:
    Project activation remains an Application lifecycle transaction and
    does not expose partial project state as active.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-048
======================================================================

Scope:
    Project identity and activation-generation isolation.

Finding:
    Project-scoped state and study/runtime references required explicit
    project identity and activation-generation provenance.

Correction:
    Project lifecycle increments activation_generation across successful
    transitions, ProjectSnapshot carries project_id and
    activation_generation, and project-scoped runtime activation validates
    the active project generation before use.

Architectural requirement:
    Project identity and activation generation remain explicit boundaries
    for project-scoped state and downstream study preparation.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================
GF-INT-050
======================================================================

Scope:
    Network.validate() Core-domain validation boundary.

Finding:
    Generic object validation was insufficient for authoritative Network
    validation.

Correction:
    Network.validate() explicitly requires Core ElectricalObject
    membership, invokes each element's validate() contract, and checks
    Network membership tokens and registered endpoint relationships.

Architectural requirement:
    Network validation operates on the Core ElectricalObject contract and
    validates authoritative membership/reference integrity.

Status:
    REMEDIATED — VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Static source reconciliation completed.
    Runtime/tests/CI verification deferred.

======================================================================

======================================================================
BATCH 18 — SLD / CORE DELETION INTENT AND TRANSACTION BOUNDARY
======================================================================

Architectural decision state:
    No authoritative engineer-facing policy was found that makes
    SLD-node deletion imply Core-equipment deletion, Core-equipment
    deletion imply persistent SLD deletion, or both actions one
    canonical use case.

    ARCHITECTURAL DECISION REQUIRED.

Correction:
    RemoveSLDNodeCommand remains presentation-only.
    Generic Core Delete*Command objects remain presentation-neutral.
    SLDController.remove_node() and SLD selection are explicitly
    documented as presentation/UI intent only.
    No coordinated Core+SLD deletion command was introduced because
    doing so would invent an engineer-facing deletion policy that the
    repository does not currently authorize.

Batch 18 findings:
    SLD-WF-108 — No canonical Application equipment-representation
                   deletion use case — CRITICAL — OPEN
    SLD-WF-109 — Core and persistent SLD deletion are independent
                   history boundaries — CRITICAL — OPEN
    SLD-WF-110 — SLDController.remove_node() is presentation-only and
                   has no equipment-deletion intent — HIGH — OPEN
    SLD-WF-111 — SLD selection does not provide a verified
                   equipment-deletion workflow — HIGH — OPEN
    SLD-WF-112 — Core delete commands have no persistent SLD
                   reconciliation boundary — CRITICAL — OPEN
    SLD-WF-113 — Core delete undo cannot reconcile presentation
                   because the command owns no SLD mutation — HIGH — OPEN
    SLD-WF-114 — Runtime projection reconciliation is distinct from
                   persistent SLD reconciliation — HIGH — OPEN
    SLD-WF-115 — Engineer-facing meaning of SLD-node deletion versus
                   equipment deletion is undefined — HIGH — OPEN

Static verification:
    Application transaction/history boundaries were traced.
    RemoveSLDNodeCommand records only SLD restoration through the
    existing Transaction.record_undo() mechanism.
    Core deletion remains Core-scoped.
    No nested Application.execute() composition was introduced.
    Runtime projection does not receive authority to mutate the
    persistent SLDDocument.

Status:
    CORRECTED — STATIC VERIFICATION COMPLETE
    Batch 18 remains OPEN / RE-AUDIT REQUIRED pending the architectural
    deletion-intent decision.

Verification boundary:
    No tests, CI, application startup, GUI execution, or runtime
    verification performed.

GF-MASTER-0027..0029 / GF-MASTER-0034 — Protection / measurement
----------------------------------------------------------------------
Static evidence:
    core/protection/decision.py defines ProtectionDecision as an immutable

    modify Relay state, modify MeasurementChannel state, or modify network
    topology.

    protection result type.
    MeasurementChannel remains a measurement representation and its source
    references are read/association data.

    OPEN — RE-AUDIT REQUIRED

Reason:
    The decision contract is consistent with the frozen boundary, but the
    complete decision-to-command-to-Core-switching consumer chain was not
    sufficiently established for static closure.

Finding:
    GF-MASTER-0005 — Reconcile architecture and audit documentation with
    current implementation and the frozen GridForge V2 architecture.

    OPEN — DOCUMENTATION CORRECTION PARTIAL; REPOSITORY-WIDE RE-AUDIT
    AND INTEGRATION EVIDENCE REMAIN REQUIRED

    Modification_Register.txt remains the master audit register.
    README.md remains the architecture entry point.
    No competing architecture document or new audit register was created.

Current source evidence inspected (static source only):
    core/application/command_manager.py
        Describes command validation/handler resolution, transaction
        execution, rollback on handler failure, committed undo journal,
        and redo by re-executing the original command.

    core/application/transaction.py
        Defines transaction lifecycle, inverse-operation registration,
        rollback/commit, and undo-journal handoff. It does not own Core,
        UI, or Qt.

    core/application/application.py
        Provides the Application facade for commands, reads, events,
        history, project lifecycle, and studies.

    core/application/services/sld_service.py
        Describes persistent SLD presentation mutation inside the
        Application transaction supplied by CommandManager and explicitly
        does not own history or undo/redo.

    core/protection/runtime.py
        Defines ProtectionRuntime.compose(channels) and composes project
        protection configuration against a Core Network.

    core/protection/preparation.py
        Defines detached preparation structures including
        PreparedProtectionInput and ProtectionPreparation.

    ui/core/qt.py
        Is documented as the single UI Qt import boundary and imports
        PySide6 primitives. This file alone does not prove repository-wide
        compliance by every UI module or absence of imports elsewhere.

    core/measurement/README.md
        States measurement is headless domain state and UI measurement
        representations are projections.

    core/control/README.md
        States control semantics are headless and exposed through
        Application workflows.

    core/protection/README.md
        Describes the protection subsystem and its intended measurement
        and output relationships. Its architecture diagrams are not proof
        that all production provisioning-to-runtime edges are connected.

2026-10-23 — GF-MASTER-0005 — PROJECT LIFECYCLE / PERSISTENCE SOURCE TRACE
======================================================================

Finding:
    GF-MASTER-0005 — Architecture and audit-documentation reconciliation.

Status:
    OPEN — TARGETED LIFECYCLE CONTRACT DOCUMENTED; FULL ROUND-TRIP AND
    REPOSITORY-WIDE RE-AUDIT STILL REQUIRED

Current source inspected on SubhenduMishra29/GridForge main:
    core/application/project_lifecycle.py
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    core/persistence/project_persistence.py
    README.md

Source-backed findings:
    1. ProjectLifecycleService._activate_candidate() validates the candidate,
       retains prior context/network/presentation/generation/state, then
       coordinates presentation-collection activation, presentation
       activation, network activation, read-only post-network audit, and
       project-state activation.

    2. Lifecycle context, network, activation generation, and ACTIVE/NO_PROJECT
       state are committed after the activation callback sequence succeeds.
       On activation failure, registered rollback callbacks execute in reverse
       order. If one or more callbacks fail, lifecycle state becomes
       ROLLBACK_FAILED and subsequent transitions are rejected pending recovery.
       This is a source-level contract; all failure permutations were not
       executed.

    3. ProjectLifecycleService.save_project() captures the starting
       activation generation and network, detaches serialized presentation
       payloads, and checks that the active project/network/generation did not
       change during persistence. It only marks presentation documents clean
       when current serialized data equals the saved snapshot; failed or
       ambiguous comparisons preserve dirty state.

    4. ProjectPersistenceService.load() reconstructs canonical project state
       including dynamic model associations, measurement channel definitions,
       draft network, control configuration, control-signal mapping,
       protection configuration, protection presentation, network, and
       presentation/presentation collection where supplied. Missing optional
       configuration payloads may be replaced by explicit empty/default state
       according to the loader's code.

    5. ProjectPersistenceService declares separate package, project schema,
       presentation, SLD, Core serialization, and connectivity serialization
       version constants. The package contract is .gridforge with manifest.json
       metadata and project.json canonical project payload.

    6. Bootstrap's activate_project_state() captures project-scoped protection,
       dynamic-model, control, signal-mapping, and draft state for compensation.
       The complete callback and serializer/deserializer comparison is not
       claimed to have been exhaustively audited in this pass.

Documentation update:
    README.md now contains a consolidated Project Lifecycle and Persistence
    Contract section within the existing architecture entry point. No new
    architecture document or register was created. The section explicitly
    distinguishes source inspection from runtime verification and retains
    GF-PROT-042 as OPEN.

Still open:
    - Complete field-by-field comparison of every save payload field against
      load reconstruction and activation, including presentation collection,
      measurement definitions/channels, dynamic models, control configuration,
      control-signal mapping, protection configuration/presentation, and draft
      network.
    - Inspect the full bootstrap activation/rollback callback and ensure every
      persisted field is restored to the correct project/generation.
    - Complete repository-wide architecture/documentation contradiction scan.
    - Audit repository-wide Qt import boundary compliance and affected
      __init__.py consistency.
    - Trace actual event publication/read-model consumers across command,
      undo, and redo workflows.
    - Keep GF-PROT-042 OPEN until production trigger/time contract and persisted
      typed protection-function-to-target action mapping are evidenced.
    - Existing UI placement, projection, icon, property editor, draft commit,
      and startup findings remain separate and are not closed by documentation.

Acceptance boundary:
    Static source inspection and documentation change only. No tests, CI,
    application startup, GUI execution, or runtime persistence experiments
    were performed or required.

Disposition:
    GF-MASTER-0005 remains OPEN. This addendum records the latest trace and
    the remaining bounded evidence obligations; it does not claim V2 freeze
    readiness.

2026-10-23 — GF-MASTER-0005 — MEASUREMENT DEFINITION RESTORE GAP
======================================================================

Status:
    OPEN — SOURCE-IDENTIFIED PROJECT ACTIVATION / PERSISTENCE INTEGRATION GAP

Evidence inspected on current main:
    core/persistence/project_persistence.py
    core/application/project_lifecycle.py
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    core/application/services/measurement_channel_service.py

Evidence:
    - ProjectPersistenceService.save() serializes measurement definitions as
      project["measurement"]["channels"] from the measurement_definitions
      argument.
    - ProjectPersistenceService.load() reads those entries into
      LoadedProject.measurement_definitions.
    - Application bootstrap initializes MeasurementChannelService for the
      initial project and uses its serialize_definitions() result when saving.
    - In the inspected bootstrap project activation callback, protection
      configuration is activated and candidate channels are read from
      measurement_channel_service.channels, but no reference to
      loaded.measurement_definitions appears in the activation path. The
      loaded definitions therefore are not visibly restored into the active
      MeasurementChannelService by this callback.
    - Protection runtime composition and relay input binding consume the
      service's active channels. Consequently, persisted measurement
      definitions may not be available to the newly activated project after
      opening or discarding changes. This is a source-level integration finding;
      no runtime reproduction was performed.

Required correction:
    1. Add an explicit Application-owned, project-scoped activation operation
       that restores LoadedProject.measurement_definitions into
       MeasurementChannelService for the candidate context/network/generation.
    2. Validate definition ownership and source equipment/terminal references
       against the candidate network before protection runtime composition.
    3. Include previous measurement-service state in activation compensation
       so failed project activation restores the exact prior channels and
       generation, not candidate state.
    4. Ensure new/close-project transitions explicitly activate empty
       definitions for the new lifecycle generation.
    5. Trace the order: candidate network activation -> measurement definition
       activation -> protection configuration validation / relay input binding
       -> ProtectionRuntime composition -> lifecycle commit.
    6. Confirm the save path serializes definitions from the same active
       project/generation and that optional/missing persisted definitions have
       an explicit backward-compatible policy.
    7. Re-fetch changed source and review the final diff/hygiene after correction.

Related documentation:
    README.md now explicitly records this as an OPEN integration gap rather
    than implying that loader reconstruction alone guarantees service restore.

Acceptance boundary:
    Static source inspection only. No tests, CI, startup, GUI execution, or
    runtime persistence experiments were performed or required.

Disposition:
    Keep GF-MASTER-0005 OPEN. Do not claim persistence round-trip completeness
    or V2 freeze readiness until this activation gap and the remaining
    field-by-field save/load/rollback obligations are resolved.

======================================================================


----------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-23 — GF-MASTER-0005 — MEASUREMENT RESTORE CORRECTION TARGET REFINED
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Additional source trace:
    core/application/services/measurement_channel_service.py
        MeasurementChannelService.activate(context, network, definitions,
        generation) already reconstructs channels, captures exact prior
        authority, and returns a rollback callback. snapshot_state() and
        restore_state() preserve project ID, activation generation, Network
        identity, channel registry identity, and live sample state.
        _provision_definition() resolves the source equipment and terminal
        reference against the candidate project/network.
    core/application/bootstrap.py
        create_application() calls activate_project_state() from the
        lifecycle's project_state_activator slot. Within that callback,
        protection configuration validation, relay input binding, and
        ProtectionRuntime.compose() currently consume
        measurement_channel_service.channels. The inspected callback does not
        invoke MeasurementChannelService.activate() with loaded definitions.
        save_project() does serialize definitions from the active service.
    core/application/project_lifecycle.py
        _activate_candidate() invokes network activation before project-state
        activation, and appends each returned rollback callback to a stack.
        On failure, callbacks are invoked in reverse order before lifecycle
        context/network/generation are restored.

Implementation target clarified:
    Invoke measurement_channel_service.activate(context, network,
    loaded.measurement_definitions, generation) inside the project-state
    activation transaction before reading candidate_channels and before any
    protection validation, relay binding, or ProtectionRuntime.compose().
    Register its returned rollback callback in the same compensation chain,
    and ensure it runs if a later stage of activate_project_state() fails.
    The existing activate_project_state() catches internal failures and
    performs its own restoration before it returns a lifecycle rollback
    callback, so integration must account for both failure-before-return and
    later-stage failure; avoid a double-rollback design that masks the
    original activation error.
    For context=None, explicitly activate an empty definition set and preserve
    the project-less service invariant. Preserve the current policy that absent
    optional persisted definitions resolve to an empty sequence, unless source
    migration policy establishes otherwise.

Evidence boundary:
    This refines the static implementation target from source inspection only.
    No source code correction or runtime validation has been performed in this
    audit authority repository. GF-MASTER-0005 remains OPEN.

----------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-23 — GF-AUD-019 — TRANSIENT-STABILITY COUPLING RE-AUDIT UPDATE
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Source fetched from current main:
    core/analysis/transient_network.py
        PreparedTransientStability.from_power_flow() derives a detached
        network snapshot from PreparedPowerFlow, including bus IDs, branches,
        transformers, shunts, equipment states, and topology revision.
        TransientNetworkSolver solves the passive Y-bus plus machine Norton
        injections and returns bus-voltage mappings.
    core/analysis/transient_runtime.py
        TransientNetworkRuntime.solve(state, time) obtains the current
        detached view from TransientEventState and delegates to
        TransientNetworkSolver.
    core/application/bootstrap.py
        run_transient_stability() prepares the study input, builds
        MultiMachineSystem, TransientEventState and TransientNetworkRuntime,
        then passes network_runtime.solve as the network callback to
        DAESolver. TransientStabilitySolver coordinates the configured DAE
        solver. This is an explicit study-owned coupling path; the earlier
        statement that canonical network coupling was not established must
        not be read as evidence that no coupling adapter exists.

Remaining audit questions (static source only):
    - Establish the intended authority boundary for the detached network
      snapshot and whether the study execution contract requires explicit
      project ID, activation generation and source revision checks before
      execution and/or during long-running execution.
    - Confirm event-state changes (fault application/clearing and equipment
      state changes) are reflected in each algebraic solve via detached_view().
    - Confirm snapshot contents and YBus construction preserve the intended
      electrical model contract and that no live UI/presentation state enters
      the numerical solver.
    - Reconcile this source with tests as source artifacts only if needed;
      do not run tests or CI unless explicitly requested.

Status:
    GF-AUD-019 remains OPEN — RE-AUDIT / ARCHITECTURAL COUPLING CONTRACT
    REQUIRED. Current source proves an explicit detached study-owned adapter,
    so any correction prompt must target a demonstrated remaining contract
    gap, not a presumed missing network-solver connection. No source code
    correction or tests/CI were performed in this audit step.

BATCH — 2026-10-23 — GF-MASTER-0027..0029 / GF-MASTER-0034
PROTECTION DECISION → APPLICATION COMMAND → CORE STATE / MEASUREMENT
======================================================================

Files inspected on current main:
    core/protection/decision.py
    core/protection/runtime.py
    core/application/protection_execution.py
    core/application/services/protection_output_service.py
    core/application/services/protection_configuration_service.py
    core/application/services/switching_model_service.py
    core/application/bootstrap.py

Confirmed decision and measurement contracts:
    - ProtectionDecision is immutable and separates decision intent from
      physical switching; actionable requires valid, unblocked trip_request.
    - ProtectionRuntime composes project protection configuration against
      Core objects and measurement channels.
    - ProtectionConfigurationService validates configured input-channel IDs
      against active channel definitions and declared input contracts.
    - bind_measurement() updates persisted project association and Relay input
      binding in one Application transaction with an undo callback.
    - Measurement channel binding therefore has a concrete service boundary,
      but this inspection does not prove that every production protection
      function receives current, quality-qualified, timestamped measurements.

Confirmed decision-to-command mechanisms:
    - ProtectionExecutionService accepts ProtectionDecision values and uses
      an injected Application-owned action_resolver to map an actionable
      decision to ControlDecision.
    - It translates ControlDecision to an Application Command and dispatches
      through ControlCommandDispatcher, recording typed per-decision outcomes.
    - It checks an optional pre-dispatch guard and supports typed Core
      postcondition verification; command acceptance is distinguished from a
      matched/mismatched/unavailable Core state observation.
    - ProtectionOutputService.trip_from_decision() separately translates an
      actionable decision and explicit breaker_id into TripBreakerCommand
      through Application.execute().
    - SwitchingModelService provides transaction-bound Core Breaker/Switch
      mutation methods, including trip_breaker() and open/close operations.

Unresolved integration evidence:
    - The inspected bootstrap composition does not visibly instantiate and
      wire ProtectionExecutionService with a production action_resolver,
      pre-dispatch provenance guard and typed Core state observer.
    - The separate ProtectionOutputService accepts breaker_id explicitly;
      its inspected method does not itself establish a persisted typed
      protection-function → target equipment/action mapping.
    - The resolver contract is injected and can represent the correct
      Application boundary, but source evidence reviewed here does not prove
      authoritative persistence, lifecycle validation, or full consumer
      wiring for those mappings.
    - The inspected paths do not establish end-to-end preservation and
      rejection semantics for measurement value, quality, timestamp and
      activation/project provenance at the point of a protection trip.
    - No inference is made that a command acceptance equals physical Core
      state change; the typed postcondition contract explicitly distinguishes
      them.

Disposition:
    GF-MASTER-0027 — OPEN — production decision-to-command wiring and
        persisted action mapping not established.
    GF-MASTER-0028 — OPEN — target/action authority and Core switching
        consumer reconciliation not established.
    GF-MASTER-0029 — OPEN — end-to-end measurement/protection provenance
        and authorization semantics not established.
    GF-MASTER-0034 — OPEN — measurement/protection integration coverage
        remains incomplete.

Verification boundary:
    Static source/register reconciliation only. No production source changed.
    No tests, CI, application startup, or GUI execution performed.

2026-10-23 — GF-MASTER-0031 / RCA-006-PERSIST-001
GRIDFORGE PACKAGE ROUND-TRIP RE-AUDIT
======================================================================

Files inspected:
    core/persistence/project_persistence.py
    core/persistence/project_package.py
    core/persistence/network_serializer.py
    core/persistence/model_dto.py
    core/application/services/measurement_channel_service.py
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    core/application/project_lifecycle.py

Confirmed:
    - The manifest is restricted to format and package_version metadata;
      project identity/state is written to canonical project.json.
    - project.json includes schema, project metadata, serialized Core
      Network/connectivity, presentation collection, dynamic-model
      associations, measurement channel definitions, and optional
      protection, protection presentation, Control, Control signal mapping,
      and draft network payloads.
    - Loading checks package format/version, migrates and validates the
      canonical project payload, reconstructs Network and presentation
      collection, reconstructs dynamic-model associations, and returns
      measurement definitions in LoadedProject.
    - Network reconstruction restores model DTOs and references before
      validating the reconstructed graph. Save writes into a temporary
      package directory and swaps it into place with a backup/rollback path.
    - MeasurementChannelService.serialize_definitions() intentionally
      persists configuration only, excluding live measurement sample state.
      Definitions include channel identity/type/name, source identity and
      terminal reference, phase/unit, nominal value, scale and polarity.
    - ProjectPersistenceService.load() returns measurement definitions, but
      the inspected bootstrap project activation path does not call
      measurement_channel_service.activate(context, network,
      loaded.measurement_definitions, generation). The service is initialized
      with an empty definition set, and project-state activation subsequently
      validates protection input bindings against the current channel registry.
      Therefore this source trace does not establish restoration of persisted
      measurement channels on project load; it indicates a concrete integration
      gap that can block relay/protection bindings referencing saved channels.
    - Load validates measurement.channels as an array of mappings, while full
      definition validation and source/terminal resolution occur in
      MeasurementChannelService._provision_definition during activate().
      Since the inspected load path does not invoke that provisioning step,
      cross-reference validity is not proven by load-time validation alone.

Disposition:
    GF-MASTER-0031 remains OPEN — package format, canonical payload structure,
    network reconstruction and save replacement mechanics are evidenced, but
    full round-trip restoration is not established until the measurement
    definition activation handoff and all persisted consumers are reconciled.
    GF-MASTER-0005 remains OPEN; this finding aligns with its existing
    measurement-definition restoration-gap addenda and is not a duplicate
    closure.

Verification boundary:
    Static source inspection only; no production source changed.
    No tests, CI, application startup, or GUI execution performed.

2026-10-23 — GF-MASTER-0037 / RCA-003
DIRECT MUTATION / COMMAND TRANSACTION / HISTORY RECONCILIATION
======================================================================

Files inspected:
    core/application/application.py
    core/application/command_manager.py
    core/application/transaction.py
    core/application/command_handlers.py
    core/application/services/model_service.py
    core/application/services/switching_model_service.py
    core/application/services/measurement_model_service.py
    core/application/services/electrical_connection_service.py
    core/application/services/sld_service.py
    core/application/commands/draft_commands.py
    core/application/project_lifecycle.py

Positive source evidence:
    - Application.execute(), undo(), and redo() delegate to the canonical
      CommandManager; public command execution is not a second history engine.
    - CommandManager._execute_command() validates and resolves a handler,
      creates one Transaction, executes the handler, validates the result,
      invokes the coordinated pre-commit hook, commits, and records history.
      Pre-commit failures roll back. If post-commit history recording fails,
      the manager marks itself degraded rather than incorrectly rolling back
      already committed Core state.
    - Transaction owns an undo journal and immediate rollback operations.
      Domain services receive an active Transaction and record inverse
      operations for model creation/update/deletion and terminal connections.
      SLDService documents that it mutates presentation only within the
      supplied Application transaction and records undo operations.
    - ModelCommandHandlers route model operations to domain services with
      the transaction supplied by CommandManager. ElectricalConnectionService
      similarly requires a Command and Transaction.
    - Draft commit is represented by CommitNetworkCommand, consistent with a
      command boundary for promotion of draft state into Core Network.
    - Application.set_draft_network() and
      configure_control_signal_mapping() are direct setters. The latter's
      source documentation reserves it for composition and transactional
      project activation/rollback; these are privileged lifecycle/composition
      paths, not evidence by themselves of a user-facing command bypass.

Unresolved:
    - This inspection sampled representative model, switching, measurement,
      terminal-connectivity, SLD and draft paths. It did not establish
      repository-wide absence of direct Core mutations from every UI,
      controller, tool, plugin, legacy/deleted module, or public service entry.
    - Public Application facade/service exposure and all alternate or legacy
      call sites still require a complete call-site classification before
      the architectural invariant can be declared closed.
    - Therefore no unsupported claim is made that every mutation path is
      command-mediated or that every state-changing operation participates
      in command history and dirty/revision tracking.

Disposition:
    GF-MASTER-0037 / RCA-003 remains OPEN — partial positive confirmation of
    the canonical command/transaction/history mechanism; exhaustive direct-
    mutation exclusion and caller-path reconciliation remain outstanding.

Verification boundary:
    Static source inspection only. No production source changed.
    No tests, CI, startup, or GUI execution performed.

======================================================================

2026-10-23 — GF-MASTER-0038..0040 / RCA-SLD-AUTH-001
SLD TERMINAL SNAP / CONNECTION COMMAND / READ PROJECTION RECONCILIATION
======================================================================

Files inspected:
    core/application/commands/connection_commands.py
    core/application/services/electrical_connection_service.py
    core/application/sld_command_handlers.py
    core/application/services/sld_service.py
    core/application/read_models.py
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    ui/connections/terminal_resolver.py
    ui/connections/connection_validator.py
    ui/core/snap_system.py
    ui/sld/sld_controller.py
    ui/sld/sld_read_adapter.py
    ui/sld/sld_read_synchronizer.py
    ui/sld/sld_projection.py
    ui/events/sld_update_coordinator.py
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_projection.py

Positive source evidence:
    - Core terminal connectivity has immutable ConnectTerminalCommand,
      DisconnectTerminalCommand and ReconnectTerminalCommand contracts.
      ElectricalConnectionService resolves typed endpoint references,
      attaches/detaches the Core terminal and records inverse endpoint state
      in the supplied Application Transaction.
    - SLDController submits persistent SLD presentation changes through
      Application.execute(). Simple-wire removal takes a dedicated
      delete_selected_simple_wire path rather than treating a visual line
      as authoritative Core topology.
    - SLDService explicitly distinguishes persistent presentation connections
      from authoritative Core connectivity and contains audit/reconciliation
      logic mapping SIMPLE_WIRE presentation companions to Core connection IDs.
    - Application read models include immutable SimpleWireReadModel and
      NetworkReadModel values. SLDReadAdapter consumes read models without
      Core object access; SLDReadSynchronizer refreshes ephemeral projections
      and reports missing, duplicate, orphaned and unmatched simple-wire
      companions.
    - SLDUpdateCoordinator routes element lifecycle, topology/network,
      project lifecycle, protection and presentation events into the
      appropriate read synchronizer and canvas refresh. It reuses the
      synchronizer-owned projection manager rather than creating a second
      projection authority.
    - SnapSystem is a UI spatial candidate/snap service and restricts
      registered candidates to canonical SLD/draft presentation items.
      TerminalResolver and ConnectionValidator document logical terminal
      lookup/validation responsibilities, not Core topology ownership.

Outstanding reconciliation:
    - This source pass does not establish the complete production UI gesture
      path from a snapped terminal pair through EndpointIdentityAdapter /
      EndpointReference creation to Application.execute(ConnectTerminalCommand).
      A UI-level logical connection and a persistent SLD presentation
      connection must not be assumed to create Core connectivity.
    - Snap candidate correctness, endpoint-role compatibility, and the
      relationship between snap output and typed Core endpoint references
      were not exhaustively traced across every placement/connection tool.
    - Event coordinator logic is present, but end-to-end registration,
      command-to-event emission and active-canvas consumption are not proven
      for every connect/disconnect/reconnect and simple-wire lifecycle.
    - Existing reconciliation diagnostics detect projection mismatches; this
      inspection does not prove all mismatch conditions are surfaced to users
      or that repair is always transactional and deterministic.

Disposition:
    GF-MASTER-0038..0040 / RCA-SLD-AUTH-001 remain OPEN — canonical command,
    Core transaction, read-model and projection components are present, but
    complete production gesture-to-Core connectivity and event-to-visible-
    projection coverage remains unverified. Do not close based on component
    existence alone.

Verification boundary:
    Static source inspection only. No production source changed.
    No tests, CI, startup, or GUI execution performed.

======================================================================
2026-10-23 — STUDY START EVENT LIFECYCLE CLEANUP CORRECTION
======================================================================

File corrected:
    core/application/study.py

Source evidence:
    - StudyService.execute() inserted the active cancellation token into
      _tokens and published StudyStarted before entering its try/finally.
    - ApplicationEventBus dispatch is synchronous and invokes subscribers
      directly; a subscriber exception could therefore escape before the
      lifecycle cleanup block and leave the study permanently marked active.

Correction:
    - StudyStarted publication now occurs inside the existing try block.
      Subscriber failures follow the existing failed-study handling path,
      and the finally block removes the active token.

Disposition:
    - The active-token leak caused by a StudyStarted subscriber exception
      before lifecycle protection is corrected.
    - Event subscriber exception policy and failures during terminal-event
      publication remain broader event-bus concerns and are not claimed fixed.

Verification boundary:
    - Source patch committed; GitHub re-fetch verification pending.
    - No tests or CI run. Runtime behavior remains unverified.

======================================================================
2026-10-23 — STUDY SERVICE SHARED-STATE SYNCHRONIZATION
======================================================================

File corrected:
    core/application/study.py

Source evidence:
    - StudyService may be called by an Application that executes studies
      asynchronously. Handler registration, duplicate-active checks, token
      registration/removal, cancellation lookup, and result registry access
      previously accessed shared dictionaries without a common lock.
    - The active-study duplicate check and token insertion were separate
      unsynchronized operations, allowing concurrent callers to race.

Correction:
    - Added a service-owned RLock.
    - Guarded handler registration/lookups, active token admission and
      cleanup, cancellation lookup, active-study inspection, and result
      registry writes/reads/snapshots with the lock.
    - Numerical handlers and event publication execute outside the lock so
      long-running work and synchronous subscribers do not hold the state
      lock.

Disposition:
    - Shared registry access and duplicate-active admission are synchronized.
    - This does not assert that all external project-transition workflows are
      atomic with study admission; that requires caller-level coordination.

Verification boundary:
    - Final source was re-fetched from GitHub and inspected for the intended
      lock scopes after correction.
    - No tests or CI run. Runtime/concurrency behavior remains unverified.

2026-10-23 — TRANSIENT-STABILITY CONFIGURATION METADATA IMMUTABILITY
======================================================================

File corrected:
    core/analysis/transient_stability.py

Source evidence:
    TransientStabilityStudyConfiguration is a frozen dataclass, but its
    metadata was only wrapped with MappingProxyType(dict(...)). Nested
    dictionaries, lists, and sets supplied by a caller could still be
    mutated after configuration construction.

Correction:
    - Added a Core-local recursive freezer for Mapping, list/tuple, and
      set/frozenset metadata containers.
    - Applied it during configuration initialization.
    - Preserved non-container typed values rather than coercing arbitrary
      objects into another representation.

Disposition:
    Built-in nested metadata containers are now recursively frozen.
    This does not claim arbitrary custom objects stored in metadata are
    themselves immutable.

Verification boundary:
    - Source was re-fetched and checked after the correction.
    - No tests or CI run; runtime behavior remains unverified.

2026-10-23 — POWER FLOW STUDY CONVERGENCE SETTINGS HANDOFF
======================================================================

File corrected:
    core/analysis/power_flow.py

Source evidence:
    PowerFlowAnalysis.from_network accepted PowerFlowStudyConfiguration,
    whose tolerance and max_iterations define study-side convergence
    settings. When the caller omitted explicit solver options, the method
    delegated to from_prepared with None; the constructor then created
    default SolverOptions, silently ignoring the configuration's
    max_iterations value and relying on a separate default for tolerance.

Correction:
    - When options are not explicitly supplied to from_network, construct
      SolverOptions from the configuration's tolerance and max_iterations.
    - Preserve explicitly supplied options as caller overrides.
    - Keep prepared-snapshot and numerical solver boundaries unchanged.

Disposition:
    This corrects the direct from_network default-options handoff. It does
    not make a PreparedPowerFlow snapshot carry solver settings; callers
    using from_prepared still need to supply options explicitly if they
    require settings from an external study configuration.

Verification boundary:
    - Implementation and register re-fetched after correction.
    - No tests or CI run; runtime behavior remains unverified.

2026-10-23 — POWER FLOW SNAPSHOT GENERATION TYPE VALIDATION
======================================================================

File corrected:
    core/analysis/power_flow_preparation.py

Batch audit scope:
    Reviewed the Power Flow configuration, preparation snapshot, analysis
    orchestration, result conversion, and package export boundaries.

Source evidence:
    PreparedPowerFlow previously checked activation_generation by applying
    int(value) for the positivity comparison, but retained the original
    value. This could accept a positive fractional float such as 1.5 even
    though the declared contract is int | None.

Correction:
    - Reject booleans and non-integer values with TypeError.
    - Retain the positive-value check for integer generations.
    - Leave topology identity values and result conversion unchanged.

Disposition:
    The activation-generation field now enforces its declared integer type
    at snapshot construction. This batch review does not claim that every
    Power Flow preparation, solver, or result-consumer contract is closed.

    - Source and register re-fetched after the correction.
    - No tests or CI run; runtime behavior remains unverified.

2026-10-23 — POWER FLOW SNAPSHOT PROVENANCE CONSISTENCY
======================================================================

File corrected:
    core/analysis/power_flow_preparation.py

Batch audit scope:
    Reviewed Power Flow prepared-snapshot identity and revision fields,
    YBus revision propagation, and related Analysis/solver boundaries.

Source evidence:
    PreparedPowerFlow checked project_id only for blankness after coercion
    to str, so a non-string value could be retained despite the declared
    str | None contract. topology_revision had no local type/range
    validation, and the snapshot did not reject conflicting non-null
    topology revisions supplied by its YBus.

Correction:
    - Require supplied project_id to be a non-empty string.
    - Require supplied topology_revision to be an integer (not bool) and
      non-negative.
    - Reject conflicting non-null PreparedPowerFlow and YBus revisions.
    - Preserve optional provenance fields for compatibility with manually
      assembled snapshots where provenance is absent.

Disposition:
    This enforces local provenance shape and detects revision mismatch when
    both values are present. It does not establish project/generation
    consistency against a live project, nor require YBus revision metadata
    when it is absent.

Verification boundary:
    - Source and register re-fetched after the correction.
    - No tests or CI run; runtime behavior remains unverified.

2026-10-23 — POWER FLOW RESULT BUS-IDENTITY CHECK
======================================================================

File corrected:
    core/analysis/power_flow_result_conversion.py

Batch audit scope:
    Traced numerical Power Flow result bus identity into engineering
    result conversion.

Source evidence:
    PowerFlowResult can carry bus_ids, but the converter previously checked
    only the number of voltage magnitudes and angles against caller-supplied
    bus IDs. A same-length result from a different bus ordering could
    therefore be converted and associated with the wrong bus labels.

Correction:
    - When PowerFlowResult.bus_ids is populated, require exact identity and
      ordering equality with the bus_ids supplied to the converter.
    - Preserve compatibility with legacy results whose bus_ids are empty;
      those results still rely on the caller's detached ordering contract.

Disposition:
    Prevents a known bus-order mismatch when numerical result identity is
    available. It does not attach project/generation/topology provenance to
    PowerFlowResult, nor does it verify active-project freshness.

Verification boundary:
    - Source and register re-fetched after correction.
    - No tests or CI run; runtime behavior remains unverified.

2026-10-24 — SLD CORE-DELETE ANNOTATION LIFECYCLE
======================================================================

File:
    core/application/services/sld_service.py

Finding:
    Core-driven deletion of an SLD-bound equipment object can remove its
    presentation node and attached connections through
    reconcile_element_delete(), while leaving SLD annotation blocks that
    reference the now-missing node. A later canvas projection can then fail
    when resolving the stale annotation reference.

Correction:
    - Snapshot annotation configuration before node removal.
    - Remove annotation blocks bound to the deleted node in the same
      Application transaction.
    - Register the inverse before mutation; undo restores the node,
      attached connections, and annotation configuration together.
    - Preserve annotation blocks for an intentionally orphaned SLD node,
      because that node remains in the presentation document and the
      annotation reference remains structurally valid.

Verification boundary:
    Static source/diff review only. No tests, CI, syntax/import execution,
    application startup, or GUI runtime verification performed.

Disposition:
    Core-driven deletion annotation-reference integrity corrected in this
    change; runtime confirmation remains outstanding.

2026-10-24 — SLD ORPHANING TRANSACTION INVERSE ORDER
======================================================================

File:
    core/application/services/sld_service.py

Finding:
    reconcile_element_delete() mutated a customized SLD node and its attached
    connections before registering the transaction inverse. A failure while
    orphaning any connection could leave partial presentation changes without
    a registered rollback operation.

Correction:
    - Register the node/connection snapshot inverse before the first mutation.
    - Restore an existing orphaned node in place during undo; recreate it
      only if it is absent, avoiding duplicate-node restoration failures.
    - Restore attached connection snapshots and mark the document modified
      when the inverse runs.
    - Preserve the existing field-level ownership behavior and retain
      annotation blocks for the intentionally orphaned node.

Verification boundary:
    Static source and diff review only. No tests, CI, syntax/import execution,
    application startup, or GUI runtime verification performed.

Disposition:
    The inverse-registration ordering gap is corrected in source; runtime
    confirmation remains outstanding.

2026-10-24 — SLD CONNECTION / NODE DELETE INVERSE ORDER
======================================================================

File:
    core/application/services/sld_service.py

Finding:
    reconcile_connection_delete() registered undo only after removing or
    mutating a presentation connection. Its customized-connection inverse
    also attempted to recreate a connection whose identity still existed,
    risking a duplicate-identity failure during undo. The direct
    _remove_node() command likewise registered its inverse after mutation.

Correction:
    - Register inverses before connection or node deletion mutations.
    - Restore an authored/orphaned connection in place when it still exists;
      recreate it only when absent.
    - Restore the customized connection's prior endpoints and properties,
      preserving its existing route and structural identity.
    - Register the direct node-removal inverse before removing the node,
      attached connections, and annotations.

Verification boundary:
    Static source/diff review only. No tests, CI, syntax/import execution,
    application startup, or GUI runtime verification performed.

Disposition:
    Pre-mutation inverse registration and in-place restoration are corrected
    in source; runtime confirmation remains outstanding.

2026-10-24 — SLD ELEMENT UPDATE INVERSE ORDER
======================================================================

File:
    core/application/services/sld_service.py

Finding:
    reconcile_element_update() changed the live SLD node's projected
    properties, lifecycle state, and equipment binding before registering
    restore_updated_node with the Application transaction. A failure during
    presentation mutation could therefore leave a partial update without an
    inverse. Registering the inverse late also made rollback correctness
    depend on the update having completed.

    - Apply the prepared property mapping and equipment binding to the
      existing canonical node rather than replacing its identity.
    - Retain in-place rollback restoration of position, presentation,
      properties, and equipment binding, with absent-node restoration only
      as a defensive fallback.

    or verified in this batch.

2026-10-24 — SLD ELEMENT DELETE RESTORATION IDENTITY SAFETY
======================================================================

File:
    core/application/services/sld_service.py

Finding:
    reconcile_element_delete() had restoration paths that could silently skip
    a connection with a matching ID even when it was a different object, or
    remove and recreate a connection without checking whether its identity
    had been replaced by another mutation. The node restore path could also
    fail on partial rollback when the original node still existed, or treat
    an identity collision as ordinary absence.

Correction:
    - Capture original live node and attached-connection references before
      mutation, alongside immutable state snapshots.
    - Restore the original node and connection objects in place when they
      remain present; recreate only absent objects.
    - Fail closed when a different live object owns a snapshotted identity.
    - Restore annotations with the node-removal inverse and keep the inverse
      registered before the first mutation.
    - Preserve route objects and unrelated state not mutated by this delete
      path; restore the fields that the operation can change.

Verification boundary:
    Static source and diff review only. No tests or CI run. Python syntax,
    import integrity, application startup, and GUI runtime were not executed
    or verified in this batch.

Disposition:
    Identity-safe restoration correction merged in PR #565
    (merge commit 2abf1febc081d4e19ae2222aca1aeb90102bae46).

2026-10-24 — SLD ADD INVERSE IDENTITY SAFETY
======================================================================

File:
    core/application/services/sld_service.py

Finding:
    _add_node() and _add_connection() registered pre-mutation inverses that
    later removed whatever object happened to occupy the requested ID. After
    a remove/restore cycle or identity reuse, rollback/undo could therefore
    delete an unrelated node or connection with the same canonical ID.

Correction:
    - Capture the exact created node/connection reference after successful
      model insertion and require that identity during later undo.
    - For construction failures before the reference is captured, remove a
      partially inserted object only when its available creation signature
      matches the operation; otherwise fail closed on the collision.
    - Preserve pre-mutation inverse registration for partial-failure
      compensation.

Verification boundary:
    Static source and diff review only. No tests or CI run. Python syntax,
    import integrity, application startup, and GUI runtime were not executed
    or verified in this batch.

    Add-inverse identity correction merged in PR #566
    (merge commit f4b039b6ac7126c7546640afaecbd8d54d9a483a).

2026-10-24 — SLD WIRE RECONCILIATION ROLLBACK SCOPE
======================================================================

File:
    core/application/services/sld_service.py

Finding:
    reconcile_simple_wire_projection() previously removed every live SLD
    connection and recreated the pre-reconciliation snapshot on rollback.
    Endpoint reconciliation also removed/recreated a connection in place of
    updating it. These broad operations could invalidate untouched object
    references and make rollback destructive when IDs were reused. A failure
    during reconciliation before the rollback callback was returned could
    also leave partial mutations.

Correction:
    - Snapshot original connection references and serialized state before
      reconciliation.
    - Update changed endpoint fields in place, preserving canonical connection
      identity; preserve legacy migration fields through the snapshot.
    - Track newly created connections and remove only their exact identities
      during rollback, with a constrained signature fallback for partial
      creation failures.
    - Restore original objects in place and reject conflicting identity
      owners instead of deleting them.
    - Run the same rollback on exceptions raised before the callback returns.

Verification boundary:
    Static source/diff review only. No tests or CI run. Python syntax,
    import integrity, application startup, and GUI runtime were not executed
    or verified in this batch.

Disposition:
    Focused rollback-scope correction prepared on the branch pending review.

2026-10-24 — SLD INSERTION ROLLBACK IDENTITY SAFETY
======================================================================

File:
    core/application/services/sld_service.py

Finding:
    reconcile_insertion() removed replacement connections and a newly
    inserted node by canonical ID during rollback without checking whether
    those IDs still referred to objects created by the insertion. Its
    original-connection restore path also treated any occupied ID as if the
    original connection had been restored, silently accepting identity reuse.

Correction:
    - Capture exact references for the node and replacement connections as
      they are inserted.
    - Remove only those exact created objects during rollback; use narrow
      creation-signature checks only when an exception occurs before a
      creation call returns and its reference can be captured.
    - Reject conflicting live objects that reuse the inserted node,
      replacement connection, or original connection identity.
    - Restore the original connection snapshot only when its identity is
      absent, preserving the original live object when it remains registered.

Verification boundary:
    Static source/diff review only. No tests or CI run. Python syntax,
    import integrity, application startup, and GUI runtime were not executed

2026-10-24 — SLD CONNECTION CREATION COMPENSATION SIGNATURE
======================================================================

File:
    core/application/services/sld_service.py

Finding:
    _add_connection()'s partial-creation compensation compared serialized
    live endpoints against raw command endpoint values. When the payload used
    SLDEndpoint instances, equivalent canonical endpoint data compared
    unequal, so a partially inserted connection could be left behind rather
    than compensated. The structural signature also omitted route state.

Correction:
    - Canonicalize mapping and SLDEndpoint inputs to the same serialized form.
    - Include normalized route state in the compensation signature.
    - Retain the existing fail-closed identity-collision behavior.

    core/application/draft/network.py

    - Preserve the first connection and reject the duplicate with its

2026-10-26 — DRAFT WIRE PREFLIGHT BEFORE TRANSACTION UNDO REGISTRATION
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/draft/network.py
    core/application/draft/handlers.py

Finding:
    DraftCommandHandlers.add_connection() registered a full DraftNetwork
    snapshot inverse before DraftNetwork.add_connection() performed its
    rejection checks. On a rejected candidate, CommandManager rolled back the
    transaction even though no draft mutation had occurred. Snapshot restore
    reconstructs the draft equipment/connection objects, needlessly replacing
    unchanged object instances and risking stale references in projections.

Correction:
    - Add DraftNetwork.validate_connection_candidate() as a non-mutating
      domain preflight using the same canonical checks as add_connection().
    - Invoke preflight before recording the transaction inverse.
    - Keep add_connection() independently validating immediately before its
      mutation, so the domain boundary remains defensive.
    - Invalid connection commands now fail before adding an inverse operation;
      valid commands still register exact snapshot restoration before mutation.

Verification boundary:
    Focused source and diff review. No tests or CI run; syntax, import,
    startup, and GUI/runtime behavior were not executed or verified.

Disposition:
    Proposed in draft PR #651; not merged.

2026-10-26 — WIRE COMMAND FAILURE FEEDBACK
======================================================================

File:
    ui/tools/wire_tool.py

Finding:
    WireTool preserved its transient routing preview after a rejected
    connection, but did not surface the Application rejection reason in the
    preview. Duplicate endpoint-pair and other validation failures could
    leave the interaction appearing stuck without actionable feedback.

Correction:
    - Store unsuccessful result messages in ConnectionPreview validation
      state and mark the attempted target invalid.
    - Put the failure reason in the current transient preview segment tooltip.
    - Latch the failure tooltip while the engineer moves the cursor, clearing it
      on a new endpoint attempt or when the wire interaction is cancelled/reset.
    - Preserve the original exception as last_command_result and re-raise it
      so the existing interaction/error boundary still observes the failure.
    - Retain the source endpoint for retry or Escape cancellation; clear the
      preview only after successful connection creation.
    - Keep all feedback presentation-only; do not bypass Application/Core
      validation or create electrical topology from graphics.

    Focused source and diff review. No tests or CI run; syntax, imports,
    startup, and GUI runtime remain unverified.

2026-10-26 — MOTOR DYNAMICS EXTENSION Qt CHARTS AND EVENT BOUNDARY
======================================================================

Files:
    ui/core/qt.py
    plugins/motor_dynamics/dashboard.py

Finding:
    The initial motor telemetry dashboard imported Qt Charts classes from
    ui.core.qt even though the canonical boundary did not export those
    classes. It also subscribed to ApplicationEventBus with a string event
    name, while the actual bus requires an ApplicationEvent subclass and
    publishes synchronously. Direct chart mutation from a synchronous event
    callback could therefore violate Qt widget thread affinity.

Correction:
    - Export QChart, QChartView, QLineSeries, and QValueAxis through the
      existing PySide6-only ui.core.qt boundary.
    - Subscribe using the canonical StudyCompleted event class.
    - Marshal event delivery to the widget through a Qt signal bridge before
      updating chart series and axes.
    - Unsubscribe using the same event class and handler during close.

Verification boundary:
    Source-contract reconciliation only. No tests or CI run. Qt Charts
    availability in the target runtime, syntax/import integrity, and GUI
    thread behavior remain unverified.

Disposition:
    Proposed in draft PR #674; not merged.

GF-MOTOR-001
======================================================================

Scope:
    plugins/motor_dynamics/commands.py
    plugins/motor_dynamics/plugin.py
    Core Application command / transaction boundary

Status:
    🟡 COMMAND ENVELOPE RECONCILED — DOMAIN SERVICE CONTRACT BLOCKED

Source-audit findings:
    Motor commands now use the canonical immutable Command envelope.
    Handler shape follows CommandManager:
        handler(command, context, transaction) -> ApplicationResult

    CommandManager owns transaction commit/rollback. Motor handlers must
    register inverse operations on the supplied Transaction before each
    mutation and must not begin, commit, or roll back a separate transaction.

    The existing DraftNetwork commit path prepares canonical equipment
    creation commands and SimpleWire commands. No verified production
    operation was found that splits an existing SimpleWire and inserts a
    motor while preserving endpoint identity, topology invariants, undo,
    and redo semantics.

Decision:
    Do not emulate wire splitting by editing SLD graphics or by inventing
    a second topology service. Motor-to-wire insertion remains blocked
    until a Core/Application-owned atomic topology operation is defined
    against the canonical Network and SimpleWire contracts.

Remaining integration requirements:
    1. Provide the explicit motor_dynamics_composition adapter.
    2. Provide an actual motor draft service and atomic motor topology
       operation.
    3. Register handlers through the host CommandManager composition.
    4. Record inverse operations on the host transaction before mutation.
    5. Verify StudyCompleted payload semantics before dashboard refresh.

Validation:
    No tests or CI run, per project instruction.
    Runtime activation and import integrity remain unverified.
