GF-MOTOR-093
======================================================================

Scope:
    Batch time-grid validation before backend dispatch

Files:
    plugins/motor_dynamics/transient_solver.py

Status:
    🟡 IMPLEMENTED — SOURCE REVIEW ONLY

Finding:
    Batch time validation was reached inside the selected solver. Invalid
    duration or step values could therefore trigger accelerator selection and
    be recorded as an accelerator failure before the CPU reference path
    rejected the same invalid user input.

Correction:
    The batch API now calls the shared _validate_time adapter immediately
    after validating non-empty, matching batch lengths and before capability
    discovery or backend dispatch. Invalid duration/step values fail at the
    public API boundary and are not classified as CUDA failures or masked by
    accelerator-to-CPU fallback. The single-motor path continues to use the
    same shared uniform_time_grid contract.

Boundary:
    This correction centralizes time-grid validation timing; it does not
    validate backend runtime behavior or prove CPU/CUDA numerical parity.

Validation:
    Source edits and source re-fetch only. No tests, syntax checks, imports,
    Numba compilation, CUDA/NPU hardware execution, numerical comparisons,
    performance measurements, or CI were run.

======================================================================

GF-MOTOR-094 — Shared numerical backend execution contract. Added execute_numerical_problem() and NumericalExecution in core/numerics/backend.py, exported through core/numerics/__init__.py. The shared entry point selects an adapter from declared capability facts, validates the returned ODESolution type, state count, exact shared time grid, flat buffer length, finite state values, and sample period. A failed or incompatible non-CPU adapter is retried using a compatible declared CPU adapter; fallback failure details and the actual executed adapter are retained. CPU failures propagate. This standardizes dispatch/result integrity, not plugin physics or accelerator compilation; NPU remains adapter/workload-dependent and is not claimed for motor transients. Source edits and source re-fetch only; no tests, syntax/import checks, runtime adapter execution, accelerator hardware, numerical comparisons, performance measurements, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-095 — Shared backend compatibility hardening. Explicit backend requests now choose the first compatible adapter of the requested kind rather than considering only the first registered adapter of that kind. execute_numerical_problem validates the initial state shape and finiteness, time-grid inputs, and float64 precision before backend selection/invocation, keeping malformed problem inputs outside accelerator-failure fallback. Source review only; no tests, syntax/import checks, runtime execution, hardware execution, numerical comparisons, performance measurements, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-096 — Numerical result-contract integrity. The shared backend runner now rejects adapter results whose time-grid or flat state-buffer dtype violates the float64 contract, and verifies that the first returned state exactly preserves the problem's declared initial state. These checks strengthen result integrity but do not establish physical equivalence of plugin-specific derivatives or accelerator kernels; those remain workload-adapter responsibilities. Source review only; no tests, syntax/import checks, runtime execution, hardware execution, numerical comparisons, performance measurements, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-097 — Shared ODE solution immutability and storage contract. The backend runner now validates that state_count is a positive, non-boolean integer matching the declared initial-state dimension, the time grid is read-only, and the flat state buffer is contiguous and read-only in addition to existing float64, shape, finite-value, initial-state, and time-grid checks. Invalid accelerator outputs are rejected before they can be returned to consumers and enter the declared CPU fallback path. This validates storage and interface semantics only, not physical equivalence of specialized kernels. Source review only; no tests, syntax/import checks, runtime execution, hardware execution, numerical comparisons, performance measurements, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-098 — Immutable backend execution diagnostics. NumericalExecution now exposes a read-only tuple of stable key/value strings covering requested and selected backend, backend kind, selection reason, rejected adapters, actual executed backend, runtime fallback status/failure, and successful outcome. This is a numerical result envelope only: application/study layers may publish the metadata through their existing result/event lifecycle, while the numerical core remains unaware of UI, Qt, and electrical-domain mutation. The diagnostics report the adapter's declared capability and execution path; they do not certify physical equivalence. Source review only; no tests, syntax/import checks, runtime execution, hardware execution, numerical comparisons, performance measurements, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-099 — Distinguish policy fallback from runtime fallback. NumericalExecution diagnostics now separately report policy_fallback_used and runtime_fallback_used, plus a fallback_kind classification (none, policy, runtime, or policy_and_runtime). An explicit accelerator request that is rejected during capability selection and intentionally selects CPU is now correctly identified as a fallback even when no runtime exception occurred. Runtime fallback remains tied to an actual accelerator execution/result-contract failure. This is diagnostic metadata only and does not publish study results or mutate domain state; application integration must still use the existing StudyCompleted/result lifecycle. Source review only; no tests, syntax/import checks, runtime execution, hardware execution, numerical comparisons, performance measurements, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-100 — Publish solver diagnostics through the existing successful-study projection lifecycle. MotorSimulationResult.publish_to now forwards its dispatch/integrator diagnostics into MotorSimulationReadModelStore; SimulationSummary exposes detached, recursively immutable diagnostic metadata. The existing MotorTransientResultPublisher remains the only bridge: it reacts to StudyCompleted, retrieves the already-stored StudyResult, verifies completed status and motor_transient type, then publishes channels and diagnostics together. No new event bus, direct UI dispatch, or Core mutation path was introduced. Source review only; no tests, syntax/import checks, runtime execution, hardware execution, numerical comparisons, performance measurements, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-101 — Reject stale/out-of-order motor study completions. The plugin now subscribes to canonical StudyStarted as well as StudyCompleted events. MotorTransientResultPublisher records a monotonic order for each motor-transient study at start and supplies it to the read-model publication. MotorSimulationReadModelStore rejects a result whose start order is older than the latest accepted result for that motor, and replaces channel arrays plus summary/diagnostics atomically under one lock. Missing start-order metadata fails closed rather than publishing an unorderable result. This protects against an older, slower study completing after a newer study; a newer study still running does not invalidate an older result until the newer result is actually published. Source review only; no tests, syntax/import checks, runtime execution, hardware execution, numerical comparisons, performance measurements, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-102 — Motor study lifecycle cleanup and shutdown safety. The publisher now discards ordering metadata on StudyFailed and StudyCancelled, removes a study's ordering record on every valid StudyCompleted path (including missing/invalid stored results), and exposes close() to clear retained IDs and reject future callbacks. Plugin teardown closes the publisher before unsubscribing; publication is serialized against close under the publisher lifecycle lock so shutdown waits for any in-flight read-model publication and no new publication can begin after close returns. Terminal event subscriptions are included in the teardown path. Source review only; no tests, syntax/import checks, runtime execution, hardware execution, numerical comparisons, performance measurements, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-103 — Partial initialization rollback hardening. The motor plugin now installs identity-safe study-handler rollback ownership before attempting StudyService registration, and installs publisher close/unsubscribe rollback before the first event subscription. If any subscription fails midway, rollback attempts to close the publisher and unsubscribe every owned lifecycle callback, then unregister the study handler and release command registration. Existing failed-cleanup handles remain retained for retry through shutdown(). Source review only; no tests, syntax/import checks, runtime execution, hardware execution, numerical comparisons, performance measurements, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-104 — Consolidated motor-dynamics closeout audit. Corrected MotorSimulationResult.publish_to() to return the read-model acceptance result and reject non-finite/non-numeric load torque, preventing successful publications from being misreported as stale. Read-model study ordering now rejects duplicate as well as out-of-order completions; study lookup history is bounded to 4096 entries and read models expose clear() to remove cached telemetry/order metadata on successful plugin shutdown. Plugin lifecycle now prevents reinitialization while incomplete rollback remains, unwinds study publisher/handler before command registration, and clears cached projections only after all teardown succeeds. Publisher captures study-result lookup exceptions rather than letting telemetry retrieval errors escape the completion callback. Source-level review only. No tests, syntax/import checks, runtime execution, hardware execution, numerical comparisons, performance measurements, or CI were run. PR mergeability and numerical/runtime acceptance remain unresolved and must not be represented as complete.

2026-10-27 — SHARED PLUGIN SDK ARCHITECTURE AMENDMENT (PROPOSAL)
======================================================================

File:
    docs/architecture/GRIDFORGE_V2_SHARED_PLUGIN_SDK_ARCHITECTURE.md

Finding:
    Future plugin growth would duplicate lifecycle, study orchestration,
    event handling, result publication, configuration, compute dispatch,
    diagnostics, and UI projection infrastructure, increasing maintenance
    cost and creating inconsistent plugin behavior.

Architecture decision proposed:
    - Reuse one authoritative owner for each shared responsibility; adapt
      existing Application and plugin-platform services rather than create
      a competing framework.
    - Define small typed SDK contracts for plugin identity/capabilities,
      lifecycle/registration, study integration, event cleanup, result
      publication, configuration, compute capabilities, diagnostics, and
      optional read-only UI projections.
    - Keep Core headless and authoritative; Application owns study execution,
      controlled mutation, canonical events, and result/read-model contracts.
    - Keep plugins focused on domain algorithms, inputs, typed results, and
      genuinely plugin-specific integration/presentation.
    - Require CPU as the portable baseline where supported; treat GPU/NPU as
      optional, capability-gated backends. Never imply NPU physics support
      from inference-provider availability.
    - Prefer composition and explicit plugin kinds over a universal base
      class. Do not create duplicate managers, registries, schedulers,
      event buses, or result stores.
    - Audit current implementations, refactor Motor Dynamics as the first
      study-plugin reference, then validate the abstraction against a second
      distinct plugin before freezing the SDK.

Verification boundary:
    Architecture source review and repository-path inspection only. No tests,
    CI, syntax checks, imports, runtime, numerical, or hardware validation run.

Disposition:
    Proposed in branch architecture/shared-plugin-sdk-v2. This is not yet
    frozen or merged; it supplements, and does not supersede, the existing
    frozen GridForge V2 architecture until reviewed and accepted.
======================================================================


======================================================================
2026-10-27 — SHARED PLUGIN SDK PROPOSAL RECONCILIATION / FREEZE BOUNDARY
======================================================================

Files:
    docs/architecture/GRIDFORGE_V2_SHARED_PLUGIN_SDK_ARCHITECTURE.md
    Modification_Register.txt

Static audit findings:
    - core/application/study.py already owns StudyService execution, typed
      StudyResult storage, cancellation, and study lifecycle events. Its
      audited main version exposes register/execute/cancel/result retrieval,
      but not unregister.
    - core/application/event_bus.py already owns canonical synchronous
      Application event delivery and subscribe/unsubscribe.
    - ui/plugins/plugin_contract.py and ui/plugins/README.md already define
      UI plugin contracts and separate PluginLoader, PluginRegistry,
      PluginManager, PluginStateStore, and PluginContext responsibilities.
    - The engineering plugins/ namespace and ui/plugins/ composition
      subsystem must remain distinct. A shared SDK must not collapse them
      into one lifecycle manager.
    - Motor Dynamics feature branch expects StudyService.unregister(), which
      is absent from the audited main baseline. This is a concrete integration
      dependency to reconcile before claiming reference-plugin compatibility.

Correction to proposal:
    - Made the StudyService callable signature and existing event/result
      ownership explicit.
    - Required reuse of ApplicationEventBus.
    - Preserved existing UI plugin lifecycle ownership.
    - Froze architecture principles only; concrete SDK package placement,
      class names, signatures, and any StudyService API additions remain open
      until a reuse matrix and a second distinct plugin are reconciled.
    - Prohibited a parallel generic plugin manager or duplicate study/result
      framework.

Freeze decision:
    Freeze the reuse and ownership principles as the architectural amendment.
    Do not freeze speculative implementation APIs before reference-plugin
    evidence exists.

Verification boundary:
    Static source inspection and documentation reconciliation only. No tests,
    CI, syntax checks, imports, runtime, numerical, or hardware validation run.

Disposition:
    Updated in PR #675 on architecture/shared-plugin-sdk-v2. Pending merge;
    the existing frozen Core/Application/UI architecture remains authoritative
    until the amendment is merged.

GF-APP-CREATION-001
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-27

Scope:
    Canonical equipment creation contract construction and regression alignment

Findings:
    creation_definition_for() built the complete equipment-definition catalogue
    while passing the requested equipment's terminal inventory into every
    definition. This made the current-transformer definition inherit an empty
    Bus inventory when the catalogue was built from the Bus entry, causing
    startup to fail with missing S1/S2 measurement terminals. The direct
    CreationCommandPreparer regression also used an obsolete call shape that
    omitted the now-required trusted canonical_contract and equipment_type.

Corrections:
    - Use each equipment type's own _BUILTIN_TERMINAL_NAMES entry when building
      that definition's terminal requirements.
    - Preserve the input terminal_names argument as a validation of the one
      requested equipment type, not as a shared inventory for the whole catalog.
    - Update the preparer regression to identify equipment_type and pass the
      trusted contract from creation_contracts_from_definitions().

Scope boundary:
    This is a focused Core/Application creation-contract correction. It does
    not weaken canonical contract enforcement or alter unrelated UI/plugin
    behavior.

Status:
    🟡 SOURCE CORRECTION APPLIED — AUTOMATIC WORKFLOW VERIFICATION PENDING

GF-SLD-ROUTE-001
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-27

Scope:
    Immutable route payload compatibility at SLD persistence boundary

Finding:
    DraftConnection freezes route payloads using mapping proxies and tuple
    point sequences. SLDRoute.from_dict accepted only list-valued points, so
    a valid immutable internal route payload was rejected during DraftNetwork
    commit despite having the canonical two-coordinate point shape.

Correction:
    Accept list or tuple as the point sequence container at the SLD boundary;
    SLDRoute still normalizes and validates coordinates and serializes back to
    the canonical JSON array representation.

GF-SLD-ENDPOINT-002
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-27

Scope:
    Draft endpoint identity translation into persistent SLD presentation

Finding:
    DraftNetwork committed endpoint mappings use Core/Application identity
    fields (kind, object_id, terminal_role, attachment_id). The SLD presentation
    schema instead requires node_id plus equipment_id/terminal_role or
    bus_id/attachment_id. Passing draft mappings directly into SLDService
    caused canonical endpoint parsing to reject object_id.

Correction:
    Add an explicit Application-side projection adapter that resolves the
    committed object to its unique SLD node and emits the strict SLDEndpoint
    mapping. Core endpoint identity remains authoritative; SLD stays projection.

GF-SLD-DRAFT-PRESENTATION-001
======================================================================

    2026-10-28

    commit projection passed that metadata directly to SLDService as if it were
    a canonical SymbolBase mapping. The resulting mapping had no symbol_id and
    failed strict SLD node creation during the runtime engineering smoke.

      non-empty canonical symbol_id.

      symbol presentation factory.

      command field and validate it before applying it to the resolved symbol.

      identity; do not invent a symbol when the canonical factory is absent.

      canonical workspace identifiers in the current definitions.

GF-MOTOR-105 — Fail-closed committed Bus resolution for draft connections
======================================================================

Scope:
    MotorDraftService.connect_motor_to_bus

Finding:
    A Bus ID absent from DraftNetwork was treated as a Core-scoped endpoint
    without first resolving it against the active project's authoritative
    Application read model. A typo or stale ID could therefore survive
    connection preflight and fail only later during commit.

Correction:
    Before constructing a Core-scoped DraftEndpointReference, the service
    now resolves the requested ID through Application.read_element("bus", id).
    A missing Bus is rejected at the Application command boundary. The
    returned read model must also identify itself as a Bus; the type check
    normalizes missing/None values before comparison so malformed read models
    fail closed with the intended validation error. Draft Bus handling remains
    unchanged, and no Core objects are passed into DraftNetwork.

Boundary:
    This checks existence and type through the canonical read boundary; it
    does not replace full topology, terminal, duplicate-connection, or commit
    validation.

Verification:
    Source inspection only. No tests, syntax checks, imports, application
    startup, runtime, or CI were run.

Disposition:
    Submitted in PR for review; merge and runtime validation pending.

GF-SLD-ROUTE-EDIT-001

    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Preserve distinct SLD presentation and Core identities during route edits

Finding:
    Route geometry is persisted on the SLD connection and therefore must be
    addressed by presentation connection_id. The same interaction request also
    carries core_connection_id, which must verify the domain binding rather
    than replace the SLD mutation key.

Correction:

      requests.
    - Keep presentation connection_id as the route mutation key.
    - Verify the live SLD connection's stored Core binding before submitting
      the existing Application route command.
    - Do not infer or synthesize one identity from the other.

    verification remains pending.

GF-MOTOR-106 — Reject duplicate motor identities in one transient batch
======================================================================

Finding:
    The batch solver accepted duplicate motor IDs. Results are keyed by motor
    identity in the read model, so two trajectories with the same identity
    could overwrite one another and make batch output ambiguous.

Correction:
    The public batch API now rejects repeated canonical motor IDs before
    backend discovery or execution. IDs are compared after trimming, matching
    the identity normalization used by result creation.

Boundary:
    This prevents ambiguous identity collisions; it does not validate
    cross-study concurrency or physical model equivalence.

Verification:
    Source inspection only. No tests, syntax checks, imports, runtime,
    accelerator hardware, or CI were run. The branch has been reconciled onto
    current main after the canonical startup/DraftNetwork correction in PR
    #683; automatic verification of this rebased head is pending.

Disposition:
    Submitted in a focused PR; review and runtime validation pending.

======================================================================
GF-MOTOR-107 — Preserve time-varying voltage profiles in CPU batch studies
======================================================================

Finding:
    solve_transient_batch() rejected every non-scalar dq voltage input before
    backend selection, even though solve_transient_cpu() supports validated
    time-varying voltage profiles. This unnecessarily restricted CPU batch
    studies and made backend='auto' reject a workload that the CPU reference
    solver can execute.

Correction:
    - Accept scalar or one-dimensional finite voltage profiles matching the
      validated integration grid.
    - Keep CUDA restricted to constant scalar dq voltages and reject an
      explicit CUDA request for profiles with a clear caller-facing error.
    - For backend='auto', expose only the CPU capability when profiles vary,
      so automatic dispatch selects the reference implementation.
    - Pass validated profiles through to each CPU motor trajectory unchanged.

Verification:
    Source inspection only. No tests, syntax/import checks, numerical parity,
    runtime, accelerator hardware, or CI were run manually. The branch has
    been reconciled onto current main after the duplicate-ID and explicit-CUDA
    contract fixes; automatic verification of this head is pending.

Disposition:
    Submitted in a focused PR; review and runtime validation pending.

GF-PLUGIN-SDK-001 — Shared Plugin SDK contract and activation validation
======================================================================

Files:
    core/plugin_sdk/contracts.py
    core/plugin_sdk/__init__.py
    core/plugin_sdk/README.md
    plugins/motor_dynamics/plugin.py

Finding:
    The first SDK contract draft defined plugin identity/version metadata and
    lifecycle protocols but did not validate API compatibility, and its
    compatibility helper was not adopted at a plugin activation boundary.
    The canonical UI plugin manager handles UI composition plugins and is not
    the engineering-plugin loader; routing motor dynamics through it would
    conflate separate plugin kinds and their identity namespaces.

Corrections:
    - Added SUPPORTED_PLUGIN_API_VERSION and the pure
      validate_plugin_descriptor() helper.
    - Hardened descriptor validation for plugin ID namespace/suffix,
      display-name type, capability metadata, expected identity, and
      supported API-version sets.
    - Added the immutable Motor Dynamics PLUGIN_DESCRIPTOR matching the
      existing manifest identity, version, API version, display name, and
      declared capabilities.
    - MotorDynamicsPlugin.initialize() validates its descriptor before any
      command, study, or event registration. The host may provide its explicit
      supported API-version set through
      PluginContext.metadata["host_supported_plugin_api_versions"].
    - Preserved the existing initialize(context)/shutdown() lifecycle and
      canonical host-created PluginContext. No competing plugin manager,
      context, event bus, study framework, or result store was added.
    - Updated SDK documentation to describe the activation boundary and
      compatibility fallback accurately.

Boundary:
    If the host does not advertise supported API versions in context metadata,
    Motor Dynamics uses the SDK's declared V2.0 compatibility set for backward
    compatibility. Hosts that enforce a distinct compatibility policy must
    provide their supported-version set. This does not constitute a generic
    engineering-plugin loader or discovery subsystem.

Verification:
    Repository source inspection and manifest/descriptor reconciliation only.
    No tests, CI, syntax checks, imports, numerical comparisons, or hardware
    validation were run. These remain outstanding and must not be inferred from
    successful GitHub writes or PR mergeability.

Status:
    🟡 SOURCE CORRECTIONS APPLIED — RUNTIME / AUTOMATED VALIDATION NOT RUN

GF-PLUGIN-SDK-005 — Motor activation context input validation
======================================================================

Files:
    plugins/motor_dynamics/plugin.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    The Motor Dynamics activation guard validated metadata shape but allowed
    malformed supported-version entries to reach frozenset conversion, and
    accessed context.services as if it were always a mapping. A malformed host
    context could therefore fail with an incidental TypeError/AttributeError
    rather than a deterministic contract error.

Correction:
    - Validate each advertised API version is a non-empty string before
      constructing the immutable version set.
    - Require PluginContext.services to be a Mapping before resolving the
      motor_dynamics_composition service.
    - Keep all checks before the first command/study/event registration, so
      invalid host context is rejected before activation side effects.

Verification:
    Source-level change and textual re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax/import
    checks, runtime activation/rollback checks, or hardware checks were run.

Status:
    🟡 INPUT CONTRACT HARDENED; ENGINEERING ACTIVATION OWNER STILL UNRESOLVED

Decision:
    The architecture owner approved a separate, narrowly scoped engineering-
    plugin host for PR #678. It is distinct from the UI PluginManager and does
    not provide automatic discovery or create duplicate Application services.

Corrections:
    - Added EngineeringPluginHost with explicit activation, descriptor/API
      preflight, duplicate active-ID rejection, immutable context creation,
      rollback attempt on initialization failure, reverse-order shutdown, and
      retained ownership when deactivation fails.
    - Added EngineeringPluginContext with immutable services/metadata mappings
      and the canonical Application reference.
    - Exported the host and context from core.plugin_sdk.
    - Deferred Motor Dynamics dashboard import until a UI parent is explicitly
      supplied so headless activation does not construct or import the widget.
    - Updated the architecture decision, ownership boundaries, and remaining
      integration gates.
    - Reuse the existing Application motor_dynamics_composition,
      StudyService, and ApplicationEventBus. No second event bus, study
      service, result store, or UI lifecycle manager was added.

Boundaries still open:
    - The host is not yet wired into a production composition root; this
      change does not claim Motor Dynamics automatically activates at startup.
    - The plugin remains the first lifecycle implementation; a second genuine
      non-UI lifecycle plugin must reconcile the shared contract.
    - Motor Dynamics numerical backend/hardware claims are not validated here.

Verification:
    GitHub source edits and textual re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax/import
    checks, runtime activation/rollback checks, or CPU/GPU/NPU validation ran.

Status:
    🟡 HOST IMPLEMENTED; COMPOSITION-ROOT INTEGRATION AND SECOND-PLUGIN
    RECONCILIATION REMAIN OPEN

======================================================================

GF-PLUGIN-SDK-008 — Retain ownership after failed activation rollback
======================================================================

Files:
    core/plugin_sdk/host.py
    core/plugin_sdk/README.md
    docs/architecture/GRIDFORGE_V2_SHARED_PLUGIN_SDK_ARCHITECTURE.md
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    EngineeringPluginHost attempted shutdown() when initialize() failed.
    If that rollback shutdown also failed, the host raised an error but did
    not retain the plugin instance. The host therefore lost its retry handle
    for resources or registrations that might still be owned by the plugin.

Correction:
    - Track rollback-failed plugin instances in a pending-cleanup registry,
      separate from successfully active plugins.
    - Expose pending_cleanup_plugin_ids without treating those plugins as
      successfully activated.
    - Reject reactivation of an ID while active or awaiting cleanup.
    - Allow deactivate(plugin_id) to retry pending cleanup.
    - Make shutdown_all() retry active plugins and pending rollback cleanup.
    - Remove retained ownership only after shutdown() succeeds.
    - Update SDK and architecture documentation to describe the contract.

Architecture:
    The engineering host remains separate from the UI PluginManager and
    reuses the existing Application services. No discovery framework,
    duplicate event bus, study service, result store, or scheduler was added.
    Production composition-root integration remains a separate open gate.

Verification:
    Focused source review and GitHub textual re-fetch only. No tests, CI,
    syntax/import checks, or runtime lifecycle checks were run.

Status:
    🟡 CORRECTED IN PR #678; RUNTIME LIFECYCLE VERIFICATION OUTSTANDING

GF-PLUGIN-SDK-010
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-27

Scope:
    EngineeringPluginHost reverse lifecycle ordering

Finding:
    Active plugins and failed-activation cleanup were traversed in separate
    groups. With interleaved activations, pending cleanup could run out of
    reverse lifecycle order.

Correction:
    Maintain one ownership-order ledger for both successfully active and
    cleanup-pending plugin instances. shutdown_all() traverses this ledger in
    reverse order and retains any entry whose shutdown fails. active_plugin_ids
    filters the same ledger to successful activations.

    🟡 SOURCE CORRECTION APPLIED — RUNTIME VERIFICATION NOT RUN

GF-AUD-074 — PROJECT/DOCUMENT TRANSITION CONTEXT INVALIDATION
======================================================================
Status: Corrected in branch `fix/project-document-context-sync`; static source re-audit pending.
Scope: `main.py`, `ui/workspace/project_workspace_adapter.py`.

Confirmed source defects addressed:
- Successful project close returns no ProjectContext, and the adapter previously emitted no ProjectWorkspaceChanged event for that successful transition. UI observers could therefore retain stale project/document context after close.
- The project-workspace handler previously updated only document_id for an SLD document, leaving project_id and project_name unsynchronized after project replacement/open.
- Switching project/document did not explicitly invalidate the prior SLD selection; selected object IDs could persist across document boundaries.

Correction:
- Publish the successful close transition using the previous project ID as event provenance when the resulting project is empty; keep cancellation and failed transitions unpublished.
- Synchronize project_id, project_name, document_id, and selected_ids from the committed workspace state.
- Clear selection when project/document identity changes; clear stale SLD presentation and annotation controls for an empty/non-SLD state.

Verification: Static source inspection only; no tests, CI, or GUI runtime performed. Re-audit callback ordering, presentation bridge behavior, and action state before freezing this entry.

GF-AUD-075 — COMMAND-HISTORY ACTION PRESENTATION REFRESH
======================================================================
Status: Corrected in branch `fix/refresh-history-action-state`; static source re-audit pending.
Scope: `main.py`.

Confirmed source issue:
- Undo/redo enabled state is computed from Controller.can_undo()/can_redo(), but menu and toolbar presentation refresh was subscribed to EngineeringContextStore updates only. Controller emits state_changed for command/state transitions that do not necessarily change engineering context, so QAction enabled state could remain stale until another context update or action dispatch.

Correction:
- Subscribe the shared action-presentation refresh to Controller.state_changed in addition to engineering-context changes. This keeps menu and toolbar enabled states aligned with the canonical undo/redo capability provider.

Verification: Static source inspection and branch re-fetch only; no tests, CI, or GUI runtime performed.

GF-AUD-076 — Project-transition command-history isolation
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Application undo/redo lifecycle across project activation transitions

Files:
    core/application/application.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Application._run_project_transition() reset revision state after a
    successful project lifecycle transition but retained the shared
    CommandManager undo/redo stacks. Commands and inverse journals recorded
    against an outgoing project could therefore remain available after open,
    new-project, close, or discard transitions and be replayed against a
    different active network.

Correction:
    - Clear the existing CommandManager history only after the lifecycle
      transition returns successfully.
    - Preserve history when the lifecycle transition itself fails and its
      rollback path restores the previous project.
    - Always attempt revision reset and validation invalidation after a
      committed transition, even if clearing command history fails.
    - Preserve and re-raise the first post-commit error after mandatory state
      reconciliation; a validation-event publication error does not mask an
      earlier history-clear error.
    - Reuse the canonical Application/CommandManager history boundary; no
      duplicate history manager or UI-side history state was introduced.

Verification:
    Static source inspection and direct GitHub re-fetch only. Confirmed the
    clear occurs after successful transition return, and mandatory revision/
    validation reconciliation follows it even if history clearing fails.
    No tests, CI, imports, syntax checks, or runtime/GUI verification were run.

Status:
    🟡 CORRECTION PROPOSED IN FOCUSED PR; RUNTIME VERIFICATION NOT RUN

======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Application undo/redo lifecycle across project activation transitions

Files:
    core/application/application.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Application._run_project_transition() reset revision state after a

Correction:
    - Clear the existing CommandManager history only after the lifecycle

    - Preserve history when the lifecycle transition itself fails and its

GF-AUD-077 — Refresh Controller state after command failures
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    UI Controller command/history presentation synchronization

Files:
    ui/core/controller.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Controller.execute_command(), undo(), and redo() emitted state_changed
    only after successful Application calls. CommandManager can change its
    integrity state while reporting a failure, particularly when rollback,
    inverse execution, or history restoration fails. Without a signal, menus
    and toolbars can retain stale Undo/Redo availability.

Correction:
    - Emit state_changed in finally for execute_command(), undo(), and redo().
    - Preserve Application return values and propagate original exceptions.
    - Do not add a second history state source or alter transaction semantics.

Verification:
    Static source re-fetch and exact method-body checks only. No tests, CI,
    imports, syntax checks, or runtime/GUI verification were run.

    CORRECTION PROPOSED IN FOCUSED PR; RUNTIME VERIFICATION NOT RUN

GF-AUD-078 — Isolate Application event subscriber failures
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    ApplicationEventBus synchronous dispatch semantics

Files:
    core/application/event_bus.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    ApplicationEventBus.publish() called each subscriber directly and let
    subscriber exceptions escape. Application.execute(), undo(), and redo()
    publish semantic events after authoritative command/history operations.
    A failing observer could therefore make a committed operation appear to
    have failed and prevent remaining subscribers from receiving the event.

Correction:
    - Catch ordinary Exception per subscriber and continue dispatching to
      remaining subscribers.
    - Log event type and handler context with traceback for diagnostics.
    - Do not catch BaseException, alter event ordering, or change command
      transaction/history semantics.

Verification:
    Static re-fetch and source-pattern checks only. No tests, CI, imports,
    syntax checks, or runtime/GUI verification were run.

Status:
    CORRECTION PROPOSED IN FOCUSED PR; RUNTIME VERIFICATION NOT RUN

======================================================================
GF-AUD-079 — Linearize study cancellation against terminal completion
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Application StudyService cancellation/completion concurrency

Files:
    core/application/study.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    StudyService checked token.cancelled outside its lock, then later stored a
    completed result. A concurrent cancel_study() could return True after the
    completion check but before result storage, while the final stored result
    remained completed. The token also remained cancellable until the finally
    cleanup, making the accepted cancellation outcome ambiguous.

Correction:
    - Add an explicit terminal state to StudyCancellationToken.
    - Serialize cancel() and finish() through StudyService's existing lock.
    - Decide and store completed versus cancelled result under that same lock.
    - Reject cancellation once terminal completion has been selected.
    - Mark the token finished on handler failure so late cancellation cannot
      report acceptance for a study that has already failed.
    - Keep event publication outside the service lock and retain existing
      active-token/type-count cleanup.

Verification:
    Static source edits and direct GitHub re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax,
    imports, concurrency stress checks, or runtime/GUI verification were run.

Status:
    🟡 CORRECTION PROPOSED IN FOCUSED PR; CONCURRENCY VERIFICATION NOT RUN

======================================================================
GF-AUD-080
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    RevisionService undo/redo transition exception safety

Finding:
    record_undo() and record_redo() removed the top revision transition before
    checking that its expected revision matched the current revision. A state
    mismatch therefore raised after destructively consuming the transition;
    record_undo() also removed its transition before validating/converting the
    optional Core topology revision. Application command history could remain
    intact while revision history was silently shortened.

Correction:
    - Peek at the transition and validate the expected current state first.
    - Construct and validate the complete target ProjectRevision before changing
      either revision stack.
    - Pop the source transition only after validation succeeds.
    - Preserve the existing revision values and undo/redo semantics.

Verification:
    Static source re-fetch and ordering checks only. No tests, CI, syntax/import
    checks, or runtime verification were run.

Status:
    🟡 CORRECTION APPLIED — STATIC CHECKS ONLY

======================================================================


======================================================================
GF-AUD-081
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Application explicit command-history clearing and revision journal alignment

Finding:
    Application.clear_history() cleared CommandManager undo/redo records but
    retained RevisionService undo/redo transitions. Those independent journals
    could diverge: revision transitions for commands no longer present in the
    command history remained available to later revision bookkeeping.

Correction:
    - Add RevisionService.clear_transitions() to clear only its undo/redo
      transition journals while preserving current and persisted revisions.
    - Call it after CommandManager.clear_history() succeeds.
    - Do not reset project revisions or mark the project clean.

Verification:
    Static source re-fetch and call-order checks only. No tests, CI,
    syntax/import checks, or runtime verification were run.

Status:
    🟡 CORRECTION APPLIED — STATIC CHECKS ONLY

======================================================================
GF-AUD-082
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Project lifecycle activation rollback under process-level interruption

Finding:
    ProjectLifecycleService._activate_candidate() performed a multi-component
    activation transaction but caught Exception rather than BaseException.
    KeyboardInterrupt or SystemExit raised after one or more activators had
    changed state could bypass the rollback stack, leaving the active
    presentation/network components partially switched while lifecycle
    authority still described the previous project. The presentation activator
    already handles BaseException, demonstrating that interruption cleanup is
    part of the intended transaction contract.

Correction:
    - Catch BaseException at the activation transaction boundary so rollback
      callbacks are attempted for interruption as well as ordinary failures.
    - Continue attempting remaining rollback callbacks if a callback itself
      raises BaseException.
    - Preserve the original activation failure as the raised cause when
      rollback is incomplete and keep the lifecycle in ROLLBACK_FAILED state.

Verification:
    Static source re-fetch and targeted pattern checks only. No tests, CI,
    syntax/import checks, interruption injection, or runtime/GUI verification
    were run.

Status:
    🟡 SOURCE CORRECTION APPLIED — STATIC CHECKS ONLY

======================================================================
GF-AUD-083
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Study Case registration at the Application study-execution boundary

Finding:
    Application.execute_study() registered a structured Study Case before
    checking active project identity, activation generation, validation,
    source revision, and topology provenance. A rejected stale or foreign
    StudyRequest could therefore mutate the Application-owned Study Case
    registry even though execution was refused.

Correction:
    - Move Study Case registration until after the request has passed the
      project-scope, revision, validation, topology, and snapshot checks.
    - Keep registration immediately before StudyService execution so a valid
      request remains available as a Study Case even if the solver later fails.
    - Preserve existing behavior for requests that do not satisfy the
      structured Study Case contract; they continue through StudyService's
      established request validation path.

Verification:
    Static source re-fetch and targeted ordering checks only. No tests, CI,
    syntax/import checks, or runtime study execution were run.

Status:
    🟡 SOURCE CORRECTION APPLIED — STATIC CHECKS ONLY

GF-AUD-089 — Synchronize context for SLD workspace utility actions
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Workspace lifecycle and action-enabled context synchronization

Files:
    main.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Equipment Browser, SLD editor maximize, and SLD region-toggle handlers
    activated SLD directly through WorkspaceController without publishing
    the matching EngineeringContext discipline. When invoked from another
    workspace, the visible workspace could be SLD while action gating still
    believed the active discipline was Control, Protection, or Study.

Correction:
    - Route all three utility actions through _activate_workspace(..., "sld").
    - Preserve WorkspaceController as the workspace transition owner.
    - Publish EngineeringContext only after successful workspace activation.

Verification:
    - Static source review and focused diff inspection only.

======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Files:

GF-AUD-092 — Clear transient interaction state on project transitions
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Project new/open/close lifecycle and SLD tool state

Files:
    ui/workspace/project_workspace_adapter.py
    main.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Project new/open/close transitions resolved save/discard/cancel consent
    but did not clear the active ToolManager tool or transient CreationContext.
    The document-transition guard used a tool switch that may persist the
    current transient equipment draft, which is not appropriate when replacing
    the project and risks carrying stale interaction state across project
    boundaries.

Correction:
    - Add a dedicated project-transition guard to the workspace adapter.
    - Invoke it only after transition consent is resolved and cancellation has
      been ruled out, before starting new/open/close project transactions.
    - Deactivate the current interaction tool to cancel transient creation
      state without persisting it, then restore the canonical select tool.
    - Keep the existing document-transition guard semantics unchanged.
    - Keep Application lifecycle and transactional presentation activation as
      the owners of project state and rollback.
    - If new/open/close fails after interaction reset, invoke a recovery callback
      and leave the still-active project in canonical Select mode.
    - Do not recreate a cancelled creation draft or restore a stale preview.

Verification:
    - Static source inspection and focused diff review only.
    - No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or GUI runtime were run.

GF-AUD-093 — Keep post-commit workspace publication outside rollback boundary
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Project workspace transition transaction and UI publication

Files:
    ui/workspace/project_workspace_adapter.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Workspace change handlers ran inside the same exception boundary as the
    Application transition. If a post-commit UI handler raised, the adapter
    restored the prior presentation configuration even though the Application
    and lifecycle transition had already committed, creating split ownership
    and potentially mismatched project/presentation state.

Correction:
    - Limit presentation-configuration rollback to failures from configuration
      setup or the Application transition itself.
    - Publish ProjectWorkspaceChanged only after that transaction succeeds and
      outside the rollback exception boundary.
    - Do not independently revert Application presentation state because a
      post-commit projection handler failed.

    - No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or GUI runtime were run.

GF-AUD-095 — Isolate workspace projection synchronization steps
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    ProjectWorkspaceChanged UI projection handler

Files:
    main.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    The composition-root workspace handler performed context, SLD surface,
    tab, canvas, and Properties-panel updates in one sequential function.
    An exception in an early projection step prevented later independent
    surfaces from attempting to synchronize after the Application transition
    had already committed.

Correction:
    - Execute each synchronization step through a small local failure boundary.
    - Log the failed step with operation and project ID.
    - Continue attempting independent surface updates after a step fails.
    - Keep the Application and lifecycle as authoritative; this does not claim
      that a failed individual projection has been repaired automatically.

GF-AUD-097 — Retain last-good SLD node on realization failure
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Incremental SLD canvas node reconciliation

Files:
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    The renderer removed a valid existing node before attempting to realize
    its replacement. If semantic realization or graphics-item creation failed,
    the previous visible node was lost and replaced by a degraded placeholder,
    even though a last-good item had been available.

Correction:
    - Retain the previous realized node while constructing its replacement.
    - If realization fails, keep the previous item visible, attach failure
      context, mark the node for retry, and do not stamp the failed snapshot
      signature onto the retained item.
    - Remove the old item only after replacement realization succeeds.

Verification:
    - Static source inspection and focused diff review only.
    - No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or GUI runtime were run.

    MERGED — PR #734; static checks only, runtime behavior remains unverified.

GF-AUD-098 — Atomic SLD scene/snap item lifecycle
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    SLD renderer graphics-item attachment and cleanup

Files:
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Renderer paths inserted a new graphics item into the scene before
    registering it with SnapSystem. A registration exception could leave
    an untracked scene item. Node replacement also removed the prior item
    before the new item had completed scene/snap attachment.

Correction:
    - Centralize scene attachment and optional snap registration in one
      rollback-aware helper.
    - If snap registration fails, remove the newly attached scene item and
      attempt to unregister any partially registered candidate before
      propagating the error.
    - Attach a replacement node before removing its prior realized item.
    - Ensure scene removal is attempted even if snap unregistration raises.

Verification:
    - Static source inspection and focused diff review only.
    - No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or GUI runtime were run.

    MERGED — PR #736; static checks only, runtime behavior remains unverified.

GF-AUD-100 — Best-effort cleanup of SLD renderer items
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Renderer reconciliation, item removal, and full scene cleanup

Files:
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    A snap unregistration, route callback unbinding, or scene removal exception
    could abort cleanup of the remaining tracked items. Full clear could then
    leave later graphics items in the scene and renderer registries populated.

Correction:
    - Attempt callback unbinding, snap unregistration, and scene removal
      independently for each tracked item.
    - Log cleanup failures and continue processing the remaining items.
    - Make clear() iterate tracked IDs through the shared best-effort cleanup
      path, then attempt to clear snap candidates and reset renderer metadata.

GF-AUD-101 — Bound committed connection realization failure paths
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Committed SLD connection reconciliation

Files:
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Automatic route calculation occurred outside the connection realization
    error boundary, so router failures could abort snapshot reconciliation
    without a connection diagnostic. After preserving last-good items, invalid
    or unresolved current endpoint identities could also leave a stale wire
    visible even though it no longer represented the requested connection.

Correction:
    - Include automatic route calculation in the guarded realization path.
    - Remove a previous connection when its current source/target node or
      endpoint identity is missing or cannot be resolved.
    - Preserve last-good connections for transient construction/route/attach
      failures when current endpoints remain resolvable.

GF-UNDO-001 — Deep immutability for protection configuration history
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ProtectionFunctionConfiguration snapshots retained by transactional undo

Files:
    core/protection/project_configuration.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    ProtectionFunctionConfiguration was frozen only at the dataclass and
    outer-mapping levels. Nested dictionaries and lists in settings remained
    mutable through caller-held references, so a configuration retained by an
    undo closure could change after the transaction recorded it. Metadata had
    the same shallow-container exposure.

Correction:
    - Recursively detach and freeze nested mapping/list/tuple values for
      settings and metadata when a configuration is constructed.
    - Recursively thaw those containers in to_dict() to preserve a plain,
      detached persistence representation.
    - Preserve existing input_channel_ids representation and validation.

Verification:
    - Static source review only; no tests/CI run per repository rules.
    - Runtime serialization/import behavior remains unverified.

Status:
    MERGED — PR #779; static source/diff inspection only, runtime behavior remains unverified.

======================================================================
GF-AUD-117 — Cancel parent creation state with transient interaction
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ToolManager activation, inline insertion cancellation, and transient
    equipment-placement preview lifecycle

Files:
    ui/core/tool_manager.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    ToolManager.cancel() returned early after cancelling an active temporary
    interaction. That bypassed cancellation of the parent CreationContext and
    parent placement tool state. Tool switching could therefore remain blocked
    by a stale CreationDraft, and Escape during inline insertion could leave
    the parent placement tool's local preview state available to a later
    creation session. Tool switches also did not explicitly guard an active
    temporary interaction when no CreationDraft was active.

Correction:
    - Treat an active temporary interaction as cancellable state for an
      explicitly authorized tool switch.
    - Cancel the temporary interaction and parent placement tool before
      discarding the CreationContext, then notify the controller once.
    - On Escape during inline insertion, clear the parent placement tool's
      local preview state before ending the CreationContext.
    - Retain the guard against implicit switching while a creation or
      temporary interaction is active.

Architecture:
    UI lifecycle coordination only. Core electrical truth, Application-owned
    DraftNetwork state, and the canonical UI → Controller → Command →
    Application → Core mutation path remain unchanged. No Qt imports were
    added and no Core/UI dependency was introduced.

Verification:
    - Static source inspection and focused diff review only.
    - No tests or CI were run, per repository rules.
    - Python syntax/import checks and GUI runtime remain unverified.

Status:
    MERGED — PR #775; keep issue #757 open pending full GUI verification.

GF-AUD-104 — Invalidate result overlays after project mutations
======================================================================

    2026-10-28

Files:
    ui/projection/study_projection.py
    Modification_Register.txt

    StudyProjection refreshed result freshness only when a study lifecycle
    event arrived. A subsequent committed network, topology, protection, or
    SLD presentation mutation could advance the source revision while leaving
    the previously displayed Study Case row and SLD voltage overlay stale.

      ProtectionChanged, and SLDPresentationChanged.

      and update each visible row's current/stale state.

      so the view does not depend exclusively on receiving future study events.

      unavailable Power Flow results no longer produce voltage annotations.
    - Skip canvas refresh when the selected result identity, source revision,
      status, freshness, study type, and value identity are unchanged, avoiding
      duplicate redraws when one mutation publishes related topology/network events.

Verification:
    - Static source inspection only; no tests, CI, Python syntax/import checks,
      or GUI runtime were run.

GF-AUD-109 — Reject non-finite Short-Circuit fault impedance
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Typed Short-Circuit configuration validation before sequence-network preparation

Files:
    core/analysis/short_circuit_configuration.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    ShortCircuitStudyConfiguration converted fault_impedance to complex but
    did not reject NaN or infinite real/imaginary components. Such values could
    cross the typed configuration boundary and reach numerical preparation.
    element_ids were also accepted with duplicate identifiers and untrimmed
    whitespace, making an element selection ambiguous.

Correction:
    - Require both real and imaginary fault-impedance components to be finite.
    - Normalize element IDs by trimming whitespace and reject empty or duplicate
      identifiers.
    - Keep the existing typed configuration and Application preparation path;
      no parallel validation service was introduced.

Verification:
    - Source block checked and updated through the repository API.
    - No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or numerical runtime were run.

Status:
    MERGED — PR #744; static source review only, runtime behavior remains unverified.

GF-AUD-113 — Separate numerical-study freshness from presentation/persistence revisions
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Application ProjectRevision semantics, StudyReadService result status,
    and StudyProjection Study Case status

Files:
    core/application/revision.py
    core/application/read_service.py
    ui/projection/study_projection.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    StudyReadService marked a published result current only when its entire
    ProjectRevision exactly matched the active revision. That included
    presentation_revision and persisted_revision, so a presentation-only SLD
    edit or a successful save could mark numerically identical study inputs
    stale. StudyProjection used the same overly strict equality for Study Case
    configuration status.

Correction:
    - Add ProjectRevision.matches_study_state() as the shared comparison for
      numerical-study input identity, comparing model_revision and
      topology_revision only.
    - Retain project ID and activation-generation checks at the Application
      read boundary, so a result from another project activation cannot become
      current merely because revision counters happen to match.
    - Use the shared comparison in StudyReadService and StudyProjection.
    - Preserve exact source revision snapshots for provenance and diagnostics;
      this changes only freshness interpretation, not revision tracking,
      persistence, solver inputs, or event semantics.

Verification:
    - Updated files were committed on fix/end-to-end-study-results-path and
      source re-fetch verification is required before merge.
    - No tests, CI, Python syntax/import checks, or GUI runtime were run.

Status:
    MERGED — PR #744; static source review only, runtime behavior remains unverified.

GF-AUD-114 — Include line, transformer, and cable service-state edits in topology revisions
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    RevisionService topology classification for supported model update commands

Files:
    core/application/revision_service.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    The supported model command registry includes model.update_line,
    model.update_transformer, and model.update_cable. Their update contracts
    permit changing in_service. RevisionService already advances model_revision
    for successful model.* commands, but these three commands were absent from
    _TOPOLOGY_UPDATE_COMMANDS. A successful service-state edit could therefore
    leave topology_revision unchanged even though the electrically connected
    network consumed by topology snapshots and study preparation had changed.

Correction:
    - Add model.update_line, model.update_transformer, and model.update_cable
      to the existing topology-update classification.
    - The existing _TOPOLOGY_STATE_FIELDS gate still means a parameter-only
      update does not advance topology_revision; the revision advances for
      these commands only when a topology-relevant field such as in_service is
      explicitly supplied.
    - Preserve the existing Application pre-commit transaction: failed
      commands do not record a successful revision transition, and registered
      undo restores the prior revision state.

Verification:
    - Source and register re-fetch verification required before merge.
    - No tests, CI, Python syntax/import checks, or GUI runtime were run.

Status:
    MERGED — PR #744; static source review only, runtime behavior remains unverified.

GF-AUD-115 — Classify capacitor and battery service-state changes for study topology freshness
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    RevisionService classification for capacitor and battery state mutations

Files:
    core/application/revision_service.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    The registered model command handlers expose model.update_capacitor,
    model.put_capacitor_in_service, model.take_capacitor_out_of_service,
    model.put_battery_in_service, and model.take_battery_out_of_service.
    Capacitor updates can change the Bus endpoint or in_service state, but
    model.update_capacitor was absent from _TOPOLOGY_UPDATE_COMMANDS. The
    dedicated capacitor and battery service-state commands were also absent
    from _TOPOLOGY_COMMANDS. Those successful state changes could advance the
    model revision without advancing the topology revision used by topology
    snapshots and study preparation.

Correction:
    - Add model.update_capacitor to _TOPOLOGY_UPDATE_COMMANDS; the existing
      topology-relevant payload-field gate is preserved, so parameter-only
      changes do not independently advance topology_revision.
    - Add capacitor and battery put/take-service commands to
      _TOPOLOGY_COMMANDS so their explicit service-state transitions advance
      topology_revision through the existing successful-command transaction.
    - Leave CT/CVT/PT service changes excluded as measurement equipment, and
      do not introduce a parallel revision or invalidation service.

Verification:
    - RevisionService source updated on fix/end-to-end-study-results-path.
    - Re-fetch source/register and PR metadata before treating this batch as
      verified. No tests, CI, Python syntax/import checks, or GUI runtime were
      run.

    MERGED — PR #744; static source review only, runtime behavior remains unverified.

GF-AUD-116
======================================================================

Scope:
    Study result selection and live SLD numerical overlays

Status:
    🟢 CORRECTION APPLIED — STATIC SOURCE REVIEW

Finding:
    StudyProjection previously forwarded any selected StudyResult to the
    SLD overlay callback, including failed, cancelled, or stale results.
    This could display numerical values that were not valid for the current
    authoritative project/model/topology revision.

Correction:
    Result details remain available for inspection, but the live SLD overlay
    receives a result only when its status is completed and its Application
    read model reports current=True. Otherwise the overlay callback receives
    None, clearing the numerical projection.

Boundary:
    This is a read-only UI projection correction. It does not change Core
    electrical truth, solver outputs, result persistence, or study freshness
    authority. The Application read service remains responsible for computing
    current/stale status from project/activation provenance and revisions.

Verification:
    Static source inspection only. No tests or CI were run. GUI runtime
    behavior and end-to-end overlay clearing remain to be verified separately.

GF-AUD-117
======================================================================

Scope:
    Solver convergence status and live SLD numerical overlays

Status:
    🟢 CORRECTION APPLIED — STATIC SOURCE REVIEW

Finding:
    A solver may finish its calculation without converging. StudyService
    records handler return as a completed execution, which is distinct from
    numerical convergence. The Power Flow result contract exposes a boolean
    success field, but the SLD overlay guard previously did not inspect it.

Correction:
    StudyProjection now suppresses the live numerical overlay when the result
    value explicitly reports success=False. The result details remain
    inspectable, and studies whose result contracts do not expose a success
    field continue to use the completed/current freshness gate.

Boundary:
    This correction distinguishes execution lifecycle from solver convergence
    without redefining StudyService terminal status or altering solver output.

Verification:
    Static source inspection only. No tests or CI were run. Runtime behavior
    for non-converged Power Flow and other study result contracts remains to
    be verified.

GF-AUD-120 — Distinguish numerical failure from successful study execution
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Application study handlers → StudyService terminal lifecycle → result projection

Files:
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    StudyService correctly treats a handler's normal return as an execution
    completion, but the Power Flow and Short-Circuit handlers returned solver
    result objects even when those objects explicitly reported success=False.
    This made a numerically unsuccessful calculation appear as a completed
    study and could leave downstream UI code to interpret the result contract
    inconsistently.

Correction:
    - Power Flow handler now checks the solver-level PowerFlowResult.success
      before engineering-result conversion and raises a descriptive error when
      the solver reports non-convergence.
    - Short-Circuit handler now checks ShortCircuitResult.success and raises
      with solver diagnostics when the fault calculation reports failure.
    - Cancellation checks remain before these terminal validations so a
      cancellation request is not misreported as a numerical failure.
    - The existing StudyService exception path publishes StudyFailed and
      retains the failed result/message for the read-only result projection.
    - No new study lifecycle or parallel result authority is introduced.

Verification:
    - Updated source and register on fix/end-to-end-study-results-path.
    - Static source comparison only; no tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or
      runtime numerical studies were run, per repository rules.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — PR #744 updated; runtime verification remains pending.

GF-AUD-121 — Resolve cancellation-versus-exception terminal state atomically
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Application StudyService terminal lifecycle and event consistency

Files:
    core/application/study.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    If a study handler raised while a cancellation request was already pending,
    the exception path called token.finish() but discarded its cancellation
    result. The service then stored a failed result and published StudyFailed,
    contradicting the cancellation token's documented first-terminal-outcome
    contract.

Correction:
    The exception path now resolves the terminal state using the return value
    of token.finish(). If cancellation won before finish(), it stores a
    cancelled StudyResult, publishes StudyCancelled, and returns that result.
    Otherwise it stores/publishes failure and re-raises the original exception.

Verification:
    Source-level control-flow inspection only. No tests or CI were run, per
    repository rules. Concurrent runtime behavior remains unverified.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — PR #744; runtime verification pending.

GF-AUD-124 — Reject study snapshots captured across revision changes
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Application detached ProjectSnapshot capture

Files:
    core/application/application.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    ProjectSnapshot capture serialized the active Network, copied dynamic-model
    associations, and only then read the current ProjectRevision. It did not
    verify that the project activation or revision remained stable across this
    capture sequence.

Correction:
    Capture now records project identity, activation generation, and revision
    before copying the detached Network/dynamic-model state, then rechecks the
    revision and active project scope. If the revision or activation changes
    during capture, execution is rejected rather than pairing a potentially
    mixed snapshot with the later revision.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only. No tests or CI were run, per repository rules.
    This guard narrows capture races but does not claim an atomic lock over every
    internal Network mutation; concurrent runtime behavior remains unverified.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — PR #744; runtime verification pending.

GF-AUD-125 — Validate detached network snapshot against study provenance
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

    Application study execution snapshot pairing

Files:
    core/application/application.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    execute_study() validated the live TopologySnapshot before separately
    capturing the detached ProjectSnapshot. A model mutation between these
    boundaries could leave the execution context pairing topology and project
    inputs that no longer describe the same requested revision.

Correction:
    After detached capture, execution now rechecks the active ProjectRevision,

GF-AUD-126 — Preserve Study Case configuration revision and reject stale reruns
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Application Study Case execution and re-registration provenance

Files:
    core/application/application.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    execute_study_case() created a fresh StudyRequest using the current project
    revision without checking the StudyCaseDefinition.source_revision. This
    allowed an old configuration to be treated as current after electrical
    model/topology changes. The post-execution registration path could also
    replace the original configuration revision for an unchanged case.

Correction:
    - Before execution, compare the case's recorded revision with the active
      revision using ProjectRevision.matches_study_state(), which ignores only
      presentation and persistence bookkeeping.
    - Reject execution when model or topology revisions differ, with an
      explicit instruction to reconfigure or recreate the Study Case.
    - When an unchanged case is executed against the same numerical study
      state, preserve its original source_revision and display name rather
      than silently relabeling the configuration with the execution revision.
    - Keep the execution request's current full revision so existing
      Application snapshot and execution provenance checks remain intact.

Verification:
    - Static source review and GitHub write response only. No tests, CI,
      syntax/import checks, or runtime studies were run, per repository rules.

GF-AUD-128 — Cooperatively cancel long-running Transient Stability integration
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    StudyCancellationToken → Transient Stability analysis → solver time loop

Files:
    core/solver/dynamics/transient_stability.py
    core/analysis/transient_stability.py
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    The Application handler checked cancellation only before and after the full
    Transient Stability analysis. StudyService could mark a run cancelled after
    completion, but a long transient integration did not poll the token during
    its time loop and therefore could not stop promptly.

Correction:
    - Add an optional cooperative should_cancel callback to the transient solver
      run API and poll it before recording the initial sample and on each step.
    - Forward the callback through TransientStabilityAnalysis.run().
    - Bind the callback to the Application StudyCancellationToken in the
      transient handler; the handler returns no result when cancellation wins.
    - Preserve the existing StudyService terminal-state arbitration so partial
      samples are not published as a completed study result.

      through the Application handler to the solver loop.

    cancellation latency during an individual DAE step remains bounded by that

GF-AUD-129 — Preserve configured priority and valid keyword-only event calls
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Transient Stability event configuration → analysis scheduling helpers → EventManager

Files:
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    core/analysis/transient_events.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    The breaker-open and breaker-close event handlers called scheduling helpers
    with positional arguments even though affected_equipment_ids and event_id
    are keyword-only parameters. Those event types would raise TypeError while
    configuring the study. In addition, event priority accepted by EventManager
    was not carried from typed event metadata into the scheduling helpers, so
    simultaneous events could silently fall back to event-ID ordering.

Correction:
    - Pass breaker event identifiers and affected equipment IDs by keyword.
    - Add backward-compatible optional priority=0 parameters to all transient
      event scheduling helpers and forward priority to EventManager.add().
    - Read the optional event priority from each event mapping in the
      Application configuration path.
    - EventManager's deterministic ordering now honors (time, priority, ID)
      for configured transient events.

Verification:
    - Re-fetched the modified source files and confirmed helper signatures and
      Application calls carry event priority; no tests, CI, syntax/import checks,
      or runtime studies were run.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED ON fix/end-to-end-study-results-path — PR #744 updated;
    actual event execution and same-time fault/breaker interactions remain
    runtime-unverified.

GF-AUD-130 — Fail fast when breaker events cannot alter network elements
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Transient breaker event configuration → detached network state → Y-bus view

Files:
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    TransientEventState.set_breaker() records breaker state, but the passive
    network solver builds its Y-bus from equipment_states. A breaker event with
    no affected_equipment_ids therefore changed only the bookkeeping map and
    could leave the electrical network unchanged while the simulation appeared
    to execute the event.

Correction:
    - Require breaker-open and breaker-close events to identify affected
      network equipment.
    - Validate affected IDs against the detached snapshot's branch, transformer,
      and shunt identifiers before scheduling the event.
    - Raise a descriptive configuration error for missing or unknown targets,
      rather than silently simulating a breaker operation with no network effect.

Verification:
    - Re-fetched the Application source after the change and confirmed the
      validation path is committed.
    - No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or runtime studies were run.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED ON fix/end-to-end-study-results-path — PR #744 updated;
    physical correspondence between breaker IDs and affected network elements
    remains a project-model/configuration contract requiring runtime verification.
