======================================================================
GF-AUD-131 — Validate transient fault impedance and bus identity before scheduling
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Transient fault event configuration → immutable fault intent → network solver

Files:
    core/analysis/transient_fault.py
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    TransientFault normalized impedance to complex but accepted NaN/Infinity
    components. Those values could propagate into the algebraic network matrix
    and fail only during numerical solution. Fault events also accepted bus IDs
    absent from the detached network snapshot, delaying a configuration error
    until the solver evaluated the event.

Correction:
    - Reject non-finite real or imaginary fault-impedance components when the
      immutable TransientFault intent is created.
    - Validate each fault event bus against the detached snapshot's canonical
      bus IDs before scheduling it.
    - Preserve the existing three-phase-only model contract and fault-clear
      behavior; clearing a fault changes only active_fault and does not restore
      or overwrite breaker/equipment state.

Verification:
    - Source edits committed and re-fetched for confirmation.
    - No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or runtime studies were run.
    - Fault impedance is interpreted in the same per-unit network basis as the
      prepared network; conversion from entered ohms/base values is not added by
      this correction and remains a separate explicit configuration contract.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED ON fix/end-to-end-study-results-path — PR #744 updated;
    numerical fault behavior remains runtime-unverified.

GF-AUD-132 — Reject transient fault-clear events without an active fault
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Transient event dispatch → detached event-state mutation

Files:
    core/analysis/transient_event_state.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    clear_fault() silently accepted a fault-clear event when no fault was active.
    The event manager then recorded the event as successfully executed even though
    no electrical state changed, masking an invalid or incorrectly ordered event
    schedule.

Correction:
    - Raise a descriptive ValueError if fault clearing is requested while no
      fault is active.
    - Keep clearing isolated to active_fault; breaker/equipment state remains
      untouched when a valid fault-clear event executes.
    - Event execution propagates action errors as EventError, so the transient
      study fails rather than publishing a misleading successful event sequence.

Verification:
    - Source edit committed and re-fetched for confirmation.
    - No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or runtime studies were run.
    - Same-time apply/clear ordering remains governed by EventManager's explicit
      event priority and event-ID ordering; users must configure that ordering.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED ON fix/end-to-end-study-results-path — PR #744 updated;
    runtime event-ordering behavior remains unverified.

GF-AUD-133 — Preflight transient fault event ordering
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Transient event configuration → deterministic EventManager order → fault state

Files:
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Fault apply/clear conflicts were detected only when the simulation reached
    the event time. A second apply before clearing, or a clear without a preceding
    apply in the configured schedule, could waste solver work before failing.

Correction:
    - Collect fault operations during event configuration and preflight them
      before transient integration starts.
    - Evaluate operations in the EventManager's deterministic order: event time,
      ascending priority, then event ID.
    - Reject overlapping fault applications and clears without an active fault
      using descriptive configuration errors.
    - Preserve EventManager execution-time checks as the runtime authority.

Verification:
    - Source edit committed and re-fetched for confirmation.
    - No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or runtime studies were run.
    - Equal-time apply/clear ordering remains explicitly determined by configured
      priority and then event ID; engineers should set priorities when intent
      must not depend on event-ID lexical order.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED ON fix/end-to-end-study-results-path — PR #744 updated;
    runtime event scheduling remains unverified.

======================================================================
GF-AUD-134 — Enforce transient solver time-boundary and completion contract
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    DAE integration → transient trajectory → StudyService completion status

Files:
    core/solver/dynamics/transient_stability.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    The transient solver could accept non-finite time configuration or an initial
    time outside the configured interval. It also trusted the integrator's
    returned timestamp and returned a result after the integration loop without
    asserting that a non-cancelled trajectory reached end_time. StudyService
    treats a handler return as completed, so a partial trajectory could be
    classified as a successful study if an unexpected solver exit occurred.

Correction:
    - Require finite start_time, end_time, dt, and initialization time.
    - Require initialization time to lie within [start_time, end_time].
    - Reject non-finite integrator timestamps and steps that overshoot the
      requested event/end-time boundary beyond the event tolerance.
    - Before returning, require every non-cancelled run to have reached the
      configured end_time within the event tolerance; otherwise raise
      TransientStabilityError so StudyService publishes failure rather than
      completion.
    - Preserve partial trajectories for cooperative cancellation; the existing
      Application handler converts cancelled runs to the cancellation outcome.

Verification:
    - Source edit committed and re-fetched for confirmation.
    - No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or runtime studies were run.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED ON fix/end-to-end-study-results-path — PR #744 updated;
    numerical integration and cancellation behavior remain runtime-unverified.

======================================================================
GF-AUD-135 — Validate transient event times against study interval
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-28

Scope:
    Transient event configuration → event dispatch → trajectory result

Files:
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    EventManager.process(start_time) dispatches every enabled event scheduled
    at or before the initial time. Without configuration-bound validation, an
    event before start_time could execute at initialization while retaining its
    earlier scheduled timestamp, producing event history inconsistent with the
    configured study interval and trajectory samples. Non-finite event times
    were also not rejected explicitly at the Application boundary.

Correction:
    - Reject NaN and infinite transient event times.
    - Require every configured transient event time to lie inclusively within
      the typed study configuration's [start_time, end_time] interval before
      registering it with EventManager.
    - Keep events exactly at either boundary valid; start-time events are
      applied before the initial recorded sample, and end-time events are
      processed at the final integration boundary.

Verification:
    - Source edit committed and re-fetched for confirmation.
    - No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or runtime studies were run.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED ON fix/end-to-end-study-results-path — PR #744 updated;
    event execution ordering and numerical runtime remain runtime-unverified.

    the current Application-owned DraftNetwork as JSON-serializable recovery

Authority boundary:

    replace authoritative Core state or treat SLD route geometry as topology.

GF-COMMIT-001 | Non-blocking DraftNetwork commit orchestration
Finding: PropertiesPanel invoked the synchronous Application commit path, allowing draft validation and Core command execution to block the UI thread.
Correction: Added an Application-owned single-worker commit path, captured project/activation/revision/draft provenance, and rejected stale snapshots before Core mutation. The worker executes through CommandManager without publishing UI-facing events; the Future completion is delivered through the PropertiesPanel Qt signal, then Application semantic events are finalized on the UI thread. Execute/undo/redo reject while the asynchronous commit is in flight, avoiding UI-thread waits on the worker lock. Core validation, transaction rollback, and command history remain authoritative. Follow-up: the creation controller exposes commit_network_async() as the UI entry point and routes completion/abort back through the Application finalization boundary.
Verification: Static source review only; no tests/CI run per repository rules. Runtime thread behavior, signal delivery, and large-network performance remain unverified.

GF-PERF-001 — Study result host/device presentation boundary audit
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Application StudyService, StudyReadService, StudyResultReadModel

Finding:
    StudyService stores the study handler's result value without an explicit
    host/device residency contract. StudyReadService._to_read_model() forwards
    result.value directly into StudyResultReadModel.value. The read-model
    _freeze() helper freezes mappings and Python containers but leaves typed
    result objects and numerical array objects unchanged. Therefore the
    inspected boundary does not itself prove that device-resident arrays are
    transferred, summarized, or excluded before presentation consumption.

Correction decision:
    No broad implicit copy or device-to-host transfer was added. Such a change
    could synchronously copy large solver matrices on a UI read and defeat the
    performance objective; rejecting every array-like result would also risk
    breaking existing typed study result contracts. The correct implementation
    requires explicit per-study presentation payloads/summaries and a defined
    transfer policy at the solver-result-to-Application boundary, retaining
    canonical numerical results as the authoritative study result.

Required follow-up:
    - Inventory concrete power-flow, short-circuit, contingency, and transient
      stability result types and their array/device ownership.
    - Define an explicit opt-in host presentation adapter for each result type.
    - Keep large matrices and accelerator-resident buffers out of routine UI
      snapshots; expose compact summaries and bounded slices where appropriate.
    - Ensure transfer occurs only at an explicit consumer/presentation boundary,
      never as an implicit side effect of StudyReadService.result()/snapshot().
    - Preserve result provenance, immutable read-side semantics, and successful
      StudyCompleted publication lifecycle.

Verification:
    Source-level trace of StudyService.execute(), StudyResult, StudyReadService,
    StudyResultReadModel, and the solver architecture contract.
    No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, runtime execution, or accelerator
    profiling performed.

Status:
    OPEN — verified boundary gap; implementation requires result-type inventory
    before a safe non-breaking correction can be made.

GF-SLD-INS-001 — Inline insertion scope and instrument-transformer semantics
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ElectricalInsertionService, insertion_contract, Breaker, CT, PT, CVT

Finding:
    Source inspection confirms that ElectricalInsertionService performs
    transactional replacement of a SimpleWire with two replacement connection
    records, uses canonical terminal-connection commands, and validates the
    resulting connectivity and Network before returning success.

    Breaker.__init__ defaults closed=True and normalizes the switching state
    to bool. The issue's claimed null-state defect is not established by the
    inspected constructor or insertion creation allowlist.

    The insertion contract currently supports breaker, disconnector,
    transformer, and current_transformer. It does not support PT or CVT.
    This omission must not be corrected by treating PT/CVT as series elements:
    the Core PT and CVT models each expose separate primary and secondary
    terminal pairs. Their primary measurement connection is not equivalent to
    a two-terminal series insertion; secondary measurement channels require
    a distinct instrumentation/measurement integration path.

Correction decision:
    No speculative code change was made. Adding PT/CVT to the existing
    two-terminal series insertion contract would incorrectly discard their
    four-terminal semantics and could create electrically invalid topology.
    PT/CVT insertion/tapping requires an explicit domain contract defining
    primary-side connection semantics, secondary wiring, and measurement
    channel activation before implementation.

Required follow-up:
    - Keep series insertion restricted to equipment whose Core terminal
      contract is explicitly two-terminal and series-capable.
    - Define a separate PT/CVT placement and primary-tap workflow using their
      authoritative four-terminal Core models.
    - Verify breaker state and CT measurement-channel behavior end-to-end
      only after the appropriate runtime path is available.
    - Preserve the canonical mutation path and transactional rollback.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection of electrical_insertion_service.py,
    insertion_contract.py, electrical_insertion_tool.py, breaker.py, pt.py,
    cvt.py, and measurement_commands.py.
    No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or runtime interaction were performed.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #774 REMAINS OPEN pending a separate, electrically
    correct PT/CVT instrumentation connection contract and runtime verification.

GF-SLD-WIRE-001 — Simple Wire route geometry versus electrical topology
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    DraftNetwork, DraftConnection, DraftConnectivityResolver,
    CommitNetworkHandler, DraftSLDProjection, SLDRouteEditController,
    SLDConnectionItem, WireTool

Findings:
    1. DraftConnection stores source and target as canonical
       DraftEndpointReference values. Its route is presentation metadata.
       DraftSLDProjection reads route points to construct the canvas snapshot,
       while CommitNetworkHandler derives Core connections from the explicit
       DraftConnection source/target endpoints. Route bend vertices are not
       converted into electrical terminals or topology nodes in this path.
       Therefore the reported automatic conversion of stale route vertices
       into solver-visible ghost terminals is not established by inspected
       source.

    2. SLDRouteEditController treats proposed bends as interaction-local state,
       persists them through SLDController.set_connection_route(), and clears
       its temporary point list in a finally block. SLDConnectionItem emits a
       route-edit request without overwriting its last accepted route points.
       These are positive lifecycle boundaries, but they do not alone prove
       that every route replacement normalizes malformed/duplicate points.

    3. WireTool requests SnapIntent.WIRE_START/WIRE_TARGET with grid snapping
       disabled, requires SnapType.OBJECT, resolves a canonical endpoint
       identity, and submits CreateDraftConnectionCommand or
       CreateSimpleWireConnectionCommand. DraftNetwork preflight validates
       canonical endpoint compatibility and rejects duplicate endpoint pairs.
       CommitNetworkHandler again validates trusted terminal contracts and
       translates DraftConnection endpoints to Core references. Thus the
       described “visual overlap alone creates electrical connectivity” is
       not supported by the inspected command path.

Correction decision:
    No speculative topology changes were made. The source establishes a
    deliberate separation between route geometry and electrical connectivity.
    Treating intermediate layout vertices as electrical terminals would
    violate the frozen Core/Application/UI boundary.

Remaining gap:
    Route-point hygiene is not fully established from the inspected path:
    DraftConnection currently wraps the route mapping but does not visibly
    canonicalize each point, reject non-finite coordinates, or remove
    consecutive duplicate/zero-length bends at construction. The complete
    SLDController.set_connection_route persistence path and its downstream
    serializer were not yet traced, so this is an audit finding, not a proven
    runtime defect.

Required follow-up:
    - Trace set_connection_route through command, Application handler,
      persistent SLD document and re-projection before changing route storage.
    - If confirmed absent, validate finite coordinate pairs and canonicalize
      consecutive duplicate/zero-length bend points at the presentation-route
      boundary only.
    - Preserve endpoint identity as the sole electrical topology authority.
    - Keep Core ConnectivityResolver and numerical solvers independent of SLD
      route vertices.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection of core/application/draft/network.py,
    core/application/draft/handlers.py, ui/canvas/draft_sld_projection.py,
    ui/sld/sld_route_edit_controller.py, ui/sld/items/sld_connection_item.py,
    and ui/tools/wire_tool.py.
    No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or runtime interactions were performed.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #773 REMAINS OPEN pending route-persistence trace and
    confirmation of complete route editing behavior and runtime interaction.


Correction follow-up (2026-10-29):
    Implemented in PR #787: SLDRoute now removes consecutive duplicate
    intermediate bend coordinates after validating each point as a finite
    numeric pair. This removes zero-length presentation segments without
    rewriting non-consecutive points or changing endpoint/topology semantics.
    Stale non-consecutive bends are not automatically discarded because the
    route model cannot infer whether they are intentional loops; a route edit
    replaces the complete accepted point list. Core connectivity remains
    sourced exclusively from canonical connection endpoints.

    Verification: source-level change review only. No tests, CI, syntax/import
    checks, or runtime interaction were performed. Issue #773 remains open
    until full interaction behavior is verified.

GF-CLIP-001 — Issue #772: Cut, Copy, and Placement Pipeline
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #772; ui/core/selection_manager.py;
    ui/tools/model_placement_tool.py; ui/tools/tool_shortcuts.py;
    core/application/commands/draft_commands.py;
    core/application/draft/handlers.py; ui/canvas/preview_layer.py;
    ui/plugins/canvas_plugin.py.

Findings:
    1. The active SelectionManager owns transient UI selection IDs and
       projects selection to graphics. It does not own a clipboard payload
       or define copy/cut/paste identity semantics.

    2. The inspected active tool shortcut contract defines SAVE, UNDO,
       REDO, SELECT, CREATE, CONNECT, and CANCEL; it contains no canonical
       COPY, CUT, or PASTE action. Repository tree inspection also found no
       clipboard-named source module or clipboard command in the active
       Application command set. This is evidence that the reported path
       cannot currently be traced through a canonical clipboard service,
       not proof that no ad hoc handler exists anywhere in the repository.

    3. ModelPlacementTool creates a fresh draft identity for ordinary
       placement and submits AddDraftEquipmentCommand. The Application
       DraftCommandHandlers deserialize the draft equipment, record an
       undo snapshot, and add it to DraftNetwork. The current code path
       therefore provides a canonical draft mutation boundary, but does not
       establish how a copied Core element_id would be stripped or
       regenerated because a copy/paste serialization path was not found.

    4. ModelPlacementTool.on_cancel() clears its own transient placement
       state and preview graphics. PreviewLayer documents and implements
       ownership of transient preview graphics. This is correct for a
       preview-only interaction. It does not remove an already persisted
       DraftNetwork equipment object; such cleanup must use the canonical
       RemoveDraftEquipmentCommand and must be coordinated with the
       placement transaction rather than implemented as scene-only cleanup.

    5. CanvasPlugin.synchronize_sld() reconstructs the visible composite
       snapshot from the active SLD document and DraftSLDProjection. This
       supports reconciliation from authoritative document/draft state;
       manually deleting or hiding phantom scene items would risk masking
       stale authoritative draft data instead of correcting it.

Correction decision:
    Do not add a speculative clipboard implementation or infer a copy schema
    from Core objects without tracing the intended user interaction and
    supported object types. The current source inspection does not establish
    the issue's claimed direct-Core overwrite; it establishes that the
    canonical clipboard-to-draft lifecycle is not identifiable in the
    inspected active contracts.

Required correction contract:
    - Define one Application-owned clipboard snapshot schema that carries
      source type, copied values, terminal/connection references and
      presentation metadata explicitly, while excluding source Core IDs,
      committed IDs, Qt objects and graphics-item references.
    - Allocate new draft identities for every pasted equipment and connection;
      remap all internal endpoints through a paste-local old-to-new identity
      map; never mutate or overwrite existing Core identity during paste.
    - Submit all pasted objects through canonical Application draft commands
      with transactional rollback. Do not directly mutate Core Network or
      insert permanent graphics into QGraphicsScene.
    - Treat paste placement as a cancellable draft transaction. Escape,
      tool switch, project close and failed paste must remove only objects
      created by that in-progress paste, via canonical inverse commands or a
      single atomic Application operation; they must not delete pre-existing
      draft objects.
    - Re-project the composite canvas from SLD document + DraftNetwork after
      successful mutation or cancellation so no scene-only phantom can remain.
    - Preserve endpoint contracts and validate duplicate endpoint pairs before
      the transaction commits. Do not copy stale terminal IDs or derive
      electrical connectivity from layout geometry.
    - Add explicit COPY/CUT/PASTE intent handling only after the command,
      undo/redo, and selection contracts are traced end-to-end.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only of the files listed above and active-tree
    paths. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or runtime interaction were
    performed, in accordance with the repository audit rules.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #772 REMAINS OPEN. The canonical clipboard
    serialization and atomic paste/cancel path require a dedicated
    implementation after the command/selection lifecycle is fully specified.

GF-SLD-ROT-001 — Issue #771: Rotation and Topological Alignment
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #771; ui/equipment/symbol/symbol_base.py;
    ui/items/equipment_item.py; ui/canvas/sld_graphics_item_factory.py;
    ui/sld/sld_model.py; core/model/terminal.py;
    core/model/transformer.py; core/model/breaker.py.

Findings:
    1. SymbolBase stores rotation as finite degrees in renderer-neutral
       presentation state. SLDGraphicsItemFactory passes the symbol instance
       to EquipmentItem, which applies mirror transform, scale and rotation
       to the graphics item. This is presentation transformation, not a Core
       electrical mutation.

    2. EquipmentItem.snap_points() resolves each symbol terminal anchor from
       SymbolDefinition and maps the anchor to scene coordinates through the
       same graphics-item transform. This means snapping geometry follows the
       displayed symbol transform in the inspected path; the claim that
       terminal anchors necessarily remain unrotated is not supported by
       this implementation.

    3. EquipmentItem.boundingRect() describes the local logical symbol box
       plus a margin for stroke extents. The item is transformed by Qt's
       graphics-item transform. A local bounding rectangle does not need to
       be rewritten merely because the item rotates: Qt applies the item
       transform for scene geometry and hit testing. The reported selection
       mismatch may still be a runtime defect in custom hit testing or item
       shape handling, but it is not established by boundingRect() alone.

    4. Core Terminal is the authoritative owner of electrical endpoint and
       semantic role. The inspected Terminal contract does not contain a
       presentation rotation, polar vector, or graphics orientation property.
       The inspected Breaker and Transformer models likewise separate their
       electrical properties/topology from SLD symbol rotation. This is
       consistent with the frozen architecture: visual rotation must not
       alter electrical connectivity or phase/terminal identity.

    5. SLD canvas projections consume SLD node presentation state and
       renderer-neutral snapshots; graphics items do not own authoritative
       network topology. Therefore the issue's proposed solver interpretation
       of rotated visual wire paths would violate the architectural boundary.
       Connectivity must remain sourced from canonical endpoint references,
       never from transformed scene coordinates or drawn wire intersections.

Correction decision:
    No Core terminal-orientation field or topology mutation was added.
    Such a change would introduce electrical meaning without a defined
    engineering contract. The inspected source already transforms symbol
    anchors and relies on Qt item transforms for geometry.

Required follow-up:
    - Reproduce the reported selection failure with rotated equipment,
      including 0°, 45°, 90°, and mirrored/scaled cases, before changing
      hit-test behavior.
    - Trace the complete connection-route recomputation path when a symbol
      is rotated and verify that endpoint anchors are refreshed while
      connection endpoint identities remain unchanged.
    - If route endpoints are stale, correct the presentation projection or
      route recomputation boundary; do not mutate Core Terminal identity or
      infer connectivity from route geometry.
    - Keep selection geometry in the Qt presentation layer and ensure any
      custom shape/hit-test override is consistent with the symbol's local
      geometry and graphics transform.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only of the files listed above. No tests, CI,
    syntax/import checks, or runtime interaction were performed.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #771 REMAINS OPEN pending runtime reproduction of
    the reported selection and route-refresh symptoms. The claimed Core
    terminal-angle inversion is not supported by the inspected architecture
    or source path.

GF-CANVAS-NAV-001 — Issue #770: Pan Precision and Viewport Culling
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #770; ui/canvas/navigation_controller.py;
    ui/canvas/graphics_view.py; ui/canvas/coordinate_system.py;
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py; ui/canvas/grid_scene.py.

Confirmed source finding:
    NavigationController.update_pan() converted each pointer delta to an
    integer independently before updating the scrollbars, then advanced
    _pan_start to the latest pointer position. Any fractional part of each
    movement was discarded rather than accumulated. With fractional/high-
    resolution pointer deltas, repeated subpixel motion can therefore produce
    cumulative pan under-travel. This is a concrete source-level precision
    defect consistent with the reported pan drift.

Correction:
    PR #790 carries fractional x/y displacement between pan updates. It
    applies the integral part to the integer scrollbars and retains the
    fractional remainder for the next pointer event. Remainders reset at
    pan start, invalid pan state recovery, and pan end. This remains entirely
    within the UI navigation boundary and does not change Core coordinates,
    electrical state, snapping policy, or the canonical scene transform.

Other findings:
    - GraphicsView delegates cursor conversion to QGraphicsView.mapToScene()
      and delegates navigation to NavigationController.
    - CoordinateSystem documents an integer QPoint conversion path when the
      input exposes toPoint(); this can lose subpixel pointer precision for
      those callers. It remains a follow-up candidate because replacing the
      mapping overload requires verifying supported Qt/PySide signatures and
      all event adapters.
    - The inspected SLDCanvasRenderSystem does not implement a custom
      viewport-boundary culling engine for ordinary equipment symbols.
      Qt scene/view transforms and item bounds are used for graphics; the
      render system's explicit zoom-dependent visibility rule found in this
      inspection is for annotation density. The reported equipment flicker
      is not established from this source alone.

Required follow-up:
    - Reproduce the reported micro-flicker with large/multi-segment symbols at
      deep zoom and viewport edges.
    - If reproducible, trace any custom item visibility, clipping, scene
      rectangle, or painter-bound logic before changing culling behavior.
    - Trace QPointF-to-QPoint conversions along the active event path and
      retain fractional coordinates where the Qt API permits.
    - Do not introduce Core float64 coordinate or electrical-terminal changes:
      pan/zoom are presentation transforms.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection and focused correction only. No tests, CI,
    syntax/import checks, or runtime interaction were performed.

Status:
    PAN PRECISION CORRECTED IN PR #790 — ISSUE #770 REMAINS OPEN pending
    runtime verification and separate investigation of reported equipment
    flicker and active-path subpixel coordinate conversion.

GF-WORKSPACE-TOOL-001 — Issue #769: Tool Lifecycle Across SLD Activation
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #769; ui/workspace/project_workspace_adapter.py;
    ui/workspace/project_workspace.py; ui/core/tool_manager.py;
    ui/canvas/interaction_manager.py; ui/canvas/canvas_composition.py;
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py.

Findings:
    1. ProjectWorkspaceApplicationAdapter.activate_document() captures a
       lifecycle snapshot, calls the optional document-transition guard,
       activates the lifecycle document, activates the Application
       presentation, calls the presentation-activation bridge, and publishes
       the update. On failure it restores lifecycle/Application/presentation
       state. This is a coordinated activation path.

    2. The document-transition guard is optional in the adapter and is not
       configured by the inspected adapter constructor. Repository search
       did not locate a call configuring it. Therefore the intended pre-switch
       interaction cleanup is not demonstrated by the inspected wiring.

    3. ToolManager owns the active tool and temporary interaction tool and
       exposes cancel(), deactivate(), and dispose() lifecycle operations.
       InteractionManager dispatches events to that ToolManager and owns its
       input adapter. The inspected InteractionManager does not subscribe to
       active-document identity changes or check that its input belongs to
       the currently active document before dispatch.

    4. Canvas composition builds an InteractionManager around the supplied
       ToolManager and view. SLDCanvasSurface.present_document() and
       present_composite_document() replace the visible snapshot through the
       render system. These paths establish a single-canvas projection update,
       but do not alone prove that all transient tool state and callbacks are
       invalidated before a document transition.

    5. The issue's exact symptom—mutations to a hidden document at matching
       coordinates—was not reproduced and is not established by source review
       alone. The audit did not find evidence sufficient to claim that the
       ToolManager directly mutates the inactive document; canonical
       Application presentation and draft ownership must be traced at runtime.

Correction decision:
    Do not insert a speculative global cancel/deactivate call without
    identifying the composition owner and its recovery semantics. The adapter
    already has a guard seam for document activation; the gap is proving and
    enforcing the lifecycle wiring and document ownership invariant.

Required correction contract:
    - Wire a document-transition guard at the composition root for every
      SLD document activation. It must cancel temporary interaction state and
      clear transient previews before changing active document identity.
    - Make the guard idempotent and ensure it does not discard already
      persisted DraftNetwork objects or committed SLD document state.
    - Bind each input dispatch to the currently active document/view context;
      reject or ignore stale input from a background canvas rather than
      resolving against whichever presentation happens to be globally active.
    - On activation failure, restore prior document, presentation bridge and
      interaction readiness without leaving the prior tool active against a
      partially switched canvas.
    - Keep per-document SLD geometry and draft state canonical; selection and
      graphics items are presentation state and must not be used as Core
      topology authority.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or
    runtime multi-document interaction were performed.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #769 REMAINS OPEN. The optional document guard
    appears unwired in the inspected source path; the reported hidden-document
    mutation still requires runtime reproduction and end-to-end ownership
    verification before claiming resolution.

GF-CANVAS-CULL-001 — Issue #768: Viewport Transform Scale Clipping
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #768; ui/canvas/sld_canvas_projection.py;
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py; ui/canvas/grid_scene.py;
    ui/canvas/graphics_view.py; ui/items/equipment_item.py.

Findings:
    1. Source inspection of SLDCanvasProjection did not find the issue's
       described optimized viewport-culling algorithm. The inspected
       projection produces renderer-neutral canvas snapshots; it does not
       decide equipment visibility based on an unscaled viewport rectangle.

    2. SLDCanvasRenderSystem's explicit zoom-dependent visibility behavior
       found in the inspected implementation applies to annotation density.
       The ordinary item realization/synchronization path does not show a
       custom equipment culling pass using an unscaled geometry rectangle.

    3. GridScene adapts grid tier density using the painter world transform,
       and uses scene item bounds for content fitting. This is not evidence
       of the specific SLD equipment culling defect alleged in the issue.

    4. EquipmentItem has local symbol geometry and is presented as a Qt
       graphics item with its transform. Qt scene/view mapping and item
       bounds participate in transformed scene rendering. The issue's
       specific claim cannot be confirmed from the inspected source paths.

Correction decision:
    No speculative culling algorithm was added or rewritten because the
    alleged algorithm was not found. A blanket change to item bounds or
    visibility would risk degrading Qt scene indexing, hit testing, and
    symbol realization without establishing the defect's source.

Required follow-up:
    - Reproduce the reported flicker using large transformers, thin
      polylines and multi-segment bus bars at high/low zoom and viewport
      edges.
    - Capture the affected item's sceneBoundingRect(), boundingRect(),
      transform, effective visibility, scene rect and viewport transform
      while the flicker occurs.
    - Trace any active plugin-specific rendering path or external culling
      helper invoked in the reproduction. If culling exists in a different
      path, use the inverse viewport transform to map the viewport rectangle
      into scene coordinates and expand for stroke/selection extents before
      visibility decisions.
    - Keep viewport culling and graphics geometry in UI presentation; do not
      alter Core electrical topology or derive visibility from network state.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or
    runtime zoom/pan reproduction were performed.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #768 REMAINS OPEN. The issue's claimed culling
    algorithm was not found in the inspected source. Runtime reproduction
    and active rendering-path tracing are required before a corrective code
    change can be justified.

GF-GHOST-WIRE-001 — Issue #767: Ghost Wire / Terminal Identity Split
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #767; core/application/services/simple_wire_service.py;
    core/application/services/electrical_connection_service.py;
    core/application/commands/simple_wire_commands.py;
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py; ui/sld/sld_model.py.

Findings:
    1. The inspected Core SimpleWireConnectionService validates terminal
       references against the current Network before creating a relationship.
       Its removal path resolves the registered connection by connection ID,
       checks any supplied endpoint snapshots against the registered
       relationship, then removes the authoritative relationship transactionally.
       It does not retain or create graphics terminal anchors.

    2. ElectricalConnectionService resolves a terminal reference against the
       Application context before changing terminal.endpoint. It invalidates
       Network topology after connect/disconnect and records an undo operation
       restoring the prior endpoint. A stale or missing terminal reference
       should fail through the resolver's validation/resource path; this
       source alone does not establish a thread lock or continuous error loop.

    3. SLDDocument.remove_node() removes document connections that reference
       the removed node, so the canonical SLD document does not intentionally
       preserve those connection records after node removal.

    4. SLDCanvasRenderSystem.synchronize() reconciles the realized scene
       against the complete committed/draft snapshot. It removes tracked item
       IDs absent from the new snapshot. _remove_realized() also clears
       renderer tracking/signature/diagnostic state and attempts to unregister
       snap candidates and route-edit callbacks before removing each item from
       the scene. This is the appropriate cleanup boundary for transient
       graphics and snap anchors.

    5. No SimpleWireGroup implementation or distinct internal visual-terminal
       anchor cache matching the issue's description was found in the
       inspected active paths. The claimed pan-time race and surviving pixel
       therefore remain unconfirmed. The issue's narrative combines Core
       terminal identity, Simple Wire relationships, SLD document records,
       snap candidates and Qt graphics; these must be observed separately to
       locate the actual stale owner.

Correction decision:
    No speculative Core topology rewrite or graphics deletion patch was made.
    The inspected renderer already has snapshot reconciliation and explicit
    cleanup for realized items and snap candidates. Changing canonical
    connectivity without a reproduced failing reference could corrupt valid
    relationships or hide the actual stale registry.

Required follow-up:
    - Reproduce removal of equipment inserted between two connected wire
      segments while panning, and capture the removed node ID, affected SLD
      connection IDs, Core terminal references, registered SimpleWire
      connections, scene item IDs, and snap-candidate IDs before and after.
    - If a scene item survives but is absent from the new complete snapshot,
      trace why synchronize() did not remove its tracked render ID or whether
      an untracked item was inserted outside the render system.
    - If a snap candidate survives after graphics removal, fix its owner
      registration/unregistration lifecycle; never repair this by changing
      Core terminal IDs.
    - If a connection references a missing Core terminal, reject it at the
      canonical Application resolver boundary and report a structured
      diagnostic without repeated background retries.
    - Keep Simple Wire topology based on canonical endpoint references, never
      on scene coordinates, pan transforms, or visible wire pixels.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or
    runtime pan/delete reproduction were performed.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #767 REMAINS OPEN. No matching SimpleWireGroup or
    stale visual-anchor cache was found in inspected source; runtime evidence
    is required to identify whether the stale owner is the scene, snap system,
    SLD document, or Core topology registry.

GF-CANVAS-COORD-001 — Issue #766: Subpixel Coordinate Accumulation
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #766; ui/canvas/coordinate_system.py;
    ui/canvas/graphics_view.py; ui/canvas/model_placement_tool.py.

Confirmed source finding:
    CoordinateSystem.viewport_to_scene() converted QPointF-compatible mouse
    coordinates to integer QPoint through toPoint() before mapToScene().
    GraphicsView.mouseMoveEvent() performed the same integer conversion
    before emitting cursor_scene_position. Fractional viewport movement was
    therefore discarded at two active presentation-coordinate boundaries.
    This is a concrete source-level precision defect consistent with preview
    cursor drift during pan/placement.

Correction:
    PR #794 maps floating-point viewport positions through the inverse of
    QGraphicsView.viewportTransform(), preserving subpixel positions under
    the current pan/zoom transform. CoordinateSystem retains a legacy
    mapToScene fallback for lightweight adapters without viewportTransform()
    or when the transform is not invertible. GraphicsView uses the same
    inverse-transform approach for cursor feedback and falls back to the
    prior integer mapToScene path only when the transform cannot be inverted.

Boundary:
    The change is confined to UI coordinate conversion. It does not alter
    Core coordinates, network topology, terminal identity, snap semantics,
    or persisted equipment positions. Grid/object snapping remains owned by
    SnapSystem after scene coordinates are resolved.

Required follow-up:
    - Reproduce high-resolution pointer movement while panning and dragging
      placement previews at multiple zoom levels.
    - Verify cursor signal and tool event coordinate parity under the active
      PySide6 viewport transform.
    - Inspect placement snapping separately if the preview still diverges;
      do not compensate with accumulated mutable scene-coordinate offsets.

Verification:
    Focused source inspection and correction only. No tests, CI, syntax/import
    checks, or runtime pointer interaction were performed.

Status:
    SUBPIXEL CONVERSION CORRECTED IN PR #794 — ISSUE #766 REMAINS OPEN pending
    runtime verification of cursor/preview alignment and snap parity.

GF-SLD-PATH-001 — Issue #765: Routed Path Bounds and Selection
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #765; ui/sld/items/sld_connection_item.py;
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py; ui/sld/sld_route_edit_controller.py;
    ui/sld/sld_model.py.

Findings:
    1. SLDConnectionItem.set_visual_route() updates the visual endpoints and
       route-point tuple, then rebuilds the path via QGraphicsPathItem.setPath().
       The reported direct mutation of an internal vertex collection without
       path/bounds notification is not present in this inspected route-update
       method.

    2. For Simple Wire connections, SLDConnectionItem.shape() builds a widened
       QPainterPathStroker hit envelope from the current path, and
       boundingRect() returns that shape's bounds. Both therefore derive from
       the current QPainterPath rather than a separately cached stale route
       rectangle. Other connection kinds use QGraphicsPathItem's standard
       shape/bounds behavior.

    3. SLDCanvasRenderSystem resolves endpoint positions before comparing
       connection render signatures. It calls set_visual_route() when route
       or endpoint geometry changes and removes the previous realized item if
       endpoint resolution fails. This provides an explicit geometry refresh
       path when a new snapshot is synchronized.

    4. The reported selection dead-zone and distant hit activation were not
       reproduced. The source inspected does not establish a custom parent
       bounding-box cache that remains stale after path replacement. Qt's
       setPath() manages QGraphicsPathItem geometry change notifications; a
       separate custom cache was not identified in the inspected item.

Correction decision:
    No speculative prepareGeometryChange() call was added around setPath().
    QGraphicsPathItem owns its path/bounds change contract; redundant or
    incorrectly ordered geometry notifications could create a new defect.
    A code change requires a reproducible path update where the scene index
    retains stale geometry despite setPath().

Required follow-up:
    - Reproduce engineer-owned route editing and endpoint movement while
      selecting near both the new and old path.
    - Capture path(), shape().boundingRect(), boundingRect(),
      sceneBoundingRect(), and scene.items(point) before/after route update.
    - Verify the active scene receives the updated item and that no duplicate
      stale connection item remains in the scene.
    - If the path is current but scene queries remain stale, inspect custom
      item overrides and scene indexing/reparenting before modifying bounds.
    - Keep wire hit geometry in the UI projection; route geometry must not
      create or modify Core terminal identity.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or
    runtime route-edit/selection interaction were performed.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #765 REMAINS OPEN. The alleged stale cached
    bounding-box mechanism was not found in the inspected item path; runtime
    reproduction and scene-index evidence are required before a safe fix.

GF-SLD-ZORDER-001 — Issue #764: Deterministic Canvas Layering
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #764; ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py;
    ui/canvas/sld_graphics_item_factory.py; ui/items/equipment_item.py;
    ui/sld/items/sld_connection_item.py.

Confirmed source finding:
    The renderer assigned inconsistent partial z-values: draft nodes and
    draft fallback nodes used z=1, annotations used z=5, while committed
    equipment and both committed/draft connection items retained Qt's
    default z=0. The renderer therefore did not encode a stable intended
    ordering of wire < equipment < annotation. In particular, committed
    wires and equipment could share z=0, leaving same-z overlap ordering
    dependent on scene insertion order rather than the engineering layer
    contract. The issue's claim of parallel background-thread insertion was
    not established by the inspected code.

Correction:
    PR #796 defines explicit renderer layer constants and applies them to all
    realized paths, including degraded committed equipment and draft fallback:
        connections = 10
        equipment   = 20
        annotations = 30
    Grid/background drawing remains behind scene items. The renderer continues
    to synchronize the scene on the UI projection path; no parallel item
    creation or Core mutation was introduced.

Boundary:
    Z-order is presentation-only. It changes visual stacking and hit-test
    precedence according to QGraphicsScene rules, not Core electrical truth,
    endpoint identity, topology, or solver inputs. Annotation labels continue
    to accept no mouse buttons.

Required follow-up:
    - Inspect selection handles and any future overlay/control items to assign
      explicit layer values rather than relying on the default z=0.
    - Reproduce dense layouts with overlapping routes, equipment and labels
      at several zoom levels; verify labels remain legible and equipment
      receives pointer events as intended.
    - Keep z-value policy centralized in the renderer rather than assigning
      arbitrary values in unrelated item constructors.

Verification:
    Focused source inspection and correction only. No tests, CI, syntax/import
    checks, or runtime large-layout rendering were performed.

Status:
    EXPLICIT LAYERING CORRECTED IN PR #796 — ISSUE #764 REMAINS OPEN pending
    runtime verification of overlapping scene stacking and interaction.

GF-UI-REFRESH-001 — Issue #763 — Study and Canvas Refresh Synchronization Audit
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #763; core/application/events.py; core/application/study.py;
    ui/events/update_boundary.py; ui/projection/ui_projection_coordinator.py;
    ui/events/sld_update_coordinator.py; ui/projection/study_projection.py;
    ui/canvas/canvas_composition.py; ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py.

Findings:
    1. UIUpdateBoundary subscribes to ApplicationEvent and synchronously
       delegates each event to UIProjectionCoordinator. The coordinator routes
       each event to interested projections once and catches/logs projection
       failures. It has no event coalescing, frame scheduler, or refresh-rate
       limit; therefore a burst of relevant Application events can cause
       repeated synchronous projection work on the publisher's call path.

    2. SLDUpdateCoordinator handles committed network/presentation events by
       synchronizing the SLD read projection and calling the canvas refresh
       callback. DraftChanged also projects draft state and refreshes the
       canvas. SLDCanvasSurface then builds a complete committed or composite
       snapshot and calls SLDCanvasRenderSystem.synchronize(); renderer
       reconciliation tracks IDs/signatures and is not itself evidence of a
       unconditional scene-wide repaint on every numerical solver iteration.

    3. StudyStarted, StudyCompleted, StudyFailed and StudyCancelled are
       lifecycle events. StudyProjection subscribes to relevant study and
       model events to refresh study-case/result presentation. StudyCompleted
       is not included in SLDUpdateCoordinator.event_types, so the inspected
       route does not establish that each completed study triggers an SLD
       canvas refresh. The inspected Study event contract also does not by
       itself establish per-iteration solver result publication.

    4. The issue's specific assertion that solver iteration loops continuously
       publish results through UIUpdateBoundary and thereby force global SLD
       repaint is not proven by the inspected active event path. A genuine
       risk remains: multiple committed/draft events arriving in a burst can
       synchronously cause repeated projection and canvas snapshot
       reconciliation without coalescing.

Correction decision:
    No speculative scheduler, timer, worker-thread dispatch, or dropped-event
    behavior was introduced. The coordinator is framework-neutral and does not
    own Qt event-loop scheduling. Adding asynchronous/coalesced refresh without
    a defined invalidation contract could lose ordering-sensitive project,
    draft, close, or commit transitions and could display stale electrical
    results. A safe performance correction needs a concrete trace showing the
    emitting solver/event source, event frequency, thread affinity, and which
    projection is refreshed for each event. If coalescing is implemented,
    preserve lifecycle boundaries and always project the latest authoritative
    Application snapshot on the UI thread; never publish partial solver
    iterations as committed Core truth.

Required follow-up:
    - Capture event type, publisher thread, emission cadence and projection
      refresh duration during a reproducible heavy study.
    - Confirm whether numerical iterations emit ApplicationEvents or whether
      the observed traffic originates from model edits, draft updates, or
      result-display callbacks.
    - Profile SLD canvas snapshot creation and renderer reconciliation
      separately from Qt paint scheduling and study-result panel updates.
    - If a high-frequency event source is confirmed, define safe coalescing
      rules by event family; never coalesce away ProjectClosed, project
      activation boundaries, failed/accepted command outcomes, or required
      final StudyCompleted/StudyFailed status.
    - Keep all Qt scheduling in UI adapters via ui/core/qt.py; keep solver
      computation and authoritative study results independent of UI graphics.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or
    runtime heavy-study profiling were performed, in accordance with the
    repository audit rules.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #763 REMAINS OPEN. Synchronous refresh work on
    relevant event bursts is confirmed, but the claimed solver-iteration
    repaint loop is not established by the inspected source. Runtime event
    tracing and profiling are required before a safe performance correction.


======================================================================
GF-SLD-INSERT-HIT-001 — Issue #762 — Layered Route Intersection Target Resolution
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #762; ui/tools/electrical_insertion_tool.py;
    ui/sld/items/sld_connection_item.py; core/application/commands/insertion_commands.py;
    core/application/services/electrical_insertion_service.py.

Confirmed source finding:
    ElectricalInsertionTool._resolve_target() accepted the first item supplied
    by the event or the first non-null controller hit resolver. It did not
    compare all Simple Wire candidates under the pointer. SLDConnectionItem
    provides an insertion_target() calculation that returns the closest point,
    distance and route segment for that particular connection, but the tool
    did not use those distances to disambiguate overlapping route layers.
    Consequently scene stacking/hit resolver order could select a different
    electrical connection than the nearest geometric target.

Correction:
    PR #798 inspects all scene items at the pointer position when a scene
    point-query API is available, asks each insertion-capable item for its
    route-specific target, and ranks candidates by the returned finite distance.
    If distinct Core connection identities are equally near within a small
    numeric tolerance, the tool fails closed rather than splitting an
    arbitrary wire at a true crossing. Explicit event-provided connection items
    remain authoritative; older single-item controller adapters remain a
    compatibility fallback when scene point queries are unavailable.

Boundary:
    The change is confined to UI hit-target resolution. The selected target
    still crosses the canonical InsertEquipmentIntoConnectionCommand and
    Application transaction service. No Qt graphics object is passed into
    the command/Core payload; only connection identity, coordinates, segment
    index and value contracts are sent. Core topology remains authoritative.

Required follow-up:
    - Reproduce two visually crossing Simple Wires with distinct Core
      connection IDs; verify an unambiguous offset selects the nearest route.
    - Verify an exactly ambiguous crossing is rejected without changing either
      connection or creating equipment.
    - Verify explicit connection-item events and controller-only adapters keep
      their expected behavior.
    - Confirm the active snap system exposes the same canonical scene used by
      the renderer, and retain UI-only Qt imports through ui/core/qt.py.

Verification:
    Source-level correction only. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or
    runtime interaction were performed, in accordance with repository rules.

Status:
    HIT-TARGET RANKING CORRECTED IN PR #798 — ISSUE #762 REMAINS OPEN pending
    runtime verification of layered intersections, ambiguous-hit rejection,
    and transactional non-mutation on rejected gestures.

GF-SLD-ORPHAN-001 — Issue #761 — Deleted Equipment and Orphaned Terminal Graphic Audit
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #761; ui/canvas/sld_graphics_item_factory.py;
    ui/items/equipment_item.py; ui/core/selection_manager.py;
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py; ui/canvas/preview_layer.py.

Findings:
    1. The inspected SLDGraphicsItemFactory creates an EquipmentItem for an
       equipment node. EquipmentItem exposes terminal anchor/snap metadata
       from the symbol definition and equipment terminal model; this source
       path does not establish separate persistent QGraphicsItem instances for
       each electrical terminal line as described in the issue.

    2. SLDCanvasRenderSystem._remove_realized() removes tracked renderer-owned
       graphics, unbinds route-edit callbacks, unregisters snap candidates,
       removes items from the scene, and clears associated render signatures
       and diagnostics. The canonical renderer cleanup path is explicit.

    3. SelectionManager stores selected object identities, not retained
       QGraphicsItem pointers. Its graphics synchronization and ID lookup
       enumerate the current scene and therefore do not establish a stale
       pointer to a removed equipment item. A selected identity can outlive a
       removed graphic until selection is reconciled, but that alone does not
       prove the reported wrapped-C++-object crash.

    4. PreviewLayer owns transient preview items and removes them from its
       scene during clear/remove operations. The inspected files do not show a
       terminal-line interaction buffer that retains a deleted child pointer.

    equipment/snap lifecycle behavior without addressing the real owner.

    - Reproduce multi-selection and deletion with the exact equipment symbol
      and capture scene item types, parentItem(), scene(), object identity,
      and terminal/snap metadata before and after renderer synchronization.
    - Capture the complete RuntimeError traceback and identify which owner
      retains the deleted QGraphicsItem wrapper (tool, preview layer, snap
      candidate, selection adapter, or other interaction buffer).
    - Confirm whether terminal lines are separate graphics in the active
      runtime configuration or only terminal anchor metadata on EquipmentItem.
    - Ensure any confirmed stale interaction cache is purged at the same
      renderer removal lifecycle; never pass QGraphicsItem references into
      Core or Application commands.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or
    runtime multi-select/delete reproduction were performed, in accordance
    with repository audit rules.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #761 REMAINS OPEN. The described independent
    terminal-line graphic and stale pointer owner were not found in the
    inspected source path; runtime traceback and object-lifecycle evidence
    are required before a safe correction.

GF-SLD-ACTIVATE-001 — Issue #760 — Multi-SLD Document Activation and Re-Projection Audit
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #760; ui/workspace/document_manager.py;
    ui/editors/sld/sld_document_tabs.py; ui/sld/sld_document.py;
    ui/canvas/canvas_composition.py; ui/canvas/sld_canvas_projection.py;
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py.

Findings:
    1. DocumentManager.activate() resolves the registered document and updates
       the active document identity; it does not itself construct or destroy
       canvas graphics. SLDDocumentTabs is a presentation-only tab strip that
       calls a supplied activation callback. The activation callback's full
       application wiring and runtime timing were not established by this
       bounded source inspection.

    2. CanvasComposition.present_document() and present_composite_document()
       synchronously project the selected SLD document into an immutable
       snapshot and call SLDCanvasRenderSystem.synchronize(). These calls are
       explicit UI presentation entry points.

    3. SLDCanvasRenderSystem.synchronize() is documented and implemented as
       incremental reconciliation. It compares per-item signatures, reuses
       unchanged realized nodes/connections/annotations, and removes tracked
       items absent from the desired snapshot. It does not unconditionally
       clear the complete scene before every snapshot. Changed or newly
       realized items can still require synchronous graphics creation on the
       UI thread.

    4. The issue's assertion that each activation necessarily tears down and
       rebuilds hundreds of QGraphicsItems is not supported by the inspected
       renderer path. However, each presentation call rebuilds the renderer-
       neutral document snapshot, and there is no per-document snapshot cache
       visible in CanvasComposition. Repeated activation can therefore repeat
       projection/signature work even where graphics realization is reused.
       Whether this is material or causes the reported flicker requires
       runtime profiling.

Correction decision:
    No speculative persistent cache, background graphics creation, or
    asynchronous activation was introduced. SLDCanvasSnapshot contains
    immutable projected presentation values but its validity depends on the
    document model and current Application read projections. Caching snapshots
    without an explicit invalidation/version contract could display stale
    electrical values or miss edits in background documents. QGraphicsItem
    realization and scene mutation must remain on the UI thread.

Required follow-up:
    - Trace the actual tab activation callback through its caller to
      CanvasComposition.present_document()/present_composite_document().
    - Measure snapshot projection time separately from renderer reconciliation,
      graphics item construction, and Qt paint/input latency on a multi-SLD
      project with heavy custom symbols.
    - Record document identity plus model/read-model version or revision and
      define invalidation for model edits, Application events, draft changes,
      symbol configuration changes, and document close before adding caching.
    - Verify unchanged item signatures preserve graphics identity across tab
      switches and that active-document switching cannot leak graphics or
      draft state between documents.
    - Keep all Qt/scene operations in UI adapters and preserve the canonical
      Application/Core authority boundary.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or
    multi-SLD runtime profiling were performed, in accordance with repository
    audit rules.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #760 REMAINS OPEN. Full-scene teardown/rebuild was
    not found in the inspected renderer; synchronous snapshot projection and
    potential repeated work are confirmed, but runtime profiling and complete
    activation wiring are required before a safe optimization.


======================================================================
GF-CANVAS-PRECISION-001 — Issue #758 — High-Zoom Coordinate Precision Audit
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #758; ui/canvas/sld_graphics_item_factory.py;
    ui/items/equipment_item.py; ui/equipment/symbol/symbol_definition.py;
    ui/core/snap_system.py; ui/canvas/coordinate_system.py;
    ui/canvas/graphics_view.py.

Findings:
    1. The alleged float64-to-float32 cast in SLDGraphicsItemFactory was not
       found in the inspected active factory path. Node positions and line
       endpoints are supplied as QPointF values from Python numeric
       coordinates; the factory validates finite coordinates but does not
       explicitly cast them to numpy.float32 or another single-precision type.

    2. EquipmentItem resolves terminal anchors using QPointF and mapToScene().
       SnapSystem reads current item snap points in scene coordinates, and its
       contract explicitly separates viewport conversion from scene-space
       snapping. The inspected coordinate system preserves QPointF fractional
       viewport values by inverting viewportTransform() rather than relying
       exclusively on integer mapToScene(QPoint) conversion.

    3. Qt's internal graphics/rendering implementation may have its own numeric
       representation limits, but the reported application-level float32
       narrowing is not demonstrated by these Python sources. A few-pixel
       visual discrepancy could also originate from symbol anchor definitions,
       transforms, zoom-dependent stroke/rendering, or snap tolerance; source
       inspection alone cannot distinguish these causes.

Correction decision:
    No precision-related cast or broad coordinate representation change was
    applied because the alleged narrowing site was not found. Replacing Qt
    geometry paths or changing snap tolerance without a reproducible mismatch
    could make normal-scale interactions less stable and would not establish
    that the precision issue is fixed.

Required follow-up:
    - Reproduce a high-zoom bus/breaker terminal mismatch and capture the
      viewport cursor QPointF, inverse-transformed scene point, EquipmentItem
      mapToScene terminal anchor, SnapResult position, and rendered path endpoint.
    - Compare numeric scene coordinates before and after rendering and compute
      pixel-space residual at the current view transform.
    - Confirm whether any downstream extension or custom symbol code explicitly
      narrows coordinates to float32.
    - If a real discrepancy is found, correct the first narrowing or transform
      mismatch at its owner; keep Core topology and electrical connectivity
      authoritative and do not compensate by changing electrical terminals.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or
    high-zoom runtime measurements were performed, in accordance with
    repository audit rules.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #758 REMAINS OPEN. No explicit float32 narrowing was
    found in the inspected path. Runtime high-zoom coordinate capture is
    required to localize the reported visual residual before changing code.


======================================================================
GF-UI-TOOL-LIFECYCLE-001 — Issue #757 — Equipment Placement Tool State Leak Audit
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issue #757; ui/core/tool_manager.py; ui/creation/creation_context.py;
    ui/tools/electrical_insertion_tool.py; ui/canvas/draft_sld_projection.py;
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py.

Findings:
    1. ToolManager has explicit lifecycle guards: ordinary tool switching is
       rejected while a CreationContext draft or temporary interaction remains
       active. Callers that authorize cancellation enter the explicit cancel
       path instead of silently dropping the active tool reference.

    2. Escape handling cancels the active CreationContext and notifies the
       controller when a draft existed. ToolManager.cancel() also cancels the
       temporary interaction and parent tool state, then clears CreationContext.
       ElectricalInsertionTool clears its target/preview state on deactivation
       and cancellation.

    3. CreationContext owns only transient UI authoring state. DraftSLDProjection
       reads Application-owned DraftNetwork state, and renderer synchronization
       reconciles draft graphics against the latest projected snapshot. A
       draft object that has already been placed into Application DraftNetwork
       is not equivalent to an untracked CreationDraft and must not be silently
       deleted as garbage during tool deactivation.

    4. The specific claimed path—tool pointer dropped without lifecycle cleanup,
       leaving a scene item outside both renderer ownership and DraftNetwork—
       was not established by the inspected code. A ghost could still arise if
       a caller bypasses ToolManager guards or a preview item is attached
       outside the renderer/PreviewLayer ownership paths, but this requires
       runtime evidence and the actual item identity.

Correction decision:
    No speculative cleanup sweep was added. The source already guards normal
    tool switching and explicitly cancels CreationContext on Escape/deactivate.
    A global scene sweep on every tool change could remove legitimate committed
    graphics or Application-owned draft objects and would violate projection
    ownership. Cleanup must be scoped to the owning preview/renderer IDs.

Required follow-up:
    - Reproduce the exact tool transition (Escape, palette switch, pan/select
      tool) and capture ToolManager.active_tool_id, CreationContext.active,
      Application.read_draft_network(), PreviewLayer item IDs, renderer-owned
      IDs, and scene item IDs before and after the transition.
    - Determine whether the phantom is an uncommitted preview, an intentional
      Application DraftNetwork placement, or a renderer item absent from the
      latest snapshot.
    - If an orphan is confirmed, fix its owning lifecycle transition and
      remove only its tracked IDs; preserve legitimate placed draft equipment
      until explicit cancellation/removal/commit through Application.
    - Keep Core independent of Qt graphics and preserve the standard mutation
      path for any authoritative DraftNetwork change.

Verification:
    Source-level inspection only. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or
    runtime tool-switch reproduction were performed, in accordance with
    repository audit rules.

Status:
    AUDIT RECORDED — ISSUE #757 REMAINS OPEN. Normal lifecycle guards and
    cancellation paths are present; the reported orphaned visual item and its
    owner require a reproducible runtime trace before a safe correction.

GF-RECONCILE-2026-10-29-A — Batch A issue/PR reconciliation
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issues #745, #747, #751, #753, #754, #755.
    Authoritative repository: SubhenduMishra29/GridForge, branch main.

Baseline confirmed:
    - #753: PR #777 is merged. Source preparation rejects stale or mismatched
      Power Flow operating points using revision, project activation, convergence,
      bus ordering, and prepared-input snapshot identity.
    - #754: PRs #778 and #780 are merged. DraftNetwork commit has an Application/
      Controller asynchronous entrypoint. This does not prove the commit is
      non-blocking under large-network runtime load or establish UI responsiveness.
    - #755: PR #779 is merged. Nested settings/metadata in
      ProtectionFunctionConfiguration are recursively frozen for undo history.
      This does not certify every mutation/undo closure or multi-object rollback.
    - #747: PR #784 is an audit-only change. Current inspected canonical power-flow
      path is NumPy/SparseLinearSolver and does not establish a live CUDA
      host/device-copy bottleneck. The future accelerator result-ownership and
      presentation-transfer contract remains undefined; do not add implicit
      array copies at the read-model boundary.
    - #745: PRs #781 and #782 are merged. Function-code mismatch is rejected and
      the breaker-open postcondition is checked. GF-PROT-042 remains OPEN because
      production trigger wiring, bounded operational timing, and persisted typed
      protection-function-to-target/action mapping are not proven complete.
    - #751: Existing EngineeringPluginHost validates plugin descriptors and owns
      activation/rollback, but passes the canonical Application object to in-process
      Python plugin code. MappingProxyType and JSON Schema do not sandbox code.
      No claim of hostile-plugin isolation is made. A safe correction requires an
      explicit trust model and, for hostile third-party code, a separate process
      boundary with a narrow validated IPC/capability contract; removing the

      contract would break the existing activation API.

Disposition:
    - Keep #745 OPEN pending a typed persisted mapping and production timing/trigger
      contract with a source-backed end-to-end consumer trace.
    - Keep #747 OPEN but narrow its current claim to an unimplemented future
      accelerator/presentation boundary; require per-result host/device ownership
      contracts before implementation.
    - Keep #751 OPEN pending an approved trusted-plugin threat model or process-
      isolated plugin execution contract. Do not describe in-process Python as a
      sandbox.
    - Keep #753, #754, and #755 OPEN pending the remaining issue acceptance evidence;
      merged PRs address scoped defects but are not sufficient proof of complete
      end-to-end or runtime closure.
    - No issues closed by this reconciliation entry.

Verification boundary:
    GitHub issue/PR metadata and source files were inspected. No tests, CI,
    syntax/import checks, runtime simulation, plugin isolation exercise, large-
    network performance run, or GUI runtime verification were performed.

GF-RECONCILE-2026-10-29-B — Draft, placement, and clipboard issue audit

    2026-10-29

Source findings:
    - #824: Escape cancels transient CreationContext/tool interaction. The inspected
      path does not establish whether the reported lost route is a preview-only
      interaction or an already-authored SLD route edit. Preserve canonical
      document/Application state; no global scene-buffer workaround is justified
      without a route-identity trace.

      recreates CreationDraft at the next input boundary. The described missing
      sticky-tool behavior is not established by source; runtime interaction state
      is required before adding a second sticky-tool mechanism.

      activation generation, and source revision, then rejects execution if the
      active project/revision/draft identity or serialized contents changed. The
      claimed endpoint-mapping race is not established by this source path.
    - #807: SnapSystem selects the nearest renderer-owned candidate in the active
      scene and resolves equal-distance ties by deterministic discovery order.
      Preview feedback oscillation remains possible but requires runtime candidate
      and preview geometry tracing.
    - #752: CommitNetworkHandler derives Core IDs deterministically from canonical
      equipment type plus DraftEquipment.draft_id, and resolves DraftEndpointReference
      through the same old-to-new Core ID map. A different temporary-ID generator
      alone is not identity drift; the reported collision requires reproduction.

    stress, UI interaction, or runtime verification were performed.
