GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-001 — Issue #817 — Obstacle-Aware Orthogonal Routing
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ui/connections/connection_router.py; Issue #817.

Changes:
    1. Replaced the direct-only route implementation with a deterministic
       rectilinear visibility-grid router.
    2. Added finite-coordinate and axis-aligned bounds validation, obstacle
       clearance, bend cost, and penalties for intersections with existing
       routes.
    3. Added path invariants: no duplicate consecutive points, no diagonal
       segments, and no silent straight-line fallback when obstacles block
       every route.
    4. Kept routing renderer-neutral. The router does not resolve terminals,
       change electrical connectivity, or mutate Core state.

Integration boundary:
    SLDCanvasRenderSystem supplies scene-space bounds for realized
    non-endpoint nodes and realized connection polylines when it calculates
    automatic routes. The router excludes source/target node bounds from its
    obstacle set and uses existing routes as soft crossing/overlap penalties.
    These are presentation inputs only; the router does not infer or mutate
    electrical connectivity. Runtime near-miss verification and explicit
    route-edit interaction review remain follow-up work before Issue #817 can
    be closed.

Verification:
    Repository source change only. No tests or CI were run, per repository
    rules. Python syntax/import integrity and runtime route cases remain to be
    checked before merge.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED ON fix/issue-817-orthogonal-routing — PENDING REVIEW AND
    SOURCE-LEVEL VERIFICATION. Issue #817 remains open.


======================================================================
GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-002 — Issue #817 — Exterior Visibility Corridor
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ui/connections/connection_router.py; fix/issue-817-orthogonal-routing.

Finding:
    The visibility grid initially used only source/target coordinates and
    obstacle clearance edges. When obstacle clearance regions overlap, those
    coordinates can leave no usable path around the outside of the obstacle
    group even where an exterior orthogonal route is geometrically possible.

Correction:
    Added deterministic exterior corridor coordinates beyond the full
    obstacle envelope, with a margin derived from the obstacle span and
    configured clearance. The router can now consider paths around the outside
    of overlapping obstacle groups without changing endpoint identities or
    electrical topology.

Verification:
    Source correction committed to the feature branch. No tests or CI run.
    Python syntax/import integrity and runtime route scenarios remain pending
    under the repository's stated acceptance process.

Status:
    EXTERIOR VISIBILITY CORRIDOR CORRECTED — ISSUE #817 REMAINS OPEN. Existing
    route crossing integration and the near-miss electrical-connectivity path
    still require direct source audit and verification.

GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-003 — Issue #817 — Existing Route Awareness
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py; ui/connections/connection_router.py.

Finding:
    The renderer passed obstacle bounds to the router but did not pass existing
    realized connection polylines. The router's crossing penalty capability was
    therefore unused by the active SLD auto-route path.

Correction:
    The renderer now collects route endpoints and intermediate route points
    from already-realized connection items and supplies those polylines as
    soft crossing-penalty inputs for new automatic routes. Unsupported/stale
    visual geometry is skipped rather than allowed to affect electrical state.
    Existing paths remain presentation-only; this does not mutate routes or
    establish electrical connectivity.

Verification:
    Source correction committed to the feature branch. No tests or CI run.
    Python syntax/import integrity and runtime routing scenarios remain pending.

Status:
    EXISTING ROUTE PENALTIES WIRED INTO RENDERER — ISSUE #817 REMAINS OPEN.
    The separate near-miss connectivity symptom still requires tracing through
    the active authoring and Application command path before closure.

GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-004 — Snap Candidate Lifecycle Cleanup
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ui/core/snap_system.py; fix/issue-817-orthogonal-routing.

Finding:
    SnapSystem.dispose() cleared the candidate and callback registries without
    disconnecting movement signals registered on graphics items. A long-lived
    item could retain a callback to the disposed service and later attempt a
    refresh against an inactive SnapSystem.

Correction:
    Disposal now disconnects each registered movement callback before clearing
    candidate references. Already-deleted Qt senders and already-disconnected
    signals are handled as cleanup no-ops. Core topology and snap identity are
    unchanged.

Verification:
    Source correction committed to the feature branch. No tests or CI run.
    Syntax/import integrity and Qt lifecycle behavior remain pending review.

Status:
    SNAP CANDIDATE SIGNAL CLEANUP CORRECTED. ISSUE #817 REMAINS OPEN WHILE THE
    NEAR-MISS VISUAL-VERSUS-ELECTRICAL CONNECTIVITY PATH IS STILL UNVERIFIED.

GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-006 — Existing Route Coordinate Space
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py; fix/issue-817-orthogonal-routing.

Finding:
    SLDConnectionItem.visual_endpoints() and route_points() expose coordinates
    from the item's local QGraphics path, while ConnectionRouter receives
    scene-space source/target coordinates. Passing local existing-route points
    directly can skew crossing penalties when an item has a non-identity scene
    transform.

Correction:
    Existing connection endpoints and bends are now mapped through mapToScene
    before being supplied to the router. Items without that mapping capability
    retain their exposed coordinates as a compatibility fallback; unsupported
    geometry remains non-authoritative presentation data.

Verification:
    Source updated on the feature branch. No tests or CI run. Syntax/import and
    transformed-item runtime behavior remain pending source-level verification.

Status:
    EXISTING ROUTE COORDINATE SPACE NORMALIZED. ISSUE #817 REMAINS OPEN.

GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-013 — Undo Failure and Pre-Commit Rollback Audit
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    core/application/command_manager.py; core/application/transaction.py;
    fix/issue-817-orthogonal-routing.

Audit:
    1. CommandManager invokes the Application pre-commit hook while the
       transaction remains open. Handler, result-validation, and hook failures
       enter the same rollback boundary.
    2. Transaction.rollback() attempts all registered inverse operations in
       reverse order and records ROLLBACK_FAILED if any inverse fails. Rollback
       finalizers run only if every content inverse succeeds.
    3. CommandManager._rollback_safely() marks the manager DEGRADED when rollback
       fails, preventing subsequent commands from proceeding on uncertain state.
    4. A failed undo marks the manager DEGRADED; if zero inverse operations are
       reported complete, it attempts to restore the undo history record first.
       The fail-closed decision remains necessary because an inverse may mutate
       before raising.
    5. Post-commit history-recording failures also mark the manager DEGRADED;
       committed Core state is not incorrectly rolled back.

Correction decision:
    No additional code change was warranted by this source pass. The inspected
    transaction and history paths already implement reverse-order rollback,
    continue attempting remaining inverses after an inverse failure, and fail
    closed when Core/history integrity is uncertain.

Verification:
    Source inspection only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, failure injection,
    or runtime transaction/undo verification were performed.

Status:
    SOURCE-LEVEL FAILURE-PATH AUDIT RECORDED. Issue #817 remains open pending
    the required end-to-end SLD/network runtime verification.

GF-RECONCILE-2026-10-29-G — Protection/control concurrency contracts
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issues #839, #840, #845, #859.

Source findings:
    - #839: MeasurementChannel accepts finite scalar and complex values and carries
      phase, signal type, scale/polarity, quality, and sample metadata. TopologyManager
      excludes measurement-secondary terminals from conductive Bus topology. These
      boundaries exist, but a complete primary-to-instrument-transformer-to-channel-
      to-protection phase-isolation/vector contract is not established by this audit.
    - #840: ControlInterlock and ControlEngine provide logic-level interlock gates.
      Application.execute serializes each command through an RLock, but this does
      not itself establish a multi-command physical interlocking contract across
      multiple targets. No parallel Pre-Execution Lock Registry should be added
      without a demonstrated race and approved command precondition semantics.
    - #845: CloseBreakerCommand exists; no synchronism-check evaluator or explicit
      ANSI 25 voltage/frequency/phase tolerance contract was found in reviewed
      Application entrypoints. A safe correction requires a defined measurement
      contract and fail-closed rules; do not infer synchronism from topology alone.
    - #859: TopologyManager builds island/topology snapshots and tracks topology
      revision; it does not own global frequency state or a CUDA frequency buffer.
      The alleged race owner/backend is not established by current source evidence.

Disposition:
    - Keep all four issues open pending the specific source/runtime evidence and
      contracts recorded in their issue comments.
    - Any ANSI 25 implementation must execute at the Application/Core breaker-close
      mutation boundary and record its decision; it must not be a UI-only warning.
    - Measurement secondary references remain outside conductive power topology.
    - Frequency is study/runtime state, not TopologyManager-owned topology state.
    - No implementation or issue closure is claimed by this audit.

Verification boundary:
    Source inspection only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, concurrent runtime
    trace, solver/device profiling, or protection study was run.

======================================================================
GF-RECONCILE-2026-10-29-H — Measurement, transient, and protection model gaps
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issues #841, #842, #843, #846, #847, #848, #849, #851, #852, #853,
    #854, #855, #856, #857.

Findings and dispositions:
    - #841: Dynamics package exposes event, DAE, and transient-stability solvers.
      The post-protection-trip topology-change reinitialization contract remains
      unproven. Require a trip-to-solver event trace and explicit post-event
      initialization/failure semantics.
    - #842: MeasurementChannel documentation assigns saturation, filtering, and
      instrument error to the physical measurement source/model. CT/PT saturation
      must be implemented as a physical instrument model with validated excitation,
      remanent-flux, burden, and secondary waveform contracts—not generic clipping.
    - #843: Anti-aliasing belongs to the acquisition/measurement-generation boundary
      with explicit sample rate and filter response; do not silently filter the
      generic logical MeasurementChannel.
    - #846: The claimed SER single-array bottleneck is unproven. Require the actual
      recorder, append path, buffer policy, timestamp owner, and profile.
    - #847: Multi-relay bus-zone trip requires an explicit atomic multi-target
      Application command if partial clearing is reproducible. Individual command
      serialization is not a multi-command atomicity guarantee.
    - #848: Frequency/ROCOF estimator and derivative spikes are not established by
      the issue narrative alone. Require the active estimator, sample interval,
      waveform, and response-time/error budget.
    - #849: Identify the exact interlock override owner and project.json path before
      changing persistence. Overrides must be explicit and auditable project state,
      not hidden untyped runtime booleans.
    - #851: Require the canonical ANSI 21 zone implementation, settings, input
      snapshot provenance, and out-of-zone fault reproduction before changing
      protection-zone coordination.
    - #852: Point-on-wave switching requires a defined sampled-waveform source,
      zero-crossing interpolation, noise/DC-offset model, and breaker delay contract.
    - #853: Application serializes individual commands; an atomic UFLS/UVLS batch
      contract and actual concurrent dispatch path have not been established.
      Any batch must be Application-owned and rollback-capable.
    - #854: A canonical persisted relay setting-group model and dynamic switch path
      were not established. Future changes must publish immutable setting snapshots
      at an evaluation boundary.
    - #855: Require a traced ANSI 50BF timer event → typed target mapping →
      Application command path; protection worker threads must not mutate Core
      directly.
    - #856: Cold-load pickup desensitization is a scoped load/reconnection transient
      model and coordination contract, not a global threshold increase.
    - #857: DC-offset and fundamental phasor treatment must follow the specific
      protection element's measurement quantity and an identified acquisition
      contract; do not generically replace peak/instantaneous values with filtered
      values.

Disposition:
    Keep all scoped issues open pending the source/runtime evidence and domain
    contracts recorded in the issue threads. No new protection thresholds, solver
    workarounds, filtering behavior, or unverified physics were introduced.

Verification boundary:
    Source and issue inspection only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, transient
    simulation, relay coordination study, event-buffer profiling, or runtime fault
    injection was performed.

GF-RECONCILE-2026-10-29-I — New authoring, playback, merge, and plugin capabilities
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issues #826, #827, #830, #833, #834, #835, #836.

Findings and dispositions:
    - #826: Tool/CreationContext state is transient; DraftNetwork authoring state is
      Application-owned. A dedicated draft undo journal is not established by the
      committed command history. Define gesture cancellation, draft undo/redo, and
      commit semantics before changing Escape behavior.
    - #827: Inline insertion already has an explicit SelectTool/ElectricalInsertionTool
      path. Proximity assist may select a candidate, but cannot infer electrical
      insertion intent from distance alone. Keep PT/CVT out of series insertion
      without their own electrical contracts.
    - #830: Obstacle-aware orthogonal routing is on main through PR #837 and overlaps
      #817. This issue's distinct increment is live trajectory preview; reuse the
      canonical router rather than create a second A* implementation.
    - #833: Time-indexed dynamic-study playback is a new presentation capability.
      Define StudyResult time-series schema, deterministic cursor, pause/seek,
      scheduling/backpressure, and read-only projection semantics.
    - #834: The 2D canvas needs a profiled spatial-index/culling design, not a 3D
      octree by default. Destructive scene unloading can invalidate selection,
      snap registration, and object lifecycle; preserve canonical document state.
    - #835: Visual three-way project merge needs stable identities, schema migration,
      field-level conflict classification, and typed Application patch commands.
      Generic “blend” behavior is unsafe for electrical parameters without
      property-specific semantics.
    - #836: Process-isolated plugin execution is the appropriate direction for
      untrusted third-party code, but requires versioned IPC, explicit capabilities,
      resource/time limits, cancellation/termination, and an SDK migration path.
      Current in-process Python plugins are not a sandbox.

Disposition:
    Keep all scoped issues open as capability/contract work. No feature was marked
    implemented merely because the architecture direction was described. Do not
    pass Qt objects, Application instances, live Core objects, or arbitrary Python
    callables across a future plugin process boundary.

Verification boundary:
    Source and issue inspection only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, profiling,
    multi-process plugin exercise, playback runtime, or merge-conflict fixture was run.

======================================================================
2026-10-29 — WORKSPACE TRANSITION ROLLBACK RECOVERY CORRECTION
======================================================================

Scope
-----
ui/workspace/project_workspace.py
ProjectWorkspaceLifecycle._restore_transition_snapshot()

Confirmed defect
----------------
Rollback previously stopped immediately when WorkspaceController.deactivate()
raised. That prevented independent restoration attempts for the project identity,
document collection, active document, view collection, active view, and prior
workspace/layout. A realization-layer failure could therefore suppress recovery
of logical workspace state and obscure additional recovery failures.

Correction applied
------------------
- Rollback now attempts workspace deactivation, document/view clearing,
  project restoration, each document/view registration, active selections,
  and workspace/layout restoration as independent steps.
- A failed step no longer prevents later independent restoration attempts.
- All restoration failures are retained in a combined diagnostic; the first
  failure is preserved as the exception cause and individual failures are
  attached as exception notes when supported.
- The frozen boundary remains unchanged: this is UI workspace lifecycle
  recovery; it does not move project/domain truth into UI or introduce Qt
  dependencies into Core.

Branch
------
fix/workspace-transition-rollback-recovery

Commit
------
bdb47b0794d9f40ad3667145b724098fe0d53570

Verification status
-------------------
Source-level replacement was applied against the current main version and
committed to the focused correction branch. No tests, CI, GUI runtime, or
import checks were run. Project-scoped undo/redo history and cross-workspace
selection/action synchronization remain open audit items pending their own
source trace; they are not marked corrected by this entry.

======================================================================
WORKSPACE SWITCH SELECTION / ENGINEERING CONTEXT CORRECTION
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Branch:
    fix/workspace-transition-rollback-recovery

Files:
    main.py

Finding:
    The canonical workspace activation path changed the active workspace
    and updated only EngineeringContext.discipline. The shared SLD
    SelectionManager retained selected IDs, and EngineeringContext could
    retain the previous active_tool and selected_ids. Editor-context
    construction reads the shared selection, so IDs from the prior surface
    could leak into a newly activated workspace.

Correction:
    After successful WorkspaceController.activate(), clear the shared UI
    selection and publish the new discipline, that surface's active tool,
    and an empty selected_ids tuple to EngineeringContextStore. A failed
    workspace activation does not clear selection or publish the new context.

Architecture:
    UI-only interaction/presentation state. No Qt or SLD object is passed
    into Core, and no electrical/domain truth is moved out of Core.

Verification:
    Source-level call-path audit only. No tests, CI, GUI runtime, Python
    syntax compilation, or import checks were run. Confirm the
    active_tool_id_for(surface_id) contract and runtime action-state
    behavior during the remaining integration review.

======================================================================
COMPATIBILITY SURFACE SELECTION ISOLATION
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Branch:
    fix/workspace-transition-rollback-recovery

Files:
    main.py

Finding:
    Topology and Map activation uses the compatibility-surface path
    outside WorkspaceController. That path changed discipline and cleared
    active_tool, but retained shared SLD selection and EngineeringContext
    selected_ids. This allowed SLD selection state to remain visible in
    context while a non-SLD surface was active.

Correction:
    After successful compatibility-surface activation, clear the shared
    UI selection and publish the surface discipline, active_tool=None,
    and selected_ids=(). Failed activation does not mutate selection or
    publish the new engineering context.

Architecture:
    UI-only transient interaction/presentation state. Core remains the
    authority for electrical/domain truth; no Qt/SLD objects cross into Core.

Verification:
    Source-level call-path audit only. No tests, CI, GUI runtime, Python
    syntax compilation, or import checks were run.

======================================================================
ACTIVE-DISCIPLINE GUARD FOR SLD SELECTION AND TOOL EVENTS
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Branch:
    fix/workspace-transition-rollback-recovery

Files:
    main.py

Finding:
    The composition root forwarded every SelectionManager selection event
    and every Controller tool_changed event directly into the shared
    EngineeringContextStore. Both sources represent SLD-only state. If
    a hidden SLD canvas emitted a late selection/tool event while Control,
    Protection, Study, Topology, or Map was active, it could overwrite the
    active editor's selected_ids or active_tool context.

Correction:
    Route these signals through named handlers. Selection changes update
    EngineeringContext and the workspace selection context only while the
    active discipline is SLD. Controller tool changes update active_tool
    only while SLD is active; tool shelves are still refreshed for UI
    synchronization.

Architecture:
    Presentation event routing only. No domain truth moved into UI and no
    Qt/SLD objects cross the Core boundary.

Verification:
    Source-level audit and GitHub content update only. No tests, CI, GUI
    runtime, Python syntax compilation, or import checks were run.

2026-10-29 — WORKSPACE ROLLBACK EXCEPTION BOUNDARY CORRECTION
======================================================================

Scope:
    ui/workspace/project_workspace.py
    ProjectWorkspaceLifecycle._restore_transition_snapshot()

Finding:
    The best-effort rollback helper caught BaseException, which also captures
    process-control exceptions such as KeyboardInterrupt and SystemExit and
    converts them into ordinary restoration diagnostics.

Correction:
    Catch Exception for ordinary restoration failures. Process-control
    exceptions are no longer intentionally swallowed or normalized by the
    rollback helper.

Architecture:
    UI lifecycle recovery only. No Core/domain ownership changes.

Verification boundary:
    Source edit committed to fix/workspace-transition-rollback-recovery.
    No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, or GUI runtime were run.

======================================================================
2026-10-30 — WORKSPACE EDITOR-CONTEXT SELECTION ISOLATION CORRECTION
======================================================================

Scope:
    main.py
    _editor_context_factory()

Finding:
    Editor-context construction read selected IDs directly from the shared
    SLD SelectionManager for every editor discipline. The event-routing
    guards added to the workspace activation path did not protect this
    independent read path. A Control, Protection, Study, Topology, or Map
    editor context could therefore observe IDs belonging to a hidden SLD
    canvas.

Correction:
    Read shared SelectionManager IDs only when editor_type is "sld".
    Supply an empty selected_ids tuple to all non-SLD editor contexts.
    The same discipline-scoped tuple is used by EngineeringContext and
    EditorContext.selection_context, preventing divergent selection views.

Architecture:
    UI-only selection projection. The shared SelectionManager remains the
    SLD selection authority; non-SLD contexts receive no SLD selection.
    No Core/domain authority or mutation boundary changed.

Verification:
    Static source inspection confirms the read is guarded by editor_type.
    Python syntax/import and workflow verification are pending after this
    branch update. No tests or CI were run by this correction pass.

======================================================================
GF-PROT-ARCH-001
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-30

Scope:
    Unified Protection Study and complete Protection System Engineering
    architecture for GridForge V2.

Status:
    ARCHITECTURE BASELINE ESTABLISHED — PHASED IMPLEMENTATION REQUIRED.
    NOT A PRODUCTION-READINESS DECLARATION.

Decision:
    One Application-owned Protection Study workflow shall configure,
    validate, calculate, coordinate, evaluate, report and preserve
    provenance for the complete project protection system.

    Protection Study is the engineering workflow and result lifecycle.
    Protection System is the configured protection arrangement and
    runtime orchestration. They are related but are not interchangeable.

    Protection remains a distinct subsystem integrated through explicit
    contracts with Core model/network, measurement, switching/control,
    Application study lifecycle, persistence and UI projections.

Frozen boundaries:
    - Core owns electrical/protection domain truth and calculations.
    - Application owns Study lifecycle, configuration orchestration,
      transactions, commands, provenance, events and result assembly.
    - UI/SLD owns authoring interaction and read-only projection.
    - Measurement channels and protection trip/signalling outputs are
      separate paths.
    - ProtectionElement does not own a universal ProtectionZone.
    - Differential coverage is derived from configured CT boundaries;
      other function coverage remains function-specific.
    - Protection decisions do not directly mutate breaker state.
    - No inferred feeder, relay, zone, input or trip target from SLD
      geometry, proximity, ordering or arbitrary Bus-pair matching.

Existing canonical contracts to reuse:
    RelayBase, RelayInput, ProtectionElement, ProtectionDecision,
    ProtectionSystem, ProtectionMeasurementBinding,
    ProtectionProjectConfiguration, ProtectionConfigurationService,
    ProtectionExecution, ProtectionOutputService, MeasurementChannel,
    MeasurementProvisioning, StudyRequest, StudyCaseDefinition,
    StudyExecutionContext and StudyPreparationService.

Implementation phases:
    A. Audit and reconcile existing contracts and function status.
    B. Integrate Protection Study into the existing Study lifecycle.
    C. Prepare and calculate explicit fault scenarios using the existing
       Short-Circuit solver and detached project/topology snapshots.
    D. Complete instrument and measurement association, including
       scenario-derived secondary measurement inputs.
    E. Complete protection scheme, output mapping and breaker-failure path.
    F. Complete protection algorithms, IDMT/TCC and coordination.
    G. Complete typed results, reporting and UI projections.
    H. Static acceptance, register update and scoped freeze.

Research references:
    IEC 60255-1:2022; IEC 60255-151:2009; IEC 60255-121:2014;
    IEC 60255-187-1:2021; IEC 61869-1:2023; IEC TS 60255-216-1:2025;
    IEC 61850-7-4; IEC 62271-100:2021+AMD1:2024; IEEE C37.112-2018;
    applicable IEEE C37 protection application guides.

Verification:
    Architecture and repository source inspection only.
    No tests or CI run or required in this phase.
    Protection functions and complete Study workflow remain unverified
    until their implementation phases and engineering reviews are complete.

      evaluation from incomplete contribution data.

GF-PROT-ARCH-001 — PHASE D CONTRACT AUDIT
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-30

Finding:
    The existing MeasurementGeneration short-circuit path rejects arbitrary
    branch current mapping and only accepts fault-Bus quantities. The
    ProtectionMeasurementBinding contract is not integrated into
    ProtectionProjectConfiguration persistence and identifies RelayInput
    only by its local input name, not by the consuming protection element.
    The open CT association work resolves primary Bus locations but does not
    establish a branch/protection-zone inference, as required by architecture.

Required correction order:
    1. Scope each measurement binding to a protection element and input.
    2. Persist the binding collection in the existing project protection
       configuration contract.
    3. Validate explicit source branch terminal, CT P1/P2 association,
       secondary channel, and protection input mapping.
    4. Implement branch-current per-unit to primary-A to CT-secondary-A
       conversion using the selected branch side's Bus voltage base.
    5. Feed detached scenario samples through Application's sealed
       per-element/input snapshot path; never mutate live channels.
    6. Evaluate protection decisions as non-actuating study outputs.

No branch-current conversion or scenario-derived relay evaluation is
claimed. The audit is recorded as a blocking Phase D implementation scope.

Changed files:
    docs/architecture/PROTECTION_STUDY_AND_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md
    Modification_Register.txt

Verification:
    Remote source/contract inspection only. No tests or CI run.
    Python syntax/import execution remains outstanding.

======================================================================
GF-PROT-ARCH-001 — CT INLINE SIMPLEWIRE ASSOCIATION AND PT/CVT PRIMARY PLACEMENT
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-30

Architecture clarification:
    - CT is placed inline on a SimpleWire connection. P1 and P2 are on
      separate primary SimpleWire topology components; S1/S2 remain isolated
      measurement-secondary terminals.
    - The engineer explicitly associates the CT with the monitored Branch
      and FROM/TO terminal side. A SimpleWire segment is not itself treated
      as an analysis Branch.
    - PT/CVT primary terminals connect at the intended voltage measurement
      point, using the applicable SimpleLine/line connection or direct Bus
      attachment. The graphical SimpleLine is not electrical truth; canonical
      Core endpoint connectivity remains authoritative.
    - PT/CVT secondary terminals remain measurement interfaces, not primary
      power-network Bus attachments.

Implementation correction:
    ProtectionConfigurationService now validates CT insertion using
    ConnectivityResolver.terminal_component rather than requiring both CT
    primary terminals to resolve to Bus IDs. Exactly one CT primary terminal
    must share the explicitly selected Branch terminal's SimpleWire
    component, and the CT P1/P2 components must remain distinct. This
    supports inline CT placement on split SimpleWire segments and avoids
    confusing a wire segment with the monitored Branch.

Changed files:
    core/application/services/protection_configuration_service.py
    docs/architecture/PROTECTION_STUDY_AND_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md
    Modification_Register.txt

Verification:
    Remote source/contract inspection and static review only.
    No tests or CI run. Python syntax/import execution remains outstanding.

GF-PROT-ARCH-001 — BUS-CAPABLE PT/CVT VOLTAGE SOURCE ASSOCIATION
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-30

Implementation:
    ProtectionMeasurementBinding now uses the canonical
    source_endpoint_reference and accepts either:
      - a physical terminal EndpointReference; or
      - a canonical Bus attachment EndpointReference.
    Deserialization remains backward-compatible with persisted
    source_terminal_reference mappings. New persistence writes
    source_endpoint_reference.

    ProtectionConfigurationService validates voltage measurement sources
    against PT primary_a/primary_b or CVT H1/H2 topology components.
    Exactly one primary terminal must share the selected source endpoint's
    topology component, while the two primary terminals must remain on
    distinct components. This supports direct Bus attachment and explicit
    canonical line-terminal associations.

    CT current measurement remains terminal-only and Branch-specific.
    Bus references cannot be substituted for the explicit monitored Branch
    terminal. The SLD SimpleLine projection must resolve to a canonical Core
    line terminal; graphics are not accepted as electrical truth.

    core/protection/protection_measurement_binding.py

[2026-10-30] Protection Study Phase D1 — explicit CT branch-current conversion
- Extended ShortCircuitInput with immutable study MVA base and per-Bus nominal-voltage-base metadata.
- ShortCircuitPreparation captures available positive nominal voltage bases from the detached study network; missing bases remain unavailable rather than being guessed.
- ShortCircuitSolver preserves the per-unit current-unit declaration and exact base metadata in ShortCircuitResult provenance.
- MeasurementGeneration now exposes a pure branch-current-to-CT-secondary conversion. It requires a complete short-circuit contribution set, an explicit branch ID, an explicit from/to measurement side, a phase/sequence quantity, and an explicit +1/-1 CT-reference direction supplied by the committed CT association resolver.
- The conversion applies the selected-side current base, explicit CT direction, CT polarity, and CT ratio once. It does not mutate MeasurementChannel.
- Boundary: scenario-specific RelayInput snapshot construction and ProtectionSystem evaluation remain unimplemented. No trip/action dispatch is introduced.
- Verification: source-level review only; no tests or CI run, per repository workflow.

[2026-10-30] D1 refinement — phase identity and direction contract
- Tightened CT branch conversion to require an explicit phase or sequence member; the aggregate branch.current alias is not accepted because it is phase-A-only in the current solver result contract.
- Tightened branch_current_direction to an actual integer +1/-1. The value must come from the committed CT association's explicit P1/P2 orientation; conversion does not derive it.

[2026-10-30] Protection Study Phase D2 — CT association-to-branch orientation
- Integrated the canonical Core CTMeasurementAssociation and Application CTMeasurementAssociationService into the Protection Study branch, including package exports.
- Added CTMeasurementAssociation.branch_current_direction(from_bus_id, to_bus_id). It returns +1 only for the exact P1-Bus -> P2-Bus branch orientation, -1 only for the exact reverse orientation, and rejects any branch that does not match the configured primary measurement boundary.
- MeasurementGeneration.convert_short_circuit_branch_current now requires the resolved CTMeasurementAssociation rather than a caller-supplied sign. It verifies CT identity, in-service state, topology revision, configured P1/P2 terminal-to-Bus identity, and exact branch endpoint pairing before conversion.
- Branch-reference direction and physical CT polarity remain separate: the association determines branch orientation; the physical CT polarity is applied once by the existing CT polarity conversion.
- This integrates the resolver contract into the conversion boundary. The ProtectionStudyHandler still does not construct detached scenario RelayInput snapshots or evaluate fault-scenario ProtectionDecisions; that remains the next phase.
- No tests or CI were run. No syntax/import execution was performed.

[2026-10-30] Protection Study Phase D2 topology correction — inline CT placement
- Reconciled the initial D2 Bus-pair assumption against the frozen inline CT placement contract. CT P1/P2 do not need to resolve to Bus IDs; they resolve to separate canonical SimpleWire topology components.
- CTMeasurementAssociation now retains immutable P1/P2 topology components, validates each component contains its canonical CT terminal, rejects overlapping components and Junction traversal, and requires committed SimpleWire connectivity.
- The configured Branch FROM/TO terminal is explicitly supplied from ProtectionMeasurementBinding. Branch-current sign is derived from whether that exact terminal belongs to the CT P1 or P2 component and whether the CT is on the Branch FROM or TO side.
- The conversion still validates the scenario's selected-side Bus base using ShortCircuitBranchCurrent.from_bus_id/to_bus_id. This Bus is used for per-unit scaling only; it is not used to infer CT placement.
- CT polarity remains a separate physical conversion and is cross-checked against the resolved association snapshot before being applied once.
- Updated PROTECTION_STUDY_AND_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md to remove the obsolete requirement that CT P1/P2 each resolve to distinct Bus IDs.
- Scenario-specific detached RelayInput samples and ProtectionSystem evaluation remain outstanding.
- No tests or CI were run. No syntax/import execution was performed.

[2026-10-30] D2 secondary-channel provenance guard
- CT branch-current scenario conversion now verifies that the MeasurementChannel's physical source identity matches the configured CT and that its source_terminal is exactly the resolved CT S1 or S2 terminal.
- This keeps the primary P1/P2 SimpleWire orientation contract separate from the secondary S1/S2 measurement circuit contract.
- No MeasurementChannel values or live sample metadata are modified.

[2026-10-30] Protection Study Phase D2 — topology-independent CT measurement endpoint
- Extended CTMeasurementAssociation with locate_measurement_endpoint(EndpointReference), returning P1_SIDE or P2_SIDE for an explicitly selected canonical equipment terminal or Bus attachment. This is physical measurement-location information only; it does not infer a protected feeder, zone, or differential scheme.
- Extended ProtectionConfigurationService current-binding validation to accept three explicit placement patterns: Branch FROM/TO terminal, non-Branch equipment terminal (including transformer, motor and load terminals), and Bus attachment. Each selected endpoint must belong to exactly one CT P1/P2 SimpleWire topology component; P1 and P2 must remain on distinct components.
- Branch current bindings retain explicit FROM/TO validation. Equipment terminal bindings declare electrical_side='equipment' and preserve the exact terminal role in EndpointReference. Bus-side bindings declare electrical_side='bus' and preserve the canonical Bus attachment identity.
- The existing numerical short-circuit conversion remains Branch-contribution-specific. Equipment-terminal and Bus-side scenario-current source adapters, differential scheme membership, detached RelayInput snapshots and non-actuating ProtectionSystem evaluation remain pending.
- Updated the Protection Study architecture document to distinguish CT physical placement, selected measurement endpoint and protection scheme membership.
- No tests or CI were run. Python syntax/import/runtime execution remains unverified.

[2026-10-30] Protection Study — explicit differential scheme membership configuration
- Added immutable DifferentialProtectionSchemeConfiguration as a project-scoped engineering configuration separate from CT placement and ProtectionElement identity.
- Scheme configuration identifies the protected Core object type/ID and at least two distinct explicitly selected current-measurement binding IDs. Optional per-binding phase-compensation degrees are persisted as declared configuration only; no numerical compensation is claimed.
- ProtectionProjectConfiguration now owns, validates, serializes and restores differential scheme configurations. It rejects missing/non-current member bindings and prevents removal or conversion of a referenced member binding to a non-current measurement.
- Added transactional Application create/delete operations. If a Core Network provider is available, scheme creation verifies the protected object exists and its Core type matches the declared target.
- Exported DifferentialProtectionSchemeConfiguration from core.protection.
- No CT placement pattern automatically creates a scheme; Bus-to-Bus CT placement alone does not declare Bus differential protection.
- Differential current/restraint calculations, transformer vector-group and ratio compensation, phase/zero-sequence compensation execution, restraint/stability logic, decisions and trip mapping remain pending.
- No tests or CI were run. Python syntax/import/runtime execution remains unverified.

[2026-10-30] Differential scheme boundary direction contract
- Strengthened DifferentialProtectionSchemeConfiguration so every member CT measurement binding must declare exactly one current direction: INTO_ZONE or OUT_OF_ZONE.
- Direction is scheme-specific engineering configuration and is never inferred from CT P1/P2 physical orientation, Bus attachment, or SimpleWire topology.
- The explicit direction mapping is immutable and included in project persistence. Differential phasor calculation remains pending.


[2026-10-30] Protection Study Phase E2 — pure differential current kernel
- Added headless Core differential numerical contracts: explicit settings,
  detached three-phase complex RMS current samples, per-phase results and
  immutable aggregate result.
- The kernel uses the scheme's persisted INTO_ZONE/OUT_OF_ZONE directions
  for signed phasor summation. It requires all member CT samples, exact
  binding identity, a shared explicit reference-basis ID, finite A/B/C
  phasors and a configured restraint definition.
- Added selectable half-sum, sum and maximum-magnitude restraint methods;
  continuous piecewise-linear multi-slope bias characteristic; and optional
  independent high-set pickup.
- Non-zero phase compensation is rejected until an equipment-specific
  compensation adapter exists. The kernel does not silently ignore stored
  compensation settings.
- Results are immutable and non-actuating. No ProtectionDecision, trip/action
  mapping or breaker operation is performed.
- Transformer vector-group/ratio compensation, CT conversion/normalisation,
  zero-sequence treatment, inrush and CT-saturation supervision, timing and
  end-to-end ProtectionStudy sample integration remain pending.
- No tests or CI were run. Python syntax/import/runtime execution remains
  unverified through the remote repository connector.


[2026-10-30] Protection Study Phase E3 — persisted differential characteristics
- Extended DifferentialProtectionSchemeConfiguration with optional immutable,
  validated differential_settings persisted in project configuration.
- Persisted settings include pickup current, explicit restraint method,
  piecewise bias slopes and restraint breakpoints, plus optional independent
  high-set pickup. Invalid keys, non-finite/negative values, invalid slope
  counts and unordered breakpoints are rejected at configuration creation/load.
- DifferentialProtectionSettings now serializes/deserializes the typed engine
  settings. The pure Core kernel can consume an explicitly supplied settings
  object or reconstruct it from the scheme's persisted settings.
- Existing scheme constructor positional field order is preserved.
- Settings persistence does not imply scenario integration: Protection Study
  still does not generate detached CT phasors from short-circuit contributions.
  No relay operation, ProtectionDecision, trip mapping or breaker action is
  claimed.
- No tests or CI were run. Python syntax/import/runtime execution remains
  unverified through the remote repository connector.


[2026-10-30] Protection Study Phase D4 — detached Branch CT scenario samples
- Added ProtectionScenarioMeasurementAdapter and immutable
  ProtectionScenarioMeasurementSample for explicitly configured Branch
  FROM/TO CT bindings.
- The adapter consumes complete complex short-circuit phase/sequence branch
  contributions, validates exact Branch terminal side, CT primary topology
  association, instrument/channel identity, physical CT ratio and sample
  member identity, then reuses the canonical per-unit/base/orientation/
  polarity/ratio conversion path.
- Emits detached CT-secondary ampere samples with scenario and source
  provenance. It never mutates or refreshes live MeasurementChannel values.
- Each sample carries an instrument/channel-specific current basis identity.
  These CT-secondary samples are intentionally not accepted as a shared
  differential primary-current basis; explicit CT normalisation and
  equipment-specific compensation remain required.
- Bus/equipment-terminal current adapters, PT/CVT fault-voltage conversion,
  RelayInput snapshot assembly, ProtectionSystem scenario evaluation and
  result aggregation remain pending.
- No tests or CI were run. Python syntax/import/runtime execution remains
  unverified through the remote repository connector.


[2026-10-30] CT/PT/CVT secondary measurement-channel enforcement — reconciled from PR #905
- MeasurementProvisioning accepts CT/PT/CVT measurement channels only from their physical secondary terminals: CT S1/S2, PT secondary_a/secondary_b, and CVT X1/X2.
- MeasurementGeneration verifies that the supplied MeasurementChannel belongs to the same physical instrument and identifies one of its configured secondary terminals before applying primary-to-secondary conversion.
- Applied validation to Power Flow current/voltage generation, short-circuit current generation, and detached Branch CT scenario conversion.
- The primary-side PreparedMeasurementContext remains distinct from the secondary-side MeasurementChannel source terminal.
- This correction does not infer CT measurement location, protected equipment, protection zone, or scheme membership.
- No tests or CI were run. Python syntax/import/runtime execution remains unverified.


[2026-10-30] Protection Study Phase D5 — detached RelayInput scenario evaluation
- Added the internal Application._evaluate_protection_scenario() boundary as the sole authority for
  validating scenario samples and installing immutable RelayInput evaluation
  snapshots. It validates active project/runtime/channel identities,
  ProtectionMeasurementBinding correlation, CT identity/ratio/secondary
  terminal, phase/sequence, canonical engineering units, channel engineering
  scale/polarity, input representation and project/activation/topology provenance.
- Scenario snapshots use no fabricated acquisition timestamp and do not
  modify MeasurementChannel state. MeasurementQuality.UNKNOWN is retained
  because calculated study quantities are not sensor-quality telemetry;
  MeasurementValidity.VALID is assigned only after complete result and
  provenance validation.
- ProtectionStudyHandler now resolves CT associations through the
  Application CTMeasurementAssociationService, converts explicitly bound
  Branch FROM/TO CT phase/sequence contributions, and evaluates eligible
  protection elements without dispatching output actions.
- ProtectionFaultScenarioResult retains its own ProtectionExecutionResult;
  ProtectionStudyResult exposes scenario-attributed decisions/diagnostics.
- Scope remains Branch FROM/TO CT current bindings with supported input
  contracts. Bus/equipment-terminal current adapters, PT/CVT voltage,
  ANSI 67 scenario polarising snapshots, residual-current semantics,
  differential CT normalisation/compensation and action dispatch remain
  unsupported and fail closed.
- CT scenario sample unit is canonical A; CT-secondary basis remains
  instrument/channel-specific and is not a common differential basis.
- No tests or CI were run. Python syntax/import/runtime execution remains
  unverified through the remote repository connector.


[2026-10-31] Protection Study Phase D5 — canonical CT authority and no-operate result status
- Strengthened Application scenario validation to require that each bound
  MeasurementChannel references the exact active canonical CT object, and
  that the active CT ratio and primary polarity still match the detached
  scenario sample and persisted binding. The detached sample now records the
  CT association's positive-current direction for this cross-snapshot check.
- Added an explicit relay_evaluation_completed result contract per fault
  scenario. A completed evaluation with no ProtectionDecision is now reported
  as evaluated (no operation), rather than being confused with a scenario
  for which relay evaluation never ran.
- Reconciled the D4 architecture wording: samples use canonical unit A while
  retaining an instrument/channel-specific CT-secondary reference basis.
- Verification: source-level review only. No tests or CI run, per repository
  workflow. Python syntax/import/runtime checks remain outstanding.


[2026-10-31] Protection Study Phase D6 — signed equipment terminal-current foundation
- Added immutable ShortCircuitTerminalCurrent records keyed by equipment ID and terminal role. Each record carries its canonical Bus identity, complex phase/sequence currents, per-unit declaration, and an explicit positive-into-equipment reference direction.
- ShortCircuitPreparation preserves physical FROM/TO terminal roles in the detached sequence branch snapshot.
- ShortCircuitSolver derives independent FROM and TO terminal currents from solved sequence voltages and the prepared series impedance. The TO terminal current is the negative of the FROM-oriented series current under the declared into-equipment convention.
- Added independent terminal_current_status and terminal_current_diagnostics to ShortCircuitResult. COMPLETE requires both terminal records for every prepared branch; unsupported equipment is reported as PARTIAL/UNAVAILABLE and is never replaced by Branch aggregate current.
- At the D6 stage, terminal-current output supported only series-only Line/Cable primitives. This historical limitation was superseded by D7 tap-aware transformer sequence stamping and D8 balanced three-phase transformer terminal-current equations. Unbalanced transformer sequence paths remain unsupported. Shunt/mutual models, machine stator terminals, load contributions and Bus-boundary currents remain outstanding.
- Protection CT scenario adapters do not consume the new records yet; no unsupported CT path is enabled by this change.
- Verification: remote source-level review only. No tests or CI run, per repository workflow. Python syntax/import/runtime checks remain outstanding.


[2026-10-31] Protection Study Phase D7 — transformer tap-aware sequence primitive
- Corrected canonical short-circuit sequence-network preparation to stamp a two-terminal transformer with the explicit Core from-side complex tap a = tap * exp(j * shift), using the standard four Ybus primitive coefficients. Line/Cable and single-Bus source stamps retain unity-tap behaviour.
- Extended detached SequenceBranchSnapshot to retain validated tap_ratio and phase_shift_rad; ShortCircuitPreparation copies these values from the canonical Core Transformer.
- Scope is deliberately limited to the scalar Core tap and phase-shift model. Optional VectorGroup plugin clock displacement, winding connection mapping, zero-sequence grounding, unbalanced sequence coupling, magnetising/shunt admittance and tertiary windings are not applied. D8 subsequently adds balanced three-phase transformer terminal-current equations using this same stamped primitive.
- Verification: remote source review only. No tests or CI run, per repository workflow. Python syntax/import/runtime checks remain outstanding.


[2026-10-31] Protection Study Phase D8 — tap-aware transformer terminal currents
- Extended the detached short-circuit solver to calculate transformer FROM/TO terminal currents from the same complex-tap two-port primitive used by canonical Ybus preparation.
- Both terminal currents are positive into the transformer at their respective physical terminals; the Branch contribution retains FROM-terminal orientation. Records preserve terminal role and Bus identity.
- Transformer terminal-current output is restricted to balanced three-phase faults with the positive-sequence primitive. Unbalanced transformer currents remain unavailable because winding connection/vector-group and neutral-grounding sequence paths are not modeled. Magnetising/shunt and tertiary-winding currents remain unsupported.
- External SequenceNetwork inputs are rejected for tapped/phase-shifting transformers unless they carry the canonical FROM_SIDE_COMPLEX_TAP_V1 convention marker; this prevents terminal-current equations from consuming a matrix stamped under an unknown tap convention.
- No tests or CI were run. Remote source-level review only; Python syntax/import/runtime checks remain outstanding.


[2026-10-31] Protection Study Phase D9 — configured equipment-terminal CT measurement adapter
- Added MeasurementGeneration conversion from ShortCircuitTerminalCurrent records to CT secondary amperes. Conversion verifies result/terminal availability, explicit endpoint identity and role, Bus identity, INTO_EQUIPMENT reference direction, per-unit current basis, topology revision, canonical CT association, physical CT polarity, secondary MeasurementChannel source/terminal, and configured CT ratio.
- ProtectionScenarioMeasurementAdapter now creates detached samples from the exact endpoint persisted in ProtectionMeasurementBinding; ProtectionStudyHandler routes scenario CT bindings through this terminal-current adapter rather than requiring Branch FROM/TO contribution records.
- Current sign is derived from the selected endpoint's resolved CT P1/P2 SimpleWire component and the terminal record's positive-into-equipment convention: P1-side reverses the terminal sign relative to P1-to-P2; P2-side retains it. The configured CT polarity is then applied. No protected zone or measurement target is inferred.
- PARTIAL terminal-current sets may provide an individual sample only when its exact terminal record is present and valid. FAILED/UNAVAILABLE sets and missing records are refused. Live MeasurementChannel state remains unchanged.
- Supported result records are limited to Line/Cable and balanced three-phase Transformer series primitives. Bus attachments, PT/CVT voltage, unbalanced Transformer sequence currents and unsupported equipment remain unavailable.
- No tests or CI were run. Remote source-level review only; Python syntax/import/runtime checks remain outstanding.


[2026-10-31] Protection Study Phase D10 — explicit residual-current input contract
- Added ResidualCurrentInputConfiguration with mutually exclusive
  THREE_PHASE_SUM, NEUTRAL_CT and CBCT modes. The model requires explicit
  measurement-point identity and either exactly three distinct A/B/C binding
  IDs or one dedicated direct binding ID.
- Persisted the typed residual-current configuration on
  ProtectionFunctionConfiguration and restricted its use to ANSI 50N/51N.
  Added detached serialization/deserialization and public Core exports.
- Added a fail-closed residual-current resolver. Three-phase mode uses complex
  phasor addition (Ia + Ib + Ic), not magnitude addition, and requires matching
  scenario, measurement point, topology revision, canonical ampere unit and
  identical CT-secondary reference basis. Direct neutral/CBCT modes require
  explicit N/RESIDUAL phase classification and matching residual source-mode
  provenance.
- This is the Core configuration/resolver foundation only. Application
  snapshot integration, measurement-point and source-mode provenance
  population, unequal CT ratio normalization to a common primary-current
  basis, and 50N/51N contract enablement remain outstanding. Existing 50N/51N
  input contracts remain fail-closed. No zone inference is introduced.
- No tests or CI were run. Remote source review only; syntax/import/runtime
  checks remain outstanding.


[2026-10-31] Protection Study Phase D11a — common primary-current residual basis
- Added immutable ResidualCurrentSourceSample and normalize_residual_current_source().
- The normalization boundary converts a validated CT-secondary sample to signed primary amperes by applying channel scale, channel polarity and CT ratio exactly once. CT P1/P2 orientation and physical CT polarity remain the measurement adapter's responsibility and are not repeated.
- Residual phasor resolution now requires PRIMARY_AMPS basis and explicit normalization provenance. Unequal CT ratios can be combined only after independent primary-current normalization.
- The engineer-configured measurement_point_id is supplied explicitly and retained in provenance; no measurement point or protection zone is inferred.
- Application multi-binding snapshot assembly, direct neutral/CBCT source-mode population and 50N/51N input-contract enablement remain outstanding. 50N/51N remain fail-closed. No tests or CI were run; remote source review only, syntax/import/runtime checks outstanding.

GF-PROT-D11C-01 — END-TO-END ANSI 50N/51N RESIDUAL INPUT
======================================================================

Files:
    core/protection/relay_input.py
    core/protection/runtime.py
    core/protection/input_contracts.py
    core/protection/protection_element.py
    core/protection/project_configuration.py
    core/measurement/protection_scenario_measurement.py
    core/application/application.py
    core/application/protection_study.py
    core/protection/__init__.py
    docs/architecture/PROTECTION_STUDY_AND_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md

Implementation:
    - Added a typed DerivedRelayMeasurement source. It is not a
      MeasurementChannel and is never registered in the physical channel
      service.
    - Runtime composes residual_current for 50N/51N only when an explicit
      ResidualCurrentInputConfiguration exists.
    - Enabled canonical complex-phasor CURRENT/A input contracts for 50N
      and 51N, with source-set validation against persisted A/B/C or
      direct neutral/CBCT binding identities.
    - Application validates each detached A/B/C source against the active
      project, topology revision, CT/channel identities, CT ratio and
      polarity, terminal current direction/units, and explicit measurement
      point. Phase labels are mapped to solver Ia/Ib/Ic result members.
    - Each CT-secondary sample is normalized once to signed primary
      amperes using its own CT ratio and channel scale/polarity. The
      resolver computes the complex phasor sum for THREE_PHASE_SUM.
    - Application installs a detached derived residual snapshot with
      source binding IDs and provenance. ProtectionElement verifies the
      derived source identity and provenance before RelayInput evaluation.
    - ProtectionStudyHandler composes a fresh ProtectionRuntime per
      fault scenario, isolating ANSI 51N pickup timing/latching state and
      preserving the active live protection runtime.
    - Existing 50N instantaneous pickup and 51N IEC inverse-time logic
      consume the residual phasor without modifying physical channels.
      Pickup is in primary amperes and the operating quantity is
      3I0 = Ia + Ib + Ic, not I0 alone.

Configuration contract:
    Every THREE_PHASE_SUM source binding declares
    metadata.measurement_point_id equal to the function's configured
    measurement point. A/B/C summation requires three distinct CT
    instruments and phase-specific current channels. NEUTRAL_CT and CBCT
    remain fail-closed because the current solver has no neutral-conductor
    or multi-conductor CBCT primary-window terminal-current primitive.

Boundary:
    This enables the short-circuit scenario path only for source types
    represented by the existing terminal-current solver records and
    supported CT association adapter. It does not claim general live
    telemetry integration, transformer vector-group compensation, or
    unsupported/unbalanced terminal-current coverage. Direct neutral/CBCT
    identity is an explicit persisted instrument-mode declaration; the
    current CT domain/solver model does not yet represent a neutral
    conductor or a multi-conductor CBCT primary window. Those modes must
    not be treated as operational merely because their configuration
    contracts are persisted.

Verification:
    Remote source updates committed. No tests or CI run. Python syntax,
    import integrity and end-to-end execution have not yet been verified
    in a local runtime.

GF-SC-D14G-01 — COMPONENT-AWARE SEQUENCE-NETWORK SOLVABILITY
======================================================================

Files:
    core/analysis/sequence_network_preparation.py
    core/analysis/short_circuit_preparation.py
    core/solver/short_circuit/sequence_snapshot.py
    core/solver/short_circuit/unsymmetrical_fault.py
    core/solver/short_circuit/short_circuit_solver.py
    docs/architecture/PROTECTION_STUDY_AND_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md
    Modification_Register.txt

Implementation:
    - Builds sequence-conducting connected components independently for
      positive, negative and zero sequence. A branch joins two Buses only
      when its requested sequence impedance is explicitly available.
    - Treats source impedances and explicit grounding shunts as component
      anchors. Each component is inverted independently when its Ybus block
      is nonsingular.
    - Retains floating/singular components as unavailable Bus-index sets;
      no pseudo-inverse, artificial ground, or invented impedance is used.
    - Carries unavailable-component metadata into the immutable sequence
      snapshot and validates its sequence names and Bus-index bounds.
    - Rejects a fault only if the fault Bus lacks a required sequence:
      LL uses positive/negative; LG and LLG require positive/negative/zero.
    - Suppresses contribution and terminal-current records for equipment
      whose required sequence endpoints are in unavailable components.
      Existing contribution coverage reports those omissions as partial.

Boundary:
    A transformer without a supported zero-sequence transfer primitive
    does not connect its HV/LV Buses in Z0. A one-sided grounded-wye shunt
    grounds only its configured Bus. Grounded-wye/grounded-wye transfer,
    transformer Z0 terminal currents and earth-fault relay measurement are
    still not implemented.

Verification:
    Remote source review and SHA verification only. No tests/CI, Python
    syntax/import checks, numerical component fixtures or solver execution
    were run. Numerical correctness remains unverified at runtime.

GF-SC-D14I-01 — GROUNDED-WYE / GROUNDED-WYE THREE-TEST T-EQUIVALENT FOUNDATION

      bases, non-empty test source reference, explicit LV-to-HV referral,

      checks verify all three test impedances are reproduced.
    - Does not reject a negative fitted individual series-leg resistance;

    - Exported the Core test-data contract and Analysis equivalent builder.

    handles the value; no persistence round-trip was run. The classifier

    connection, immutable solver snapshot, or two-terminal transformer

    remains unavailable in SequenceNetworkPreparation. D14H one-sided shunt
    behavior is unchanged.

GF-SC-D14I-02 — GROUNDED-WYE / GROUNDED-WYE TWO-BUS ZERO-SEQUENCE SOLVER PATH

Implementation:
    - Added a reduced two-Bus zero-sequence admittance stamp from the
      three-test-derived transformer T equivalent.
    - Adds HV 3Zn to the HV T leg and LV 3Zn to the LV T leg exactly once;
      LV neutral impedance is referred to HV using (V_H/V_L)^2.
    - Requires canonical HV/LV terminal roles, distinct Bus attachments,
      explicit study MVA base, and Bus nominal voltage bases matching the
      declared test winding voltage bases.
    - Accepts only Yy0 with normal HV/LV winding polarity; unsupported
      zero-sequence polarity/clock mappings fail closed.
    - Rejects non-unity tap and non-zero phase shift for this path until a
      validated tap-adjusted zero-sequence test model is provided.
    - Externally supplied sequence matrices must include the matching D14I
      two-Bus stamp; missing explicit transformer zero-sequence provenance
      fails preparation.
    - Coupled stamp participates in zero-sequence component connectivity and
      matrix inversion; unrelated floating islands remain independently
      unavailable.
    - Added immutable SequenceTransformerZeroTwoBusSnapshot provenance with
      test measurements, neutral impedances, voltage/MVA bases, Bus and
      terminal identities, core construction and test-data provenance.
    - Solver calculates both HV and LV terminal I0 from the frozen two-port
      admittance matrix and carries the values into terminal phase-current
      records using the existing INTO_EQUIPMENT convention.

Engineering limits:
    - No vector-group clock/polarity transformation is applied to zero
      sequence; the Yg-Yg equivalent is the explicit measured two-winding
      zero-sequence test model. Only Yy is accepted by this implementation.
    - Non-unity tap/phase shift is rejected. No tap-adjusted Z0 test mapping.
    - No core-construction multiplier or inferred magnetic return path.
    - No support for incomplete test set, missing neutral impedance, Bus/test
      voltage-base mismatch, or missing terminal mapping.

Verification:
    Remote source edits only. No tests/CI, Python syntax/import checks,
    numeric fixture, short-circuit execution, CT adapter execution or
    persistence round-trip were run. Treat as source-integrated but not
    production-verified.

======================================================================

D14I-03 — Transformer terminal CT / residual-current integration hardening
    - Audited the D14I HV/LV transformer terminal-current records through the
      configured CT terminal adapter and ANSI 50N/51N residual-input path.
    - The adapter uses the measured equipment terminal Bus base, preserves
      INTO_EQUIPMENT and sequence-model provenance, applies CT-side orientation
      and physical CT polarity once, then converts primary amperes to CT
      secondary amperes.
    - Application normalizes each configured phase CT sample to signed
      primary amperes and sums the complex A/B/C phasors.
    - Added fail-closed validation that all three A/B/C residual-current
      sources reference the same canonical equipment terminal identity
      (equipment ID plus terminal role), in addition to the existing explicit
      measurement-point, project, topology, CT, phase and ratio checks. This
      prevents a single residual sum from combining currents measured at
      different equipment terminals.
    - No protection zone is inferred and no configured CT endpoint is moved.

Verification:
    Remote source review and GitHub source edit only. No tests/CI, Python
    syntax/import checks, transformer numerical fixture, CT adapter execution,
    or end-to-end ANSI 50N/51N scenario execution were run. Not
    production-verified.

GF-SC-D15E — PHYSICAL CONDUCTOR IMPEDANCE PREPARATION AND BRANCH-LOCAL CURRENT SOLUTION
======================================================================

Files:
    core/analysis/physical_conductor_preparation.py
    core/analysis/sequence_network_preparation.py
    core/analysis/__init__.py
    core/solver/short_circuit/sequence_snapshot.py
    docs/architecture/PROTECTION_STUDY_AND_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md
    Modification_Register.txt

Changes:
    - Added immutable PreparedPhysicalConductorBranch and
      PhysicalConductorImpedanceSnapshot contracts. The snapshot preserves
      conductor IDs, matrix order, conductor roles, branch and Bus identities,
      length, MVA/kV bases, project identity, activation generation, topology
      revision and source reference.
    - Converts the engineer-supplied series impedance matrix from ohm/km to
      total ohms and per-unit using Zbase = kV_LL^2 / MVA. Full mutual
      coupling is retained through matrix inversion.
    - Converts explicit shunt-admittance matrices from S/km to total siemens
      and per-unit. Missing shunt data remains unavailable.
    - Requires matching FROM/TO Bus voltage bases and fresh canonical
      project/activation/topology provenance. Stale inventory, singular
      matrices, missing study bases and unsupported voltage transitions fail
      closed.
    - Adds a branch-local coupled current solver using complete, explicit
      conductor-voltage vectors at both terminals. With explicit shunt data,
      it calculates nominal-pi half-shunt terminal contributions. Without
      shunt data, it returns series currents only and leaves terminal totals
      unavailable.
    - SequenceNetworkPreparation carries the detached physical impedance
      snapshot separately from scalar positive/negative/zero sequence data.
      SequenceNetworkSnapshot freezes and validates topology provenance and
      complete active-branch coverage.

Engineering boundary:
    - No independent conductor nodes, neutral bonds, earth/PE/screen
      terminations, or global conductor-domain Ybus are created.
    - No neutral current is inferred from phase-current summation. No earth,
      screen or PE return path is inferred.
    - Existing sequence fault results do not provide all conductor terminal
      potentials. Therefore no physical conductor-current, neutral-CT or
      CBCT result is synthesized. Global phase-domain node stamping and
      fault-solver integration remain subsequent work. NEUTRAL_CT and CBCT
      remain fail-closed.

Verification:
    Remote source edits only. No tests/CI were run, per repository workflow.
    Python syntax, import integrity, numerical matrix fixtures, persistence
    round-trip and end-to-end execution remain unverified. Source-integrated,
    not production-verified.

GF-SC-D15F-01 — AUTHORITATIVE PHASE-DOMAIN CONDUCTOR GRAPH FOUNDATION
======================================================================

Files:
    core/network/phase_domain_conductor_network.py
    core/network/network.py
    core/network/__init__.py

Changes:
    - Added immutable Core contracts for phase-domain conductor nodes,
      earth-reference nodes, and explicit physical connections.
    - Connection kinds distinguish continuity, bonding, equipment bonding,
      screen bonding, grounding electrodes, and earth-return impedance.
    - Every connection carries an explicit complex impedance in ohms.
      Ideal zero-ohm bonds are permitted only when explicitly configured;
      negative resistance, non-finite values, self-connections, duplicate
      identities, and dangling endpoints are rejected.
    - Ground-electrode edges must terminate at an explicit EARTH_REFERENCE
      node. Screen bonds must include a SHIELD conductor. Equipment bonds
      must include a PE conductor.
    - Added deterministic immutable graph snapshots with project,
      activation-generation, and topology-revision provenance, node lookup,
      adjacency lookup, and explicit earth-reachability queries.
    - Network now owns registered phase-domain nodes and connections.
      Add/remove operations validate dependencies and invalidate derived
      topology. Network.validate rejects dangling phase-domain references.
    - Exported the new Core contracts from core.network.

Engineering boundary:
    This is the authoritative graph foundation, not yet a global numerical
    phase-domain solver. It does not synthesize Bus conductor nodes, infer
    neutral/PE/screen bonds, or treat a reachable earth node as zero
    impedance. The next integration must bind each graph node to the
    canonical branch conductor inventory and terminal topology, assemble
    branch and connection admittances, solve explicit source/fault boundary
    conditions, and populate conductor-current result records. Protection
    direct Neutral CT/CBCT execution remains fail-closed until those solver
    records are available.

Verification:
    Remote GitHub source edits only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks,
    persistence round-trip, or numerical solve were run.

GF-SC-D15F-02 — GLOBAL PHASE-DOMAIN STAMPING AND FAULT SOLVER
======================================================================

Files:
    core/analysis/phase_domain_network_preparation.py
    core/analysis/__init__.py

    core/solver/short_circuit/short_circuit_solver.py
    core/solver/short_circuit/result.py
    core/solver/short_circuit/__init__.py

Changes:
    - Added global nodal admittance assembly for coupled Line/Cable
      conductor impedance matrices, explicit shunt matrices, and declared
      connection impedances. Assembly is in SI siemens and does not assume
      a common per-unit base for grounding/bonding edges.
    - Explicit earth-reference nodes are fixed at zero potential. Explicit
      zero-ohm graph connections are contracted; non-zero connections are
      stamped using their declared complex impedance.
    - Added explicit phase-domain Norton source equivalents and fault
      connections. The solver uses an augmented constraint formulation for
      bolted faults rather than substituting epsilon impedances.
    - Added optional phase-domain execution to ShortCircuitInput and routed
      it through ShortCircuitSolver when a typed PhaseDomainShortCircuitCase
      is supplied. Existing sequence execution remains the default path.
    - Produces physical conductor current records for Line/Cable A/B/C/N,
      PE, screen, pilot and other declared conductor roles. It also exposes
      finite-impedance continuity/bond/grounding connection currents in the
      result values. Ideal zero-ohm bond current division is explicitly
      reported indeterminate rather than fabricated.
    - Added direct physical conductor CT conversion and Neutral CT/CBCT
      window sample assembly. CBCT sums only explicitly configured conductor
      IDs at one equipment terminal, with each declared reference direction.

Engineering boundary:
    Phase-domain cases require explicit graph nodes, branch-terminal to Bus
    conductor continuity, impedance matrices, Norton source equivalents and
    fault connections. No source, earth return, conductor or CBCT membership
    is inferred. Sequence-domain source/equipment contribution records are
    not synthesized by this path. Protection direct-current conversion is
    record-specific; overall conductor-current coverage may remain PARTIAL
    when ideal bond current division is indeterminate.

Verification:
    Remote GitHub source edits only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks,
    numerical fixtures, persistence round-trip or end-to-end protection
    scenario execution were run.

GF-SC-D15F-04 — EXPLICIT LOCAL EARTH AND GLOBAL REFERENCE SEMANTICS
======================================================================

Files:
    core/network/phase_domain_conductor_network.py
    core/solver/short_circuit/phase_domain_solver.py
    core/solver/short_circuit/input.py

Correction:
    - Split EARTH_NODE (a local soil/earth-domain unknown potential) from
      EARTH_REFERENCE (the single global zero-potential datum).
    - A graph may contain at most one EARTH_REFERENCE. Local earth nodes
      remain unknowns in the global nodal matrix and therefore do not become
      equipotential merely because they are both earth-domain nodes.
    - GROUND_ELECTRODE must connect a physical conductor to an explicit
      local EARTH_NODE. EARTH_RETURN must connect a local earth node to
      another earth-domain node, including the one global reference where
      configured. Every edge retains its explicit impedance.
    - Earth reachability is true only when an explicit connection path
      reaches the global reference. An isolated electrode/soil node is not
      treated as an ideal ground.
    - Phase-domain source terminals cannot be attached directly to earth
      nodes. Fault Bus location checks exclude both local and global earth
      nodes from Bus identity matching.

This removes the previous implicit ideal-earth equivalence between separate
reference nodes. No tests/CI or numerical ground-grid fixtures were run.
