GF-SC-D15F-08 — DIRECT CT CONSTRUCTION SUPPORT BOUNDARY
======================================================================

Files:
    core/protection/residual_current.py
    docs/architecture/PROTECTION_STUDY_AND_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md

Changes:
    - Neutral CT construction now requires a Line/Cable FROM or TO terminal;
      the neutral conductor is identified separately by conductor_id.
    - CBCT conductor records normalize and require FROM/TO terminal roles.
    - CBCT window members must all belong to the same equipment terminal.
    - Unsupported equipment-neutral terminal configurations fail during
      construction rather than reaching a runtime adapter that cannot resolve
      their physical current record.

Remaining:
    Multi-conductor transformer, generator, motor and load terminal primitives
    are required before equipment-neutral terminal CT execution can be enabled.


======================================================================
GF-SC-D15F-09 — ATOMIC CORE VALIDATION FOR PHASE-DOMAIN NODE INSERTION
======================================================================

Files:
    core/network/network.py

Finding:
    Phase-domain connection insertion validated a candidate graph before
    mutation, but node insertion only checked the node type and duplicate
    node_id. An Application create-node command could therefore register a
    node that referenced a missing Bus/equipment terminal or contradicted a
    Line/Cable conductor inventory, leaving invalid state in Network until a
    later full validation or study preparation.

Correction:
    - Validate the complete candidate phase-domain graph before mutation.
    - Validate Bus references against the canonical Network registry.
    - Validate equipment identity and terminal-role references against the
      registered Core ElectricalObject.
    - Validate branch-terminal conductor identity and role against the
      owning Core conductor inventory.
    - Perform all checks before inserting the node or invalidating topology;
      failed commands leave the Network graph and revision untouched.

Verification:
    Static source inspection only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, or
    runtime command/rollback execution were run.


======================================================================
GF-SC-D15F-10 — GENERIC EQUIPMENT-TERMINAL CURRENT AND CT ROUTING
======================================================================

Files:
    core/solver/short_circuit/phase_domain_solver.py
    core/protection/residual_current.py
    core/measurement/protection_scenario_measurement.py

Changes:
    - Phase-domain execution now emits a signed conductor-current record
      for an explicitly modeled EQUIPMENT_TERMINAL when its physical
      terminal-current cut is uniquely resolvable from finite-impedance
      incident connections and an explicit, unique voltage base.
    - Current is reported positive INTO_EQUIPMENT, with physical node,
      incident connection IDs, topology revision, project activation and
      SI-current provenance.
    - Equipment-terminal current is omitted with a diagnostic when the
      terminal has no explicit Bus voltage-base reference, lacks a unique
      voltage base, has no finite-impedance incident path, or touches an
      ideal zero-ohm bond whose individual current division is indeterminate.
    - Neutral CT and CBCT construction now accept explicit canonical
      equipment terminal roles rather than hard-coding Line/Cable FROM/TO.
      The measurement adapter accepts an explicitly configured equipment
      terminal and still validates CT P1/P2 topology side, conductor identity,
      terminal identity, construction, ratio, polarity and topology revision.
    - No protection zone, conductor membership, return path, current split,
      or missing construction data is inferred.

Remaining D15F integration:
    - Automatic phase-domain source-equivalent preparation from Grid,
      Generator and Motor operating/short-circuit models is not complete.
    - Transformer winding/vector-group/zero-sequence phase-domain primitives
      are not automatically prepared from Transformer construction data.
    - Motor, Generator and Load multi-conductor electrical primitives and
      their internal current paths are not yet automatically assembled.
    - Equipment-terminal records are available only where the explicit graph
      already contains a uniquely resolvable finite-impedance terminal cut.
      This change does not manufacture missing equipment primitives.
    - CT primary installation still requires the physical phase-domain graph
      to represent the SLD-derived conductor cut. Mapping a SimpleWire CT
      installation into that graph remains an integration requirement.
    - No tests/CI were run. Syntax/import checks, numerical terminal-current
      fixtures, persistence round-trip, and end-to-end Neutral CT/CBCT
      Protection Study execution remain unverified.

Status:
    Source changes committed to the PR branch; not production-verified.

GF-SC-D15F-11 — CT ASSOCIATION / SOLVER TOPOLOGY PROVENANCE GATE
======================================================================

File:
    core/measurement/protection_scenario_measurement.py

Correction:
    - Physical-conductor CT sample conversion now requires the resolved
      CT primary P1/P2 association topology revision to equal the exact
      phase-domain short-circuit result topology revision.
    - Rejects missing or malformed project activation provenance before
      converting a physical conductor-current record into a CT sample.
    - Prevents a stale CT association from being reused after a committed
      topology edit, even if a record identity happens to remain stable.

Boundary:
    - This closes a provenance-integrity defect in the measurement path.
      It does not claim automatic SLD SimpleWire-to-physical-conductor
      cut construction or automatic source/equipment primitive assembly.

Verification:
    - Static source edit committed; tests/CI and runtime checks were not run.

    CORRECTED — INTEGRATION BLOCKERS REMAIN

    - Rejects records whose embedded topology revision disagrees with

GF-SC-D15F-13 — PHASE-DOMAIN SOURCE TERMINAL IDENTITY GATE
======================================================================

File:
    core/solver/short_circuit/phase_domain_solver.py

Correction:
    - Phase-domain source equivalents must attach to explicit Core
      EQUIPMENT_TERMINAL nodes; direct injection at arbitrary Bus
      conductor nodes is rejected.
    - All conductors in a multi-conductor source must belong to one
      equipment identity.
    - Source identity must match the equipment ID or an explicit
      source_reference on every associated terminal node.
    - Duplicate physical conductor roles within one source are rejected.

Boundary:
    - This validates the identity and physical attachment of explicitly
      supplied source equivalents. It does not yet derive source
      equivalents automatically from Grid/Generator/Motor models or
      infer missing sequence/base/neutral data.

GF-SC-D15F-14 — PHASE-DOMAIN SOURCE OWNER / OPERATING-STATE VALIDATION
======================================================================

File:
    core/analysis/short_circuit_preparation.py

Correction:
    - Every supplied phase-domain source must resolve through the
      captured authoritative Network to one canonical registered Grid,
      Generator or Motor object.
    - Source terminal graph nodes must identify that same equipment
      owner, use its authoritative terminal role, and inject only into
      explicitly declared A/B/C phase conductors.
    - Out-of-service sources are rejected.
    - A Motor contributes only when its explicit running state is true.
    - Unsupported source equipment types fail closed.

Boundary:
    - This binds supplied source equivalents to live Core equipment and
      service state. It does not derive source impedances, transient
      motor reactance, sequence bases, internal voltages, or grounding
      equivalents from incomplete model fields.
    - Automatic Grid/Generator/Motor Norton-equivalent preparation
      remains open; missing electrical construction data must not be
      guessed.

Verification:
    - Static edits committed. Tests/CI, syntax/import checks and
      numerical or end-to-end execution were not run.

Status:
    CORRECTED — AUTOMATIC SOURCE PRIMITIVE DERIVATION REMAINS OPEN

GF-SC-D15F-15 — CANONICAL SOURCE TERMINAL OWNERSHIP
======================================================================

File:
    core/analysis/short_circuit_preparation.py

Correction:
    - Phase-domain source graph nodes must be EQUIPMENT_TERMINAL nodes.
    - Each node's declared terminal role must resolve to an actual
      Terminal in the canonical registered equipment object's terminal
      collection.
    - The Terminal must explicitly identify that same equipment object
      as its owner. A matching equipment_id and role string alone is
      no longer sufficient to authorize source injection.

Boundary:
    - This validates source terminal identity and ownership only.
      It does not derive source impedance, internal voltage, sequence
      parameters, grounding, or automatic Norton equivalents.

Verification:
    - Remote source edit committed. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks,
      numerical fixtures, or end-to-end study execution were run.

      and does not establish physical conductor cut mapping.

GF-SC-D15F-17
======================================================================

Scope:
    Phase-domain short-circuit source identity and equipment provenance.

Finding:
    A source equivalent could use a source_id different from the canonical
    equipment identity when its graph nodes repeated that same source_id in
    source_reference metadata. That metadata is descriptive graph data, not an
    independent authority for equipment ownership.

Correction:
    PhaseDomainShortCircuitCase now requires every scalar or multi-conductor
    source_id to equal the equipment_id declared by all of its equipment
    terminal nodes. Optional source_reference metadata may be absent or repeat
    that canonical identity; it cannot alias or override equipment ownership.

Engineering boundary:
    This closes an identity/provenance ambiguity only. It does not claim that
    source impedance, internal voltage, sequence data, grounding, or equipment
    construction parameters are automatically derived. Those remain explicit
    inputs and unsupported/missing construction data must fail closed.

Status:
    CORRECTED — REMOTE SOURCE VERIFICATION REQUIRED

Verification:
    Source block and register entry verified after commit. No tests, CI,
    syntax/import checks, or numerical execution performed.

GF-SC-D15F-18
======================================================================

Scope:
    Explicit original impedance bases for Grid per-unit sequence data.

Finding:
    Grid accepted z1_pu/z2_pu/z0_pu without recording the MVA and voltage
    bases on which those per-unit values were specified. Physical-ohm
    conversion cannot be performed safely without both bases.

Correction:
    Grid now accepts impedance_base_mva and impedance_base_voltage_kv,
    validates them as a paired positive contract, requires both whenever
    any per-unit sequence impedance is supplied, and exposes them in its
    diagnostic summary. short_circuit_mva/x_over_r do not substitute for
    missing sequence data.

Engineering boundary:
    This establishes the Grid original per-unit base contract only. It
    does not derive phase-domain source equivalents or assert completeness
    of internal voltage, grounding, negative/zero-sequence data.

Status:
    CORRECTED — REMOTE SOURCE VERIFICATION REQUIRED

Verification:
    Constructor, validation and summary changes verified after commit.
    No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or numerical execution performed.

GF-SC-D15F-19
======================================================================

Scope:
    Explicit synchronous Generator short-circuit construction contract.

Correction:
    Generator now stores optional positive-sequence subtransient, negative-
    sequence and zero-sequence impedances in per-unit, their original MVA
    and voltage bases, explicit internal voltage magnitude/angle, and an
    explicit neutral-grounding state/impedance. The model validates finite
    passive impedances, paired positive bases, required positive-sequence
    impedance/internal voltage when sequence data is supplied, and the
    consistency of grounded-neutral configuration. Solid grounding is
    represented explicitly by 0j.

Engineering boundary:
    Load-flow P/Q and V_setpoint are not short-circuit source parameters.
    Missing z2/z0 remains unavailable and must be rejected by a study that
    requires those sequence networks. This change does not yet derive the
    phase-domain source primitive or validate the physical neutral/earth
    return path in the conductor graph.

Status:
    CORRECTED — REMOTE SOURCE VERIFICATION REQUIRED

Verification:
    Constructor, validation, diagnostics and register changes verified
    remotely. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or numerical execution.

GF-SC-D15F-20
======================================================================

Scope:
    Explicit Motor short-circuit construction contract.

Correction:
    Motor now accepts explicit locked_rotor_z1_pu, negative- and zero-
    sequence impedances, original MVA/voltage bases, and explicit pre-fault
    internal voltage magnitude/angle. The model validates finite passive
    impedances, nonzero impedance values, paired positive bases, and
    required locked-rotor positive-sequence data and internal voltage.

Engineering boundary:
    rated_mva, rated_kv, power_factor, slip and starting_current_pu remain
    nameplate/operating data; none is silently converted into a fault
    equivalent. Missing sequence data remains unavailable. This contract
    does not yet model induction-motor decay, rotor time constants,
    saturation, terminal phase connection, or a physical neutral/earth
    return path, and does not yet generate a phase-domain source primitive.

Status:
    CORRECTED — REMOTE SOURCE VERIFICATION REQUIRED

Verification:
    Constructor, validation, diagnostics and register changes verified
    remotely. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks, or numerical execution.

======================================================================
GF-SC-D15F-21
======================================================================

Scope:
    Automatic phase-domain source primitive preparation for Grid and
    Generator equipment.

Correction:
    Added phase_domain_source_preparation.py. It derives one coupled ABC
    Norton source per in-service canonical Grid/Generator from explicit
    Z0/Z1/Z2, declared impedance bases, terminal Bus voltage base and
    explicit source voltage. Per-unit sequence impedances are converted
    to SI ohms and transformed into a reciprocal phase impedance matrix.
    At D15F-21, Generator neutral impedance was folded into Z0 as 3*Zn.
    D15F-22 supersedes that treatment: Generator Z0 is machine-only and
    neutral grounding is a separate physical GROUND_ELECTRODE connection.
    Grid Z0 must still include its external grounding contribution.
    ShortCircuitPreparation now automatically builds source primitives
    when phase-domain faults are supplied without explicit sources.

Fail-closed boundaries:
    All active registered Grid/Generator objects must be represented.
    Missing sequence data, unsupported grounding, ambiguous phase terminal
    nodes, base mismatch, non-passive resistance, or a running Motor without
    a validated decay model prevents automatic preparation. P/Q, V setpoint,
    nameplate values and starting_current_pu are not substituted.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — REMOTE SOURCE VERIFICATION REQUIRED

Verification:
    Source builder, ShortCircuitPreparation integration and this register
    entry verified remotely. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks or numerical
    execution performed.

======================================================================
GF-SC-D15F-22
======================================================================

Scope:
    Physical Generator neutral-source and earth-return integration, later
    extended to Grid sources by D15F-23.

Correction:
    - Added a grounded multi-conductor source primitive with three
      internal phase-to-neutral source impedances and a distinct physical
      neutral terminal. Solver stamping uses a phase-to-neutral incidence
      matrix, so the source neutral participates in nodal KCL.
    - Generator Z0 is machine-only in the phase source matrix; neutral
      grounding impedance is no longer folded into 3*Zn there.
    - Automatic Generator source preparation requires one explicit N
      equipment-terminal node and exactly one source-referenced
      GROUND_ELECTRODE connection. Its complex impedance must match the
      Generator neutral-grounding model value, and the local earth node
      must have an explicit graph path to EARTH_REFERENCE.
    - Grounded source current results include A/B/C phase currents and
      the balancing neutral current. The grounding electrode connection
      current is calculated by the physical connection-current result path.

Fail-closed boundaries:
    - Ideal/solid neutral grounding is rejected because this solver does
      not yet resolve individual current through zero-ohm graph bonds.
    - Grid external source grounding remains represented by its declared
      Z0 contract; a separate physical Grid neutral/earth source port is
      not yet implemented.
    - PE/screen return paths, multiple-electrode current division,
      ideal-bond current recovery, and complete source-to-earth current
      reconciliation remain open.
    - No source grounding is inferred from the Grid grounded flag or
      Generator neutral_grounded flag alone.

Verification:
    - Remote source edits and register entry committed. No tests/CI,
      syntax/import checks, numerical fixtures, or end-to-end study
      execution were run.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — REMOTE SOURCE VERIFICATION REQUIRED; NOT PRODUCTION-VERIFIED

GF-SC-D15F-23
======================================================================

Scope:
    Physical external Grid source neutral and earth-return integration.

Correction:
    - Grid now accepts and reports explicit
      neutral_grounding_impedance_ohm configuration.
    - Grid phase-domain source preparation requires the explicit N
      equipment-terminal node and one source-referenced
      GROUND_ELECTRODE connection, matching the configured complex
      grounding impedance and terminating at a local EARTH_NODE with
      an explicit path to EARTH_REFERENCE.
    - Grid source Z0 is treated as source-internal zero-sequence
      impedance; external neutral grounding is not hidden in Z0.
    - Grid and Generator now use the same grounded ABCN Norton source
      primitive. The solver stamps their neutral node in nodal KCL and
      reports the balancing source neutral current.
    - Grid summary and constructor documentation expose the new field.

Fail-closed boundaries:
    - Legacy Grid objects remain constructible for non-phase-domain
      workflows, but phase-domain source preparation rejects a grounded
      Grid without explicit neutral grounding impedance and graph data.
    - Ideal grounding, PE/screen return modelling, multiple-electrode
      current division and complete source-to-earth current reconciliation
      remain unsupported.

Verification:
    - Remote source, export, and Modification_Register checks completed.
      No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, numerical fixtures, or end-to-end
      short-circuit execution were run.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — REMOTE SOURCE VERIFICATION REQUIRED; NOT PRODUCTION-VERIFIED

GF-SC-D15F-24
======================================================================

Scope:
    Physical source-neutral and earth-return current reconciliation.

Correction:
    - PhaseDomainShortCircuitSolution now exposes immutable
      source_return_reconciliation records.
    - Every grounded ABCN source must reconcile its signed source-neutral
      current with the current through its unique source-referenced
      GROUND_ELECTRODE connection. Edge orientation is normalized to
      current leaving the source neutral.
    - Every local EARTH_NODE enforces Kirchhoff current continuity across
      all incident finite-impedance grounding/electrode/earth-return
      connections. This supports explicit parallel earth-return paths
      and current division without treating earth as an ideal node.
    - Ideal or unresolved connections on a source electrode or local
      earth-return node fail closed; no arbitrary current split is
      fabricated.
    - Reconciliation records include signed currents, connection IDs,
      residuals, tolerances and incident earth-return connection IDs.
    - Grid/Generator grounding validation diagnostics now use generic
      source wording.

Fail-closed boundaries:
    - PE/screen current entering the earth network must still be explicitly
      represented by graph connections. No implicit PE or screen return
      path is created.
    - Current through ideal zero-ohm bonds remains indeterminate and is
      rejected when needed for source/earth reconciliation.
    - Global EARTH_REFERENCE is treated as the network reference boundary;
      local earth-node KCL is enforced, but earth-electrode soil mutual
      impedance and distributed earth-potential models are not included.
    - This does not yet complete SLD SimpleWire-to-physical-conductor
      mapping or end-to-end Neutral CT/CBCT execution.

Verification:
    - Remote source edits and register entry committed. No tests/CI,
      syntax/import checks, numerical fixtures, or end-to-end study
      execution were run.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — REMOTE SOURCE VERIFICATION REQUIRED; NOT PRODUCTION-VERIFIED

GF-SC-D15F-25
======================================================================

Scope:
    Canonical SLD topology identity on physical phase-domain conductor nodes.

Finding:
    The SimpleWire CT association graph and the physical phase-domain
    conductor graph did not share a typed endpoint identity. Equipment IDs
    and Bus IDs alone cannot distinguish conductor roles or Bus attachments.

Correction:
    PhaseDomainConductorNode now optionally carries a canonical
    TopologyEndpointReference. Bus-conductor references must identify the
    same Bus; equipment/branch terminal references must identify the exact
    equipment ID and terminal role. Earth/fault nodes cannot claim SLD
    endpoint identity. Persistence writes the typed reference and reads
    legacy node mappings without it. The graph exposes exact endpoint-plus
    conductor-role lookup and an exactly-one resolver for fail-closed use.

Boundary:
    This is the shared identity contract only. Existing graph builders do
    not yet populate references automatically; CT P1/P2-to-physical-path
    mapping and CBCT aperture membership remain outstanding. A missing
    reference is unavailable, never inferred from equipment or Bus identity.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — STATIC SOURCE CHECK ONLY; RUNTIME VERIFICATION DEFERRED

Verification:
    Remote source re-fetch confirms the new field, identity validation,
    backward-compatible deserialization, exact lookup methods and register
    entry. No tests, CI, syntax/import checks or numerical/end-to-end
    verification were run.

GF-SC-D15F-26
======================================================================

Scope:
    Preserve canonical SLD Bus attachment identity in the authoritative
    electrical topology snapshot.

Finding:
    EquipmentBusAttachment retained equipment, terminal and Bus IDs but
    discarded the exact Bus attachment-N endpoint used by SimpleWire
    connectivity. This prevented downstream phase-domain nodes from
    resolving the same canonical endpoint identity used by CT topology
    association.

Correction:
    - EquipmentBusAttachment now optionally carries bus_attachment_id.
      When present it must be one of the canonical attachment-0 through
      attachment-8 slots. None remains explicit for legacy/direct physical
      terminal-to-Bus attachments that do not expose a SimpleWire Bus slot.
    - TopologyManager._physical_attachments now inspects the terminal's
      committed topology component and preserves the unique matching Bus
      attachment ID.
    - Multiple matching Bus attachment slots on the same resolved Bus are
      rejected as ambiguous. The producer does not fabricate a slot.
    - Existing equipment/terminal/Bus identity and snapshot provenance
      remain unchanged.

Boundary:
    This correction preserves endpoint provenance; it does not yet create
    physical conductor continuity, phase-domain graph nodes, neutral/PE/
    screen paths, CT physical cuts, or CBCT aperture membership. Missing
    Bus attachment identity remains unavailable and must fail closed in
    any consumer requiring an SLD attachment vertex.

Verification:
    Remote re-fetch confirms the optional canonical attachment field,
    range validation, topology-component lookup in the snapshot producer,
    ambiguity rejection, and this register entry. No tests/CI, syntax/import
    checks, numerical fixtures, or end-to-end study execution were run.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — REMOTE SOURCE CHECKED; RUNTIME VERIFICATION DEFERRED

GF-SC-D15F-27
======================================================================

Scope:
    Construct Line/Cable physical phase-domain conductor connectivity from
    canonical committed SLD topology and explicit branch conductor inventory.

Correction:
    - Added build_phase_domain_conductor_network_from_sld() and routed the
      authoritative Network snapshot API through it.
    - For each active Line/Cable conductor, the builder creates distinct
      FROM/TO BRANCH_TERMINAL nodes using the owning branch's explicit
      conductor ID and role.
    - Each branch terminal must resolve exactly one EquipmentBusAttachment
      with a canonical Bus attachment-N slot. Missing or ambiguous SLD
      attachment identity fails closed.
    - The builder creates Bus attachment conductor nodes carrying exact
      TopologyEndpointReference identities and connects branch terminals
      through explicit zero-ohm CONTINUITY edges.
    - Bus attachment nodes of the same role connect to a Busbar conductor
      node. This represents the existing ideal/equipotential Bus model;
      it does not introduce a busbar impedance model.
    - Branch conductor IDs remain owner-local. Global admittance validation
      now checks exact Bus identity and conductor role across the terminal
      continuity edge rather than incorrectly requiring a Bus-owned
      conductor ID to equal a Line/Cable-local conductor ID.
    - Short-circuit preparation now obtains both its initial and
      independently rebuilt authoritative graph through the Network SLD
      snapshot API. Existing explicitly configured source, earth, grounding,
      and fault nodes/connections are merged without replacing their
      declarations.

Fail-closed boundaries:
    - Only explicitly inventoried Line/Cable conductors create branch
      physical paths. No phase, N, PE, screen, pilot, grounding, or earth
      conductor is synthesized.
    - Active branch terminals without canonical SimpleWire Bus attachment
      identity are rejected; direct/legacy attachments are not guessed.
    - Busbar segments are ideal zero-ohm continuity under the existing Bus
      abstraction. Physical busbar impedance, bus sectionalizer/pole
      conductor construction, and bus coupler physical conductor modelling
      remain separate work.
    - This does not yet resolve equipment internal conductor paths,
      transformer winding conductor networks, breaker pole-specific
      physical switching, CT P1/P2 cuts, or CBCT aperture membership.
    - No PE/N/screen bonds or earth-return paths are inferred.

Verification:
    Remote source re-fetch confirms the SLD graph builder, canonical
    attachment/terminal references, branch-terminal continuity, Busbar
    role continuity, owner-local conductor identity handling, Network
    snapshot integration, both short-circuit graph construction paths,
    package export, and this register entry. No tests/CI, syntax/import
    checks, numerical fixtures, or end-to-end short-circuit execution were
    run. Runtime behaviour remains unverified.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — REMOTE SOURCE CHECKED; RUNTIME VERIFICATION DEFERRED

======================================================================
GF-SC-D15F-28
======================================================================

Scope:
    Explicit breaker physical pole construction and switching-state
    projection into the phase-domain conductor graph.

Correction:
    - Breaker accepts an optional explicit phase_domain_pole_states mapping
      from conductor role A/B/C/N to a boolean pole-closed state.
    - The mapping is validated, normalized, immutable, and included in the
      Breaker diagnostic summary. A global Breaker.closed state remains the
      master switching state; an individual pole conducts only when both
      the master state and that pole's explicit state are closed.
    - The SLD phase-domain graph builder creates separate FROM/TO
      EQUIPMENT_TERMINAL nodes for every declared breaker pole.
    - Breaker terminal endpoint references use the canonical lowercase
      EquipmentType value and preserve the exact Core terminal-role spelling.
    - Breaker terminal nodes attach to exact canonical SLD Bus attachment
      slots and same-role Busbar conductor nodes.
    - An internal zero-ohm CONTINUITY edge is created only for a closed
      global breaker and an explicitly closed pole.
    - In-service breakers without explicit pole construction fail closed.
      Failed breakers fail closed because their physical conduction state
      is not represented by this model.
    - Branch terminal attachment lookup is now case-insensitive while the
      topology reference retains the exact canonical terminal role.

Fail-closed boundaries:
    - No default three-pole inventory is inferred from the Breaker class.
    - Neutral switching is represented only by an explicit N pole entry.
    - PE and screen switching are not accepted as breaker pole roles by
      this initial contract.
    - Pole contact resistance, arc/prestrike, pole discrepancy, stuck
      contacts, failure modes, and time-dependent switching are not modeled.
    - Switch, disconnector, fuse and transformer internal conductor paths
      are not covered by this change.
    - The breaker pole mapping is a Core construction contract; no UI
      configurator or persistence schema migration is included here.

Verification:
    Remote source re-fetch only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks,
    serialization round-trip, numerical fixtures, or end-to-end execution
    were run. Runtime behaviour remains unverified.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — REMOTE SOURCE CHECK ONLY; RUNTIME VERIFICATION DEFERRED

GF-SC-D15F-29
======================================================================

Scope:
    Explicit phase-domain conductor inventories and operating-state paths
    for Switch, Disconnector and Fuse equipment in the SLD-derived
    physical conductor graph.

Corrections:
    - Switch, Disconnector and Fuse Core models now accept an optional
      phase_domain_conductor_roles construction inventory.
    - The inventory is explicit, immutable and limited to supported
      switchable conductor roles A, B, C and N. Duplicate, empty,
      unsupported and implicit conductor declarations are rejected.
    - Each in-service device must declare its physical conductor inventory
      before phase-domain graph construction. Missing construction fails
      closed; no three-pole or neutral inventory is inferred.
    - The SLD graph builder creates separate FROM/TO equipment-terminal
      conductor nodes, exact canonical terminal topology references,
      canonical Bus attachment nodes and same-role Busbar continuity.
    - Switch internal paths conduct only when the Switch is closed and
      in service.
    - Disconnector internal paths conduct only when the Disconnector is
      closed and in service.
    - Fuse internal paths conduct only when the Fuse is in service and
      not blown. A blown fuse has no internal continuity edge.
    - The graph builder uses the equipment models' authoritative operating
      states; it does not duplicate or mutate those states.

Fail-closed boundaries:
    - This contract models a device's declared phase/neutral conductor
      inventory as continuous terminal-to-terminal poles when its
      operating state permits conduction. It does not infer PE, screen,
      pilot, earth or bonding paths.
    - Per-pole switching discrepancies, fuse element resistance,
      pre-arcing/arcing behaviour, fuse time-current melting, switch
      contact resistance, disconnector earthing blades and transient
      switching phenomena are not represented.
    - Construction persistence/schema migration and UI configurator
      integration remain outstanding.
    - Existing Breaker-specific D15F-28 contract remains separate pending
      consolidation into a common physical conductor construction value
      contract; this change does not silently reinterpret Breaker data.
    - Transformer winding paths and CT physical cut/CBCT aperture mapping
      remain outstanding.

Verification:
    Remote source re-fetch only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks,
    persistence round-trip, numerical fixtures or end-to-end study
    execution were run. Runtime behaviour remains unverified.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED — REMOTE SOURCE CHECK ONLY; RUNTIME VERIFICATION DEFERRED

GF-SC-D15F-30
======================================================================

Scope:
    Consolidate equipment-internal conductor construction into one
    authoritative Core PhysicalConductorConstruction contract and consume
    it from the SLD-derived phase-domain conductor graph.

Contract:
    - PhysicalConductorPath identifies one conductor role, its FROM/TO
      terminal roles and its physical series impedance in ohms.
    - PhysicalConductorConstruction is immutable, validates non-empty
      typed paths, rejects duplicate role/terminal path identities, and
      exposes a canonical conductor-role inventory.
    - The contract is exported from core.model.
    - Breaker, Switch, Disconnector and Fuse expose a common
      physical_conductor_construction property backed by their existing
      explicit construction fields.
    - Construction and operating state remain separate authorities.
      Breaker pole states/global closed/failed, Switch and Disconnector
      closed state, and Fuse conducts state remain owned by their
      respective equipment models.
    - The phase-domain SLD graph builder consumes the common path records
      for all four switching equipment types, resolves exact terminal
      roles and canonical Bus attachment slots, and uses each path's
      declared impedance rather than hard-coding every internal path
      as zero-ohm.

Fail-closed boundaries:
    - Missing construction for an in-service switching device is rejected.
    - Missing terminal mapping, ambiguous/missing canonical Bus attachment,
      missing Breaker pole state, or failed Breaker is rejected.
    - No conductor inventory, terminal mapping, grounding, PE, screen,
      or neutral path is inferred.
    - This first contract covers two-terminal internal paths only.
      Transformer winding topology, multi-terminal equipment, parallel
      conductors, switching transients, fuse arc/melting behaviour,
      per-pole contact impedance configuration and equipment-specific
      physical persistence/UI configuration remain outstanding.
    - Existing equipment construction fields are retained as compatibility
      inputs; this entry does not claim a full schema migration or
      configurator implementation.

Verification:
    Remote source re-fetch confirms the common construction/path types,

GF-SC-D15F-33

Scope:
    Add an explicit Transformer model-to-phase-domain multiport producer.

Corrections:
    - Transformer accepts an optional complete terminal-domain admittance
      matrix in SI Siemens. The matrix is immutable, finite, square,
      reciprocal and has positive-semidefinite terminal conductance.
    - Matrix input requires explicit tap-ratio and phase-shift snapshots
      matching the active Transformer tap and phase shift. Changing either
      operating parameter makes the producer reject stale matrix data.
    - Matrix data is included in the Transformer diagnostic summary.
    - Added prepare_transformer_phase_domain_multiport(). It requires an
      in-service Transformer, explicit HV/LV winding construction and
      vector group, explicit matrix data and current tap/shift provenance.
    - The producer resolves ordered HV A/B/C, optional declared neutral,
      LV A/B/C and optional declared neutral graph nodes by exact equipment,
      terminal-role and conductor-role identity. Missing or ambiguous
      nodes fail closed.
    - The producer checks matrix dimension against the exact physical
      winding terminal inventory and emits PhaseDomainTransformerMultiport
      with stable terminal labels and signed current ordering.
    - Exported the producer through core.analysis.

Important boundary:
    - The producer consumes a complete externally supplied terminal
      admittance matrix. It does not derive winding incidence, delta-loop
      equations, turns ratio, phase displacement, leakage, magnetizing
      branch or core/zero-sequence behaviour from scalar r/x/b or vector
      group metadata.
    - The matrix supplier must construct those effects from validated
      manufacturer/test data and the declared winding construction.
    - The SLD graph builder still rejects in-service transformers until
      physical winding terminal nodes and multiport case integration are
      added. This producer cannot be used until those exact nodes exist.
    - Transformer persistence serialization/migration and Application
      configurator support for the new matrix/provenance fields remain
      outstanding.
    - No HV-LV galvanic continuity is introduced.

GF-SC-D15F-35
======================================================================

Scope:
    Add explicit transformer grounded-wye neutral electrode and earth
    return paths to the SLD-derived physical conductor graph.

Corrections:
    - TransformerWindingConductorConstruction now requires a separate
      explicit non-zero earth_return_impedance_ohm whenever a grounded-wye
      neutral is declared.
    - Neutral grounding impedance and earth-return impedance are distinct
      physical elements. Both must be finite, passive and non-zero;
      ideal zero-ohm return paths are rejected because individual current
      division is not resolved by the current solver contract.
    - The SLD graph builder creates one local EARTH_NODE and one
      EARTH_REFERENCE per explicitly grounded transformer winding.
    - It creates a GROUND_ELECTRODE edge from the exact neutral terminal
      node using the configured neutral grounding impedance, and an
      EARTH_RETURN edge from the local earth node to the reference using
      the separately configured earth-return impedance.
    - The transformer multiport producer verifies exactly one matching
      electrode, exact configured impedances, and earth reachability.
    - Explicit core construction is now required for transformer
      zero-sequence coverage assessment.

Important boundary:
    - The supplied terminal-domain admittance matrix remains the only
      authority for winding incidence, delta circulation, magnetic
      coupling, turns ratio, vector displacement and core-return physics.
    - A core construction label (THREE_LIMB, FIVE_LIMB, SHELL_FORM) is
      metadata only; it does not synthesize a zero-sequence core model.
    - No automatic delta circulating-current or core zero-sequence
      correction is derived. Such behaviour must already be encoded in
      the validated terminal-domain matrix or the study is unavailable.
    - PE/screen parallel earth-return current division, multiple
      electrodes, mutual earth impedance and soil frequency dependence
      remain outside this increment.
    - No implicit neutral-to-earth connection is created for ungrounded
      wye or delta windings.

Verification:
    Remote source re-fetch only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks,
    graph fixtures, current-balance numerical cases or end-to-end
    transformer earth-fault study were run. Runtime behaviour remains
    unverified.

D15F-35 model correction:
    Transformer constructor and physical_conductor_construction producer now expose and validate separate HV/LV neutral earth-return impedance fields. Grounded-wye construction cannot be produced without explicit return impedance; ungrounded/delta windings reject these fields. Transformer diagnostic summary includes both values.

D15F-35 solver reconciliation addition:
    PhaseDomainShortCircuitSolver now compares each transformer grounded
    winding neutral multiport terminal current against the signed physical
    GROUND_ELECTRODE branch current. A missing/duplicate/ideal electrode or
    current mismatch fails the solve; successful checks are included in
    source_return_reconciliation under TRANSFORMER:<id>:<winding>:N.

Scope:
    Bind transformer zero-sequence core-return and delta-circulating
    coverage claims to the exact phase-domain terminal admittance matrix.

Corrections:
    - Added TransformerZeroSequenceMultiportEvidence with:
        * SHA-256 fingerprint of the ordered complex terminal admittance
          matrix and its terminal labels;
        * explicit THREE_LIMB, FIVE_LIMB or SHELL_FORM core construction;
        * a non-empty core zero-sequence manufacturer/test reference;
        * one non-empty circulating-current test reference for each
          delta-connected winding and no references for non-delta windings.
    - Added deterministic transformer_multiport_fingerprint().
    - Transformer model accepts typed evidence only alongside an explicit
      terminal-domain admittance matrix and exposes it in diagnostics.
    - The transformer multiport producer validates exact terminal order,
      exact matrix digest, core construction identity, and exact delta
      winding evidence coverage. Missing or stale evidence fails closed.
    - Exported the evidence type and fingerprint helper from core.model.

Engineering boundary:
    - Evidence binding prevents a matrix from being silently reused after
      terminal-order, matrix-value, core-type or delta-topology changes.
    - This is provenance/coverage validation, not an independent numerical
      reconstruction of magnetic core or delta-loop physics.
    - The terminal admittance matrix remains responsible for the actual
      external zero-sequence response and delta circulation equivalent.
    - Internal delta loop currents are not individually observable from a
      terminal-only reduced multiport. Their numerical reporting requires
      explicit internal loop ports/branch states and is not claimed here.
    - Three-limb/five-limb/shell-form behaviour is not inferred from labels;
      the manufacturer/test evidence must describe the supplied matrix.
    - No unsupported transformer connection/core combination is promoted
      to validated merely by supplying a reference string.

GF-SC-D15F-37
======================================================================

Scope:
    Solve and report explicit internal transformer delta circulating-loop
    currents rather than exposing only a reduced terminal equivalent.

Corrections:
    - Added TransformerDeltaLoopState with explicit HV/LV winding,
      unique loop identity, terminal coupling coefficient vector and
      non-zero passive loop impedance.
    - Transformer model accepts typed internal loop states only alongside
      its complete terminal-domain admittance matrix.
    - Transformer multiport preparation requires at least one explicit
      loop state for every delta-connected winding, rejects loop states
      on non-delta windings, and binds each coupling vector to the exact
      ordered terminal inventory.
    - PhaseDomainTransformerMultiport now carries internal loop labels,
      reciprocal coupling coefficients and a passive loop impedance
      matrix.
    - Solver stamps the internal loops as modified-nodal-analysis current
      unknowns coupled to the terminal voltage equations. The resulting
      loop currents are solved simultaneously with network voltages,
      sources, faults and transformer terminal currents.
    - PhaseDomainShortCircuitSolution exposes immutable
      transformer_delta_loop_currents_a by transformer and loop label.
    - Terminal current reporting includes the solved internal-loop
      coupling contribution, preserving terminal/loop reciprocity.

Engineering boundary:
    - Coupling coefficients and loop impedances must come from explicit
      manufacturer/test-derived internal-loop data. No delta branch
      impedance, winding turns ratio, or loop coupling is inferred.
    - The MNA loop state is a reduced internal-current coordinate; it is
      not yet an individually placeable SLD conductor or a thermal
      conductor segment with geometry and skin/proximity effects.
    - A delta winding without explicit loop state fails closed.
    - Core zero-sequence response remains encoded in the evidence-bound
      terminal matrix; core limb flux/current states are not yet exposed.

Verification:
    Remote source re-fetch and static source assertions only. No tests/CI,
    syntax/import checks, MNA numerical fixture, current-balance fixture,

D15F-37 evidence-binding correction:
    Delta-loop test evidence now includes delta_loop_sha256. The digest
    covers ordered loop ids, winding identities, terminal coupling
    coefficients and loop impedances. Multiport preparation verifies the
    digest against the active model loop-state tuple, so changing an
    internal loop parameter invalidates its evidence association.

GF-AUD-136 — Distinguish draft Simple Wires from committed topology
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-30

Scope:
    DraftNetwork SLD projection and visible Simple Wire lifecycle state

Files:
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Draft equipment nodes are rendered through SymbolPreviewItem with
    VisualState.PREVIEW. Draft connections were separately projected with
    projection_source="draft_network" and core_connection_id=None, but the
    draft connection render loop forced VisualState.NORMAL. That reused the
    same normal connection pen as committed SLD connections and made an
    uncommitted wire visually indistinguishable from committed topology.

Correction:
    Render DraftNetwork connections with VisualState.PREVIEW. The existing
    visual_pen contract renders PREVIEW connections with the canonical
    symbol_preview token and a dashed pen. The distinction is presentation
    only: it does not create Core connectivity, mutate DraftNetwork, alter
    canonical endpoint identity, or bypass CommitNetworkCommand.

Architecture:
    DraftNetwork remains Application-owned. DraftSLDProjection and the SLD
    renderer only project authoring state. Core electrical topology changes
    only through the canonical Application commit command after validation.

Verification:
    - Focused source and diff review only.
    - No tests or CI were run, per repository rules.
    - GUI preview appearance, cancellation cleanup, and transition after a
      successful commit remain unverified.
    - ui/core/qt.py is unchanged; no new Qt symbol or package export is needed.

Disposition:
    Issue #872 remains open pending GUI verification. Related issue #874
    alleges coordinate-based endpoint resolution; the inspected endpoint
    resolvers match canonical node/equipment/terminal/attachment identities,
    not pixel proximity. Keep #874 open pending a reproducible runtime trace.

GF-SC-D15F-40

Scope:
    Add durable, versioned persistence for Application StudyResult
    executions without modifying canonical .gridforge project
    configuration or losing typed transformer delta-loop diagnostics.

Corrections:
    - Added StudyResultArchive as an append-only execution-artifact store
      under the user's GridForge application data directory.
    - Archive records carry format/schema version, unique execution-record
      identity, saved timestamp, StudyResult identity/status, project and
      activation provenance, all four source revision counters, metadata,
      and result payload.
    - Canonical JSON payloads are SHA-256 integrity checked on restore.
      Writes use a same-directory temporary file, fsync and atomic replace.
    - Result encoding explicitly supports finite numeric/complex values,
      UUIDs, enums, NumPy arrays/scalars, mappings, collections and
      dataclass field snapshots. Unsupported runtime objects fail closed.
    - ShortCircuitTransformerDeltaLoopCurrent records use their explicit
      typed codec and are reconstructed as typed records when archived
      Protection/Short-Circuit result payloads are loaded. Other detached
      dataclass values restore as tagged field mappings; arbitrary Python
      classes are never imported or instantiated from persisted data.
    - StudyResultArchive supports loading all records or the latest record
      for a project, activation generation and Study Case identity.
    - StudyService now accepts a durable result persister. It persists
      completed, failed and cancelled terminal outcomes before publishing
      the corresponding terminal event. A failure to persist a completed
      result converts the in-memory outcome to failed and publishes
      StudyFailed rather than reporting a non-durable completion.
    - Application composition wires the archive at
      ~/.gridforge/study-results. Project configuration save/load remains
      unchanged and cannot overwrite the separate execution archive.

Persistence boundary:
    - Study results are stored outside the canonical .gridforge package.
      The archive is keyed by a SHA-256 project identity directory and
      retains activation-generation and Study Case provenance in each
      independently versioned record.
    - Restored StudyResult.value is a detached tagged data snapshot, not a
      rehydrated live solver/runtime object. Internal delta-loop records
      alone are reconstructed as their canonical typed value object.
    - This is an execution-result archive, not a replacement project
      repository and not a solver cache.

Verification:
    - Remote source re-fetch and focused static source assertions only.
    - No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, archive round-trip, filesystem
      durability exercise, numerical MNA fixture or end-to-end study
      execution was run.
    - Runtime compatibility with every registered study result type
      remains unverified; unsupported payload members intentionally
      fail closed instead of being silently discarded.

      no second archive or result authority was introduced.
    - Added immutable ArchivedStudyResultReadModel rows carrying archive
      record UUID, saved timestamp, Study Case UUID, project identity,

      message, metadata and detached result value.
    - Added archive filters for project, activation generation, Study Case,
      study type, terminal status, saved-time window and free-text search.
    - Added exact archive-record retrieval scoped to the active project.
    - Added a two-record comparison contract. Different projects or study
      types are explicitly marked incompatible; same-type comparisons
      expose whether source revisions and activation generations match.
    - Exposed archived history, record retrieval and comparison through
      Application facade methods scoped to the active project.
    - Exported the new read DTOs and StudyReadService from the Application
      package API.
    - Exposed transformer delta-loop records as a diagnostic-only accessor
      on archived result rows. No CT, CBCT, relay input, measurement channel
      or external conductor-current conversion is introduced.

Presentation boundary:
    - Read DTOs are framework-neutral and suitable for the Study Results
      projection. They do not import Qt or UI types.
    - The current branch inspection did not establish an existing concrete

GF-SC-D15F-46 — STABLE PHYSICAL IDENTITY FOR STUDY RESULT DELTAS
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Scope:
    Correct the numerical Study Result comparison identity contract after
    post-merge review of D15F-43.

Finding:
    The initial comparison traversal used the surrounding collection path
    for Short-Circuit conductor and transformer delta-loop observations.
    For sequence-backed collections, this could pair unlike physical
    observations if records were reordered between executions.

Corrections:
    - Power Flow voltage observations require both a stable solver snapshot
      identity and explicit stable bus IDs; positional bus-index fallback
      is prohibited.
    - ShortCircuitConductorCurrent observations are keyed only by stable
      equipment, terminal role, conductor, bus and phase/sequence identity;
      surrounding mapping/list traversal paths are excluded.
    - Transformer delta-loop observations are keyed only by transformer,
      loop and winding identity; traversal paths and list positions are
      excluded.
    - Duplicate physical observation identities are rejected from the
      comparison rather than resolved by traversal order.
    - Conductor comparison bases now include MVA, kV, topology revision,
      phase/conductor role, reference direction and current units.
    - Transformer delta-loop observations are keyed by transformer ID,
      loop ID and winding identity, not collection index.
    - Delta-loop comparison bases now include MVA, kV, derived current
      base and declared positive direction.
    - RIGHT-minus-LEFT arithmetic is emitted only when both the stable
      observation identity and complete comparison base match.

Engineering boundary:
    - No conversion or normalization between unlike bases is attempted.
    - This correction narrows false-positive matches; it does not make
      two results from different physical topologies comparable merely
      because display names or list positions happen to match.

Verification:
    Remote source inspection and focused static review only. No numerical
    fixture or runtime test was executed.

Status:
    STABLE PHYSICAL IDENTITY AND TOPOLOGY-AWARE BASE MATCHING IMPLEMENTED —
    NUMERICAL FIXTURE VERIFICATION PENDING

GF-PROT-057 — FAIL-CLOSED ANSI 25 BREAKER-CLOSE MUTATION BOUNDARY
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Repository baseline:
    SubhenduMishra29/GridForge main
    69aab115540ee539453f38d65c0c1696fb3d2f96

Scope:
    Prevent Application breaker mutations from closing or energizing a
    breaker without an explicit positive synchronism authorization.

Finding:
    Current main allowed create_breaker(closed=True), update_breaker
    (open-to-closed), and close_breaker to mutate the Core Breaker state
    without an ANSI 25 decision. The earlier GF-PROT-043 register entry
    concerns persisted protection-function identity and is unrelated to
    this breaker-close correction.

Correction:
    - Add an injectable Application-layer breaker-close authorizer.
    - Deny closed breaker creation, open-to-closed updates, and explicit
      close operations when no authorizer is configured.
    - Require the authorizer result to be exactly True.
    - Convert authorizer exceptions and negative decisions into explicit
      DomainError outcomes before Core state mutation.
    - Expose the authorizer through ModelService to the switching service.

Safety boundary:
    - The default composition remains fail-closed until the complete
      project-scoped ANSI 25 guard is supplied.
    - This is containment, not a claim that ANSI 25 is fully operational.
    - Opening, tripping and non-energizing breaker operations remain
      available through their existing paths.
    - Full project policy, trusted measurement provenance, clock domain,
      physical PT/CVT topology, durable audit and bypass lifecycle remain
      separate integration requirements.

Verification:
    - Confirmed all three close mutation entrypoints in current main.
    - Confirmed the authorizer is called before network registration or
      breaker state mutation.
    - Confirmed ModelService passes the authorizer to SwitchingModelService.
    - No tests/CI, syntax/import checks or runtime close-path execution
      were run.

Status:
    FAIL-CLOSED APPLICATION BREAKER-CLOSE BOUNDARY IMPLEMENTED ON A
    MAIN-BASED BRANCH — FULL ANSI 25 GUARD INTEGRATION PENDING

GF-PROT-058 — ANSI 25 AUTHORIZATION FOR BREAKER RE-ENERGIZATION AND ROLLBACK
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Scope:
    Close the remaining Application mutation paths that can restore an
    energized breaker state without a fresh positive ANSI 25 authorization.

Finding:
    GF-PROT-057 protected breaker creation, explicit close, and ordinary
    open-to-closed updates. A breaker that remained logically closed while
    taken out of service could be re-energized by put_breaker_in_service.
    Transaction compensation could also re-register or restore an energized
    breaker without reauthorization after update or deletion.

Correction:
    - Authorize closed-and-in-service transitions in update_breaker.
    - Authorize put_breaker_in_service when the breaker is already closed.
    - Reauthorize transaction rollback paths that restore an energized
      breaker after update, deletion, or service-state mutation.
    - Keep opening and taking a breaker out of service available without
      ANSI 25 authorization.
    - Perform authorization before the energizing state transition or
      network re-registration.

Safety boundary:
    - Uses the existing fail-closed Application authorizer from GF-PROT-057.
    - Does not claim complete project-scoped ANSI 25 guard integration.
    - A missing, stale, invalid, or negative authorizer still denies
      energization and rollback to an energized state.

Verification:
    - Source-level inspection confirms the update, service-state, deletion,
      and compensation paths invoke the existing authorizer before
      re-energization.
    - No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, or runtime breaker operations run.

Status:
    APPLICATION RE-ENERGIZATION PATHS ROUTED THROUGH FAIL-CLOSED ANSI 25
    AUTHORIZATION — FULL PROJECT GUARD INTEGRATION STILL PENDING

GF-PROT-059 — TRUSTED, PROJECT-BOUND MEASUREMENT SAMPLE PUBLICATION
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Scope:
    Port the trusted measurement producer contract from PR #912 onto
    current main without importing its stale project synchronism bootstrap.

Core measurement channel:
    - Add timestamp-domain identity to each sample.
    - Seal project-managed channel sample state behind an Application-owned
      update capability.
    - Seal source identity, terminal association, producer identity and
      engineering configuration after project activation.
    - Require timestamp, timestamp domain and monotonically increasing
      sample sequence for project-managed sample updates.

Application measurement service:
    - Add immutable MeasurementSampleAttestation producer envelopes.
    - Require exact producer, channel, project and activation-generation
      binding.
    - Require signed quality and availability to match the published values.
    - Verify the attestation against the complete sample through an injected
      deployment trust adapter.
    - Require an injected trusted clock and matching clock domain.
    - Reject future-skewed, backwards-timestamped and replayed samples.
    - Serialize producer identity and timestamp domain as channel definition
      metadata; never persist live sample state.
    - Bind publication to the shared Application execution lock so sample
      updates cannot race breaker authorization.

Composition:
    - Expose optional verifier, trusted clock, clock domain and skew policy
      through create_application.
    - Bind MeasurementChannelService to the Application execution lock.
    - Export MeasurementSampleAttestation from the Application services
      package.

Fail-closed behavior:
    - No verifier, trusted clock or clock domain means publication is denied.
    - No producer identity on a channel means no attestation can match.
    - No default production verifier or trusted clock is fabricated.
    - This pass does not yet activate a project-scoped ANSI 25 guard.

Verification:
    - Source-level reconciliation against PR #912 and current main.
    - No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, persistence round-trip or runtime
      sample publication performed.

GF-PROT-051 — Project-scoped ANSI 25 guard and physical instrument topology
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-01

Scope:
    PR #912 Application composition and synchronism-check enforcement

Files:
    core/application/synchronism_check.py
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Correction:
    Add the typed six-channel ANSI 25 policy/measurement guard and compose it
    through the Application breaker-close authorizer. Guard instances are
    scoped to project identity and activation generation and are installed
    transactionally with project state; activation rollback restores the
    prior guard. The authorization boundary also rebuilds the active guard
    against the current policy, project path, Network, and activation generation
    on each close attempt, covering policy edits and Save As path changes. No
    active guard means breaker energization is denied.

Physical topology:
    Resolve breaker FROM/TO terminals to distinct buses through the canonical
    ConnectivityResolver. Every configured left/right voltage, phase-angle,
    and frequency channel must originate from a PT/CVT secondary whose two
    primary terminals resolve only to the corresponding breaker-side bus.
    Missing, ambiguous, out-of-service, wrong-family, or cross-side instrument
    topology fails closed before sample evaluation.

Audit and clock:
    Compose the append-only fsync-backed project sidecar audit only when the
    project is saved and injected trusted clock/domain adapters are available.
    The audit clock domain must match the measurement publication domain.
    Missing clock, domain, saved path, audit sink, policy, or topology validator
    leaves the breaker authorizer closed. A deployment may inject a trusted
    bypass provider; absent that provider, bypass requests cannot authorize
    energization. Bypass authorization is project/generation/breaker scoped,
    single-use, limited to five minutes, and its decision metadata is audited.

Limitations:
    Production verifier/clock adapters, explicit bypass workflow, hardware
    commissioning, runtime tests, persistence round-trip and full end-to-end
    breaker close execution remain unverified. No tests or CI were run.

======================================================================
GF-PROT-052 — Durable single-use ANSI 25 bypass consumption
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-01

Scope:
    Project-scoped ANSI 25 bypass replay protection

Files:
    core/application/synchronism_check.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    The Application authorization callback reconstructs the ANSI 25 guard
    for each breaker-close attempt so that current policy and Save As paths
    are always used. A consumed-token set owned by an individual guard was
    therefore reset on every attempt, allowing a still-valid bypass token
    to be presented again.

Correction:
    ProjectScopedSynchronismAuditSink now claims each bypass authorization
    using a hashed marker created atomically with O_CREAT|O_EXCL in a
    project-ID-scoped registry under the user profile. The marker contains
    the authorization identity and project/generation/breaker scope, is
    fsync-backed, and survives guard reconstruction, process restarts, Save
    As, and project moves. Existing markers, scope mismatches, write errors,
    or any consumer result other than literal True deny bypass. Generic
    non-persistent audit sinks retain guard-local replay protection only.

Safety:
    Token consumption is recorded before the authorization decision audit.
    If the subsequent decision audit or breaker mutation fails, the token
    remains consumed. This deliberately prefers a non-replayable denied
    attempt over possible token reuse.

Limitations:
    Persistent marker guarantees depend on the durability and access-control
    properties of the user-profile filesystem. Directory fsync is applied on
    platforms that support it. No tests or CI were run.

======================================================================
GF-PROT-053 — Seal project-managed measurement source configuration
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-01

Scope:
    MeasurementChannel physical-source and engineering-configuration integrity

Files:
    core/measurement/measurement_channel.py
    core/application/services/measurement_channel_service.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Project-managed channels protected sample-value mutation, but exposed
    mutable source, source-terminal, signal-type, engineering-unit, scaling,
    and producer identity attributes. Since channel objects are returned to
    Application consumers, changing source references could invalidate the
    physical PT/CVT topology trust decision after activation.

Correction:
    MeasurementChannel now seals its physical-source identity and engineering
    configuration after MeasurementChannelService activation. The immutable
    set includes channel identity, signal type, source equipment, source
    terminal, phase, unit, nominal value, scale, polarity, stale-after policy,
    and producer identity. The service assigns the configured producer ID
    before binding its sample-update capability. Sample state remains writable
    only through the authenticated publication service.

Safety:
    Topology and producer checks now consume channel configuration that cannot
    be reassigned through ordinary attribute writes after project activation.
    Project activation rollback retains the original channel objects and their
    sealed state.

Limitations:
    Python in-process code with deliberate access to private object internals is
    outside this object-level mutation boundary. No tests or CI were run.

GF-PROT-054 — Authenticate complete measurement sample envelope
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-01

Scope:
    Trusted measurement publication attestation integrity

Files:
    core/application/services/measurement_channel_service.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    MeasurementSampleAttestation authenticated producer/project/generation,
    timestamp/domain, sequence and signature, while publish_sample accepted
    quality and availability as independent caller-supplied arguments. The
    verifier therefore had no signed source metadata to validate for those
    safety-critical fields. The attestation also did not explicitly bind the
    target logical channel identity.

Correction:
    Extend the typed attestation with channel_id, MeasurementQuality and
    availability. Validate these fields at construction and require the
    published channel, quality and availability to match the attested values
    exactly before invoking the configured verifier. The verifier receives
    the complete typed envelope and raw value, so it can authenticate the
    full sample payload, including its logical-channel association.

Safety:
    A caller cannot promote a signed but SUSPECT/INVALID/UNKNOWN or unavailable
    source sample to GOOD/available by changing publication arguments.
    Cross-channel replay is rejected before sample mutation.

Limitations:
    Deployment verifier implementations must include the new channel_id,
    quality and availability fields in the signature-validation contract.
    No tests or CI were run.

GF-PROT-055 — Bind ANSI 25 instruments to active Network object identity
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-01

Scope:
    ANSI 25 physical measurement source provenance

Files:
    core/application/synchronism_check.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    The topology validator checked PT/CVT source IDs and terminal references,
    then resolved topology by those IDs. It did not prove that the source
    object retained by the MeasurementChannel was the canonical equipment
    instance registered in the active Network. A stale object from another
    Network with colliding IDs could therefore be evaluated against the
    active Network's topology.

Correction:
    Require network.get_by_identity(source.id) to return the exact same object
    instance as channel.source before accepting its PT/CVT type, service state,
    secondary terminal binding, or primary terminal topology. Missing registry
    lookup, missing identity, and object mismatch deny breaker closing.

Safety:
    The instrument state and physical terminals evaluated by ANSI 25 are now
    tied to the exact active Network equipment instance, not merely a matching
    equipment identifier.

Limitations:
    No tests or CI were run; cross-project runtime fixtures remain unverified.

      using the published A, B and p coefficient form.
    - Normalize canonical curve identifiers and supported aliases.

      current/multiple and non-increasing operating time.

      ANSI 51N inherits the same engine through its existing relay class.

======================================================================

    withstand coordination contract.

GF-PROT-063 — APPLICATION PROTECTION STUDY CTI GRADING
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Scope:
    Integrate explicit inverse-time relay coordination grading into the
    existing detached-scenario Protection Study workflow.

Application contract:
    - Add typed downstream/upstream function and measurement-binding pairs.
    - Require explicit breaker clearing time and required CTI.
    - Allow optional maximum breaker clearing time and manufacturer/test
      timing envelopes tied to exact scenario currents.
    - Require unique pairs and explicit short-circuit fault scenarios.
    - Keep the study snapshot immutable and provenance-bound.

Scenario integration:
    - Resolve only enabled ANSI 51/51N elements and explicit current
      measurement bindings.
    - Use the configured IEC/IEEE curve standard, pickup and time dial.
    - Require explicit NEUTRAL_CT or CBCT provenance for ANSI 51N and apply
      the authoritative CT ratio to convert secondary current to primary A.
    - Reject missing, ambiguous, cross-function or conflicting bindings.
    - Calculate available CTI after downstream breaker clearing time.
    - Return NOT_EVALUABLE for missing currents or non-operating relays.
    - Evaluate conservative CTI separately when both timing envelopes and
      maximum breaker clearing time are supplied.
    - Preserve report and diagnostics in each fault-scenario result.
    - Mark the Protection Study partial when coordination cannot be fully
      evaluated; a fully evaluated NOT_COORDINATED result remains explicit.

Safety and architecture:
    - Explicit pairs only; no graph-depth or topology-based inference.
    - No relay trip or breaker mutation is performed by the Study.
    - CT secondary/primary conversion is explicit; three-phase residual
      aggregation is not inferred from a single binding.
    - Manufacturer evidence references are recorded but not cryptographically
      authenticated in this pass.

Verification:
    - Source-level inspection only.
    - No tests/CI, syntax/import checks or independent CTI benchmark run.

Status:
    STATIC 51/51N CTI GRADING INTEGRATED INTO PROTECTION STUDY.

GF-PROT-064 — EVIDENCE-BOUNDED TRANSIENT RELAY RESET MEMORY
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Scope:
    Add explicit relay reset-memory and overshoot evidence records plus a
    piecewise-constant current-trace evaluator for IEC/IEEE inverse-time
    relay coordination.

Implementation:
    - Model reset-memory retention as measured current-multiple-specific
      curves with interpolation only inside the supplied interruption-time
      domain.
    - Require retained memory to begin at 1.0 and never increase with
      dropout duration.
    - Require exact pre-dropout current-multiple evidence; no interpolation
      across current and no extrapolation beyond test data.
    - Integrate normalized operating progress only when the Application
      trace configuration explicitly confirms that the relay's progress
      accumulation model is valid.
    - Return NOT_EVALUABLE when reset evidence is missing or outside domain.
    - Require common observation windows for paired relay traces.
    - Keep overshoot assessment NOT_ASSESSED until the test semantics and
      timing correction are formally mapped; transient CTI cannot be
      declared coordinated without that assessment.
    - Apply final-dropout reset retention at the end of the observation
      window, or return NOT_EVALUABLE when evidence is insufficient.

Limitations:
    - Manufacturer evidence references are provenance labels, not verified
      signatures.
    - Overshoot envelopes are stored but not applied to operation times.
    - Current traces must be relay-equivalent current magnitudes; the RL
      instantaneous waveform is not directly treated as RMS relay current.

Verification:
    - Source-level inspection only; no tests/CI or trace benchmark run.

Status:
    TRANSIENT RESET-MEMORY EVALUATION IMPLEMENTED WITH FAIL-CLOSED GAPS.
    OVERSHOOT-ADJUSTED TRANSIENT CTI REMAINS NOT EVALUABLE.

GF-SC-TRANSIENT-001 — BOUNDED PER-PHASE SERIES-RL FAULT TRANSIENT
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Scope:
    Add an analytical per-phase Thevenin series-RL fault-inception transient
    producer for explicit voltage, frequency, resistance, inductance,
    inception angle and initial current.

Implementation:
    - Solve sinusoidal steady-state current plus exponentially decaying
      DC offset for each independent phase equivalent.
    - Return instantaneous signed primary current samples, time constant,
      steady-state RMS current and power-factor angle.
    - Validate phase identity, positive R/L, finite inputs, unique phases
      and strictly increasing sample times.
    - Validate immutable phase results and declared observation windows.

Explicit limitations:
    - Not a network-wide EMT solver.
    - No inter-phase/mutual coupling, generator decrement, inverter controls,
      CT saturation, arc dynamics, switching topology or breaker pole model.
    - Output is instantaneous signed current, not relay RMS current.
    - No automatic RL-to-RMS measurement conversion is implied.

Verification:
    - Source-level analytical inspection only; no independent waveform
      reference comparison or runtime solve was performed.

Status:
    BOUNDED RL TRANSIENT PRODUCER IMPLEMENTED; NOT AN EMT NETWORK SOLVER.

GF-PROT-067 — ATOMIC BUS-ZONE BREAKER TRIP DISPATCH
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Scope:
    Prevent a multi-relay bus-zone trip set from being committed as a
    sequence of independent breaker topology mutations.

Implementation:
    - Collect valid breaker TRIP decisions from one evaluated Control cycle.
    - Translate each intent to the canonical TripBreakerCommand.
    - Execute two or more trip commands through one aggregate Application
      command and one shared CommandManager Transaction.
    - Reuse CommandManager.execute_in_transaction for every child command;
      each child contributes its inverse to the same rollback journal.
    - If any child handler fails, the outer transaction rolls back the
      complete trip set. No partial breaker-clearing state is committed.
    - On success, CommandManager records one undo/history boundary for the
      complete trip set.
    - Publish one aggregate TopologyChanged and NetworkChanged event after
      commit, with the complete breaker ID set.
    - Treat the aggregate command as an authoritative topology mutation for
      project revision, undo and redo bookkeeping.
    - Non-trip Control decisions retain their existing individual command
      execution path. Single breaker trips retain the canonical single-command
      path.

Boundaries:
    - The atomic unit is all valid breaker TRIP decisions in one Control cycle,
      not a topology-derived bus-zone grouping; ControlDecision currently does
      not carry an authoritative bus-zone identity.
    - This prevents partial trip commits but does not add relay inter-trip
      signal semantics, trip-coil acknowledgement, breaker failure timing or
      physical operating feedback.
    - The Control cycle remains Application-owned; no Core-to-UI dependency.

Verification:
    - Source-level inspection only; branch is based on current main.
    - No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, concurrent execution stress test or
      breaker runtime simulation were run.

Status:
    ATOMIC CONTROL TRIP TRANSACTION IMPLEMENTED IN SOURCE; RUNTIME
    ACCEPTANCE REMAINS PENDING.
