GF-TS-EVENT-954 — DISPATCH EVENTS DUE AT CURRENT SIMULATION TIME
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Finding:
    TransientStabilitySolver.run() dispatched initial-time events only when
    record_initial=True. With record_initial=False, next_event_time() excluded
    events at or within the scheduler tolerance of the current time, allowing
    them to remain pending while simulation time advanced. The incremental
    step() API shared the same omission.

Correction:
    - Add one solver-owned _process_due_events() boundary helper.
    - Dispatch EventManager.process(current_time) independently of sample
      recording, both at run initialization and before each integration step.
    - Dispatch due events before next-future-event lookup in step().
    - Re-evaluate the algebraic DAE state at the current time after any due
      event action so event state changes are reflected before integration.
    - Reuse EventManager's existing tolerance and deterministic event ordering;
      no second scheduler or altered event priority policy is introduced.

Tolerance policy:
    An event is due when its scheduled time is at or before
    current_time + EventManager.tolerance. This permits early dispatch only
    inside the already configured tolerance window. Events outside that window
    remain future events and are integrated to as explicit boundaries.

Boundary:
    EventManager remains the authoritative event scheduler. This correction
    does not change EventExecution schema, failure propagation, event
    persistence or transient result contracts.

Verification:
    - Source-level inspection of run(), step(), EventManager.process() and
      next_event_time().
    - No tests or CI were run, per project instruction.
    - Runtime comparison of run()/step() dispatch traces remains pending.

Status:
    CURRENT-TIME EVENT DISPATCH CORRECTION IMPLEMENTED IN SOURCE.
    RUNTIME EVENT-TRACE ACCEPTANCE REMAINS PENDING.

    - Recheck the project revision after the synchronous saver returns and

    - Reuse the existing Application execution lock and commit-state guard;
      no second lock, transaction manager or persistence scheduler is added.

Boundary:
    ProjectLifecycleService and ProjectPersistence retain their existing

GF-SC-TOPO-949 — VALIDATE SHORT-CIRCUIT TOPOLOGY AND MODEL SCOPE
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

    without proving that its project/activation provenance and canonical

      activation_generation and topology_revision independently, emitting

    - Reject dirty topology rather than silently mixing a supplied snapshot
      with current Network equipment.

      a second mutable revision counter.

    references; it does not replace a future explicit immutable Network
    model snapshot/revision contract.

Verification:
    - Source-level inspection of conventional and phase-domain return paths,

    - No tests or CI were run, per project instruction.
    - Runtime cross-project/cross-activation/stale-content rejection and

    IMPLEMENTED IN SOURCE. RUNTIME ACCEPTANCE REMAINS PENDING.

GF-TS-NORTON-955 — DETACHED MACHINE CONNECTION PATH AND SOURCE GATING
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Finding:
    TransientNetworkSolver assembled a Norton admittance and internal-voltage
    source for every registered dynamic machine, regardless of detached
    breaker/equipment state. A generator could therefore remain electrically
    injecting after its trip was recorded. The detached transient state did
    not carry a machine-to-breaker-controlled connection mapping.

Correction:
    - Extend DynamicMachineModelAssociation with a persisted, ordered
      connection_path of canonical switching-equipment IDs and an explicit
      disconnected_rotor_policy.
    - Keep legacy association records loadable when connection_path is
      absent, but reject transient preparation for any machine without an
      explicit path. No display-name, proximity or guessed-ID fallback.
    - In Application StudyPreparationService, validate each ordered path
      against canonical equipment terminal-to-Bus attachments:
        * every path item resolves by canonical registry identity;
        * each item is a breaker, switch, disconnector or fuse;
        * every item has exactly two distinct Bus attachments;
        * the ordered path is continuous from the machine's canonical
          terminal Bus and contains no repeated switch or Bus loop.
    - Capture initial switch conduction and operability separately from the
      detached ProjectSnapshot Network. Failed, blown or unavailable devices
      cannot be restored by a simulated close event.
    - Carry machine paths, rotor policy, switch states, project/activation
      identity, source revision and topology revision in the immutable
      DetachedTransientNetworkState.
    - Require an exact machine-to-path identity match when constructing the
      transient network solver. Missing or unexpected mappings fail closed.
    - Capture every fully Bus-attached canonical switching device with
      its exact two-Bus connection, conduction state and availability state.
      This includes closed ideal-switch connections outside machine paths.
    - Contract closed and available ideal-switch Bus pairs into common
      numerical nodes for each detached transient solve; an open switch
      separates those Bus nodes. This keeps the transient Y-bus aligned with
      detached switching state instead of treating a closed breaker as a
      display-only/topology-only edge.
    - Gate both Norton admittance and internal-voltage source assembly on
      every switching device in the machine's captured path being conducting
      and available.
    - When opening a machine breaker leaves its terminal Bus electrically
      isolated with a zero Y-bus row/column, assign only that floating node a
      zero-voltage numerical reference. No shunt is added to the energized
      network and the isolated node does not couple into other Bus equations.
    - Preserve machine differential state layout across trips. The explicit
      rotor policy is continue_with_zero_electrical_power: disconnected
      machines retain their rotor angle/speed states and continue the swing
      equation with Pe=0 while the configured mechanical input remains
      applied. This is distinct from electrical Norton-source participation.
    - Feed the same detached path state into DAESolver differential
      evaluation. When the network callback is the bound
      TransientNetworkRuntime.solve method, DAESolver uses the runtime's
      is_machine_connected callback; an explicit callback remains available
      for adapted composition roots.
    - Re-evaluate the DAE after a scheduled event through the existing
      TransientStabilitySolver event boundary, so the post-event algebraic
      solve and rotor Pe calculation use the updated detached path state.
    - Record machine IDs, breaker/path IDs, connected-before/after values,
      switch transition, source topology revision and resulting detached
      network revision in EventExecution result payloads.
    - Include machine connection paths, rotor policy, initial switch state,
      switch availability, project/activation/source revision and final
      detached network revision in transient result provenance.
    - Update transient electrical-power samples to report zero P/Q for a
      disconnected machine rather than calculating terminal output from an
      unrelated bus voltage.
    - Update Modification_Register.txt.

Boundary:
    All switching mutations occur in TransientEventState, a study-owned
    detached copy. Live ProjectSnapshot equipment and Network topology are
    not mutated. EventManager remains the only event scheduler. Core owns
    machine model association and detached network semantics; Application
    validates project/topology scope and captures initial switch state;
    Solver consumes detached connection state.

Coordination:
    - #954 remains the prerequisite for dispatching events at the current
      simulation-time boundary when record_initial=False.
    - #841 post-trip dynamic reinitialization remains separate; this change
      does not alter machine differential-state continuity or introduce a
      reinitialization algorithm.
    - #950 simulated protection-to-breaker coupling remains separate; this
      change consumes detached breaker events, not protection decisions.

Verification:
    - Source-level inspection of association serialization, canonical
      topology attachments, Network registry lookup, switching operability,
      detached event-state mutation, Norton assembly, DAE rotor derivatives,
      event result payloads and transient result provenance.
    - No tests or CI were run, per project instruction.
    - Runtime breaker open/close, failed-device close rejection, rotor
      continuity and post-event algebraic re-solve acceptance remain pending.

Status:
    EXPLICIT MACHINE PATH, DETACHED NORTON GATING, ROTOR POLICY AND EVENT /
    RESULT PROVENANCE IMPLEMENTED IN SOURCE.
    RUNTIME ACCEPTANCE REMAINS PENDING.

======================================================================
GF-TS-NORTON-955A — DETACHED EVENT / DAE CONNECTIVITY PARITY
======================================================================

Finding:
    TransientEventState.is_machine_connected() checked only the detached
    breaker position, while DetachedTransientNetworkState.is_machine_connected()
    also required the captured switching device to be available. A device
    captured as failed, blown or out of service but initially closed could
    therefore be excluded from Norton injection yet still contribute
    electrical power to the rotor swing equation. Event transition records
    could also report the wrong connected-before/after state.

Correction:
    - Apply the same fail-closed predicate in TransientEventState: every
      switching device in the machine's explicit path must be closed and
      explicitly available in the captured snapshot.
    - A missing availability entry is treated as unavailable, matching the
      detached network solver's connectivity contract.
    - Event transition records and DAESolver's inferred connectivity callback
      now consume the same effective machine connection state as Norton
      source assembly.

Verification:
    - Source-level inspection of both detached connectivity predicates and
      their runtime consumers.
    - No tests or CI were run, per project instruction. Runtime verification
      remains pending.

======================================================================
GF-TS-EVENT-960 — IMMUTABLE EVENT DISPATCH AND TRANSITION EVIDENCE
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Finding:
    EventExecution previously stored only event identity/type, scheduled
    timestamp and an untyped action result. EventManager invoked actions
    without recording actual simulation dispatch time, omitted priority,
    description and metadata, and discarded failed-action evidence by raising
    EventError before appending history. Event state mutations did not expose
    typed before/after revision evidence. The transient solver did not record
    whether the same-time post-event algebraic re-evaluation succeeded.

Correction:
    - Preserve the legacy EventExecution.time field as scheduled time and add
      explicit scheduled_time and dispatch_time fields.
    - EventManager.process() records its current simulation time as dispatch
      time; process_interval() records the actual interval end reached by the
      integrator. Scheduled and dispatch times are never conflated.
    - Capture event priority, description and a recursively detached,
      immutable metadata/action-result representation.
    - Add explicit succeeded/failed execution status, failure type/message,
      pre-event/post-event revision, and post-event algebraic
      re-evaluation status/failure evidence.
    - Add an optional revision_reader to SimulationEvent/EventManager
      registration. Transient event factories bind it to the authoritative
      TransientEventState revision.
    - Return structured transition records from breaker open/close,
      equipment-state and fault apply/clear actions. Records include previous
      and resulting state, machine/path transitions where applicable, source
      topology revision and pre/post detached-network revisions.
    - Record failed action evidence in EventManager.history before raising
      EventError. EventError exposes the immutable failed EventExecution.
    - Use at-most-once failure semantics by default: a failed event remains
      triggered and is not silently retried. EventManager.retry(event_id)
      explicitly re-arms only a recorded failed attempt and preserves the
      previous attempt in history.
    - Preserve deterministic ordering by scheduled time, priority and event
      ID for simultaneous events.
    - After successful event dispatch, annotate each EventExecution with the
      same-time post-event algebraic re-evaluation outcome. If re-evaluation
      fails, the event remains recorded as successfully dispatched while its
      re-evaluation status is failed, and the transient solver fails safely.
    - Update Modification_Register.txt.

Boundary:
    EventManager remains the sole scheduler and TransientEventState remains
    the sole detached transient mutation owner. The solver supplies actual
    simulation dispatch time and records algebraic re-evaluation outcome.
    No duplicate scheduler, live-project mutation or automatic event retry
    mechanism is introduced.

Coordination:
    - #954 remains responsible for current-time due-event dispatch when
      record_initial=False.
    - #958 must update transient result archive encoding/decoding to preserve
      the expanded immutable EventExecution fields and nested metadata.
    - #959 can consume the enriched event timeline/read model.
    - #955 supplies the machine path and switch transition payloads retained
      by this event record.

Verification:
    - Source-level inspection of EventManager.add/add_event, process(),
      process_interval(), _execute_event(), explicit retry, revision capture,
      TransientEventState mutation results and TransientStabilitySolver
      post-event algebraic re-evaluation paths.
    - No tests or CI were run, per project instruction.
    - Runtime dispatch-time tolerance, failure history, explicit retry,
      immutable metadata and post-event re-evaluation acceptance remain
      pending.
    - Result archive round-trip remains pending under #958.

Status:
    TYPED EVENT DISPATCH / FAILURE / TRANSITION / RE-EVALUATION EVIDENCE
    IMPLEMENTED IN SOURCE. RUNTIME AND ARCHIVE ACCEPTANCE REMAIN PENDING.

======================================================================
GF-STUDY-ARCHIVE-958 — TYPED TRANSIENT RESULT RECONSTRUCTION
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-02

Finding:
    StudyResultArchive encoded TransientStabilityResult as a generic
    dataclass tag because the canonical transient result was absent from the
    solver-result allowlist. On reload, schema-v1 generic dataclasses became
    detached dictionaries rather than the canonical typed result. Nested
    EventExecution records had the same reconstruction gap.

Correction:
    - Advance new archive writes to schema version 2.
    - Keep schema-v1 records readable under their historical semantics:
      generic dataclass tags still decode to detached
      {"$dataclass": qualified_name, "fields": ...} mappings. No attempt is
      made to reinterpret old v1 generic payloads as typed results.
    - Explicitly allowlist TransientStabilityResult as a solver result and
      EventExecution as a nested event-execution record.
    - Encode EventExecution using its dedicated event_execution tag and
      reconstruct only the exact allowlisted class. Unknown classes, wrong
      tags, missing fields and extra fields fail closed.
    - Encode NumPy arrays in schema v2 with explicit dtype, shape and data.
      This preserves zero-row state arrays (for example shape (0, n)) as well
      as normal time/state arrays without losing dimensions.
    - Reconstruct TransientStabilityResult through its canonical constructor,
      restoring finite/dimension validation, read-only NumPy arrays,
      immutable terminal-voltage/electrical-power mappings and provenance.
    - Reconstruct every nested EventExecution through its canonical
      constructor, restoring immutable metadata/action results and all
      scheduled/dispatch timestamps, status, failure, revision and
      post-event re-evaluation fields.
    - Reject generic dataclass fallback for known transient result/event
      classes in schema v2. No archive-supplied module import or dynamic type
      loading is used.
    - Add focused tests for typed result/event round-trip, read-only arrays,
      immutable event evidence, malformed event/result fields, empty-array
      shape preservation and schema-v1 legacy mapping semantics.
    - Update Modification_Register.txt.

Boundary:
    StudyResultArchive remains the single durable Study Result archive.
    Study execution results remain outside the canonical .gridforge project
    configuration package. The explicit class allowlists remain the only
    reconstruction authority; unknown runtime classes are never imported.

Coordination:
    - #960 supplies the expanded EventExecution contract reconstructed here.
    - #955 supplies machine connection-path and switch-transition provenance
      carried inside transient results/events.
    - #959 may now consume a canonical typed transient result and its event
      timeline.
    - Schema-v1 transient generic dataclass records remain readable as
      mappings; only schema-v2 records provide canonical typed reconstruction.

Verification:
    - Source-level inspection of archive schema gate, explicit result/event
      allowlists, recursive encoder/decoder, NumPy shape handling,
      TransientStabilityResult validation and EventExecution reconstruction.
    - Focused round-trip and malformed-payload tests were added as requested.
    - No tests or CI were run, per project instruction.
    - Runtime archive v1/v2 loading, checksum validation and typed
      reconstruction acceptance remain pending.

Status:
    SCHEMA-V2 TYPED TRANSIENT RESULT AND EVENT RECONSTRUCTION IMPLEMENTED.
    SCHEMA-V1 LEGACY READ SEMANTICS PRESERVED.
    RUNTIME / TEST ACCEPTANCE REMAINS PENDING.

GF-TS-DAE-965 — TRANSIENT ALGEBRAIC SOLUTION QUALITY CONTRACT
======================================================================

Finding:
    The transient DAE accepted finite bus voltages without evidence that
    they satisfied the exact assembled algebraic network equations.
    Singular-matrix exceptions were the only matrix-quality diagnostic;
    no residual, condition estimate, or per-sample quality record was
    propagated to transient results.

Correction:
    - Add immutable AlgebraicNetworkSolution with bus voltages, status,
      acceptance flag, quality-verification flag, solver identity,
      absolute infinity-norm residual, normalized backward error,
      acceptance tolerance, condition estimate/limit, reduced Bus
      identities, topology revision, simulation time, connected machine
      source IDs, active-fault type/Bus context and project/activation/source provenance.
    - Compute the residual from the exact contracted matrix and source
      vector used by numpy.linalg.solve:
          r = YV - I
      and normalize it using the normwise backward-error denominator:
          ||Y||_inf * ||V||_inf + ||I||_inf
    - Default normalized residual tolerance to 1e-10 and condition-number
      limit to 1e12; both are configurable on TransientNetworkSolver.
    - Distinguish SINGULAR, ILL_CONDITIONED, RESIDUAL_REJECTED, NON_FINITE
      and ACCEPTED outcomes. The DAE rejects every non-accepted typed result.
    - Preserve generic Mapping callbacks for compatibility, but label their
      quality UNVERIFIED rather than implying a residual check occurred.
    - Carry algebraic quality through DAESolution and persist one quality
      record alongside each recorded transient sample, including the
      post-event algebraic re-evaluation. Enforce exact time-sample alignment.
    - Preserve #955 detached machine-path gating, switch contraction,
      disconnected rotor policy and study provenance in the quality record.

Boundaries and coordination:
    - This adds solution-quality verification; it does not replace the
      direct linear solver with Newton iteration.
    - It does not implement post-switching island partitioning, which
      remains owned by #957. A singular global matrix is reported and
      rejected with the reduced Bus/source/topology context; no reference
      source is fabricated.
    - Legacy generic callbacks remain callable but are explicitly marked
      UNVERIFIED. Production transient network solving returns verified
      quality metadata.
    - No live project state, telemetry or device acknowledgement is added.

Verification:
    - Source-level inspection only; no tests/CI or numerical benchmarks
      were run, per project instruction.
    - Runtime acceptance remains pending for a well-conditioned reference
      case, a deliberately inaccurate finite solution, singular versus
      ill-conditioned classification, and event-boundary re-solve metadata.

Status:
    TYPED ALGEBRAIC QUALITY CONTRACT AND TRANSIENT RESULT PROPAGATION
    IMPLEMENTED IN SOURCE; RUNTIME NUMERICAL ACCEPTANCE REMAINS PENDING.

======================================================================

      class identity, archive tag and complete dataclass field set.
    - Reconstruct only through AlgebraicNetworkSolution's canonical
      constructor, retaining its immutable voltage/provenance mappings and
      status, residual, tolerance, conditioning, time, topology, source and
      fault-context invariants.
    - Require every schema-v2 TransientStabilityResult algebraic_quality
      entry to reconstruct as an allowlisted AlgebraicNetworkSolution.
    - Reject generic schema-v2 dataclass encoding for the known algebraic
      solution class. Preserve schema-v1 generic mapping semantics; no
      legacy quality records are synthesized.
    - No UI or Application read-model behavior is changed in this archive
      correction.

Boundary:

    It imports only the explicit Core type and never imports archive-provided
    module names. Core remains independent of Application and UI.

Verification:

    - No tests or CI were run, per project instruction.

Status:

GF-ARCH-IMPORT-001 — SHORT-CIRCUIT ANALYSIS/SOLVER PACKAGE IMPORT CYCLE
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-11-03

Finding:
    Importing the Analysis short-circuit facade caused eager initialization
    of core.solver.short_circuit.phase_domain_solver. That solver module
    imports immutable preparation snapshot types from core.analysis while
    core.analysis.__init__ is still initializing, creating a package import
    cycle.

Correction:
    - Keep foundational sequence solver, input, result and compatibility
      exports eager in core.solver.short_circuit.
    - Resolve the eight phase-domain solver exports lazily through the
      package __getattr__ boundary on first attribute access.
    - Cache resolved exports in the package namespace and retain __all__
      compatibility for wildcard/public API discovery.
    - Preserve Analysis ownership of phase-domain preparation snapshots;
      no domain types or numerical algorithms were duplicated or relocated.

Architecture:
    - Analysis remains the owner of engineering preparation.
    - Solver consumes prepared immutable inputs and owns numerical execution.
    - The package facade no longer imports Analysis-backed phase-domain
      solver implementation while Analysis is importing its own facade.

Verification:
    - Inspected the import chain and the top-level imports of both phase-domain
      preparation modules.
    - Inspected the updated package facade and confirmed phase-domain exports
      are resolved only on explicit attribute access.
    - No tests, CI, syntax checks or runtime import experiments were run.

Status:
    SOURCE-LEVEL CYCLE CORRECTION COMMITTED TO MAIN.
    RUNTIME IMPORT-INTEGRITY VERIFICATION REMAINS PENDING.
